# Emend UX Design (v2 rewrite)

ABOUTME: Flow-level UX design for the Emend rewrite — journeys, states, and lo-fi wireframes.
ABOUTME: Companion to docs/functional-spec.md (decisions cited as D#/FR-#). Built strawman-by-strawman with CJ.

**Status:** Complete (2026-07-05). All 8 journeys interviewed with CJ; fresh-eyes review findings incorporated. **UXR-Q5** (§5) ratified by CJ 2026-07-22 (DH-7). **J2 project rail** (#698) ratified by CJ 2026-07-30 (DH-7) — the entire document is ratified.
**Fidelity:** deliberately lo-fi — ASCII wireframes, annotated. Pixel design now exists and lives in `docs/design/` (see below); this document does not duplicate it.

**Visual design layer — `docs/design/` (ratified by CJ 2026-08-04):** the pixel layer is a **peer constitution document**, binding on appearance exactly as this one is binding on behavior. This document keeps *what happens* — principles (§1), states (§2), journeys (§3), chrome inventory (§4). `docs/design/` keeps *what it looks like* — tokens, spacing, platform chrome, and the mock option ids (`1a`–`1m` lo-fi wireframes; `3a`/`3b`/`4a`/`4b` hi-fi authoring shell). Where the two disagree inside the other's domain, the owning document wins; where they disagree about the same thing, or where `specs/state.md` "Known gaps" leaves a question open, **raise it with CJ** — neither document is amended by the reader. Appearance authority does not extend to flow: a mock implying behavior this document does not record is a **J# delta to capture and sign off**, never a licence to build it. `docs/design/` is regenerated wholesale from CJ's design project and is never hand-edited: a J# delta lands here, a visual change goes back to CJ as a brief. `docs/ux-design.md` is the only authoritative copy of this file.
**Amendment — J4 Address all host and chooser, ruled by CJ 2026-10-04 (#2676):** In the main app, the batch trigger lives in the editor toolbar, moving into its `⋯` overflow at 900px; the Timeline-header host is standalone-component-only. The chooser omits the held-count clause and opt-in at zero held, keeps its filter through close and reopen on the same document but resets it on a document switch, focuses its first filter on open, and closes on Escape or an outside click in both hosts. After a successful submission, the admitted run card takes focus whether Timeline began collapsed or expanded. J4 and §5 carry the rule; generated `docs/design/` refresh (26) awaits the matching designer correction.
**Amendment — FR-ED-5 error-mic recovery, ruled by CJ 2026-10-02 (#2635):** activating any of the four named device or permission error mics opens Settings at Voice instead of retrying dictation; §2 lists the reasons and focus behavior. Backend-unavailable, model/download and transcription errors retain their retry paths. Generated `docs/design/` refresh (20) predates this ruling and needs its error-click gap and visual route updated by the designer.
**Supplement — UXR-Q23 collapsed-host fit, ruled by CJ 2026-10-02 (#2540):** At 900px the project rail remains 236px and the collapsed comments column remains 400px; the one-row editor toolbar keeps a compact, visible Timeline return action with its Pause badge when a permission is pending. At a fitting 1000px width the full Timeline chip remains inline through both permission arrival and clearance, even where the old ordinary overflow rule would move an idle chip into `⋯`. At both widths the physical collapsed button persists across badge changes, so an arrival never moves focus and a focused chip retains focus when the badge clears. Formatting and other non-fitting controls move to the existing overflow. The compact idle and pending appearance uses existing tokens provisionally; generated `docs/design/` needs both states in the next designer refresh. This supersedes J7/§4/UXR-Q23's pending-only Windows overflow exception, not the rest of J7.

**Supplement — #2641 compact toolbar fit, ruled by CJ 2026-10-02:** At a 900px window, retain the 236px project rail and 400px comments column. The editor mic, 44×26px collapsed Timeline return, and `⋯` remain visible in the one-row 263–264px toolbar. If existing context and formatting controls have moved and the Rich text/Markdown segmented switch still cannot fit, that switch also moves into the same `⋯`, preserving both actions, their pressed states, keyboard and pointer operation, and focus recovery as it returns inline at wider widths. The switch is last in the collapse order. This expressly supersedes the generated `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md` narrow rule that the segmented control never moves; the designer must revise that rule and draw its moved state. The generated folder is not hand-edited.
**Amendment — UXR-Q13 comment ordering, ruled by CJ 2026-10-01:** `Position` puts anchored, non-orphaned threads in current passage order; document-level and orphaned threads follow in a stable unpositioned tail. `Recency` puts the thread with the most recent accepted activity first, including replies, edits, and resolution changes. In Recency mode, an in-place edit stays in its current column slot on Save; its new activity time is applied on the next sort change or tab reload. This clarifies §4's sort labels and J10's no-re-sort-on-Save rule without changing the generated mock.
**Supplement — UXR-Q13.3 comment run-link lifetime, ruled by CJ 2026-10-02:** each persistent comment card keeps one `Show the run` link to its latest accepted single-comment run after that run completes and across tab switches in the window. Only another accepted run for that same comment replaces the link, whether started from the collapsed column, expanded Timeline card, or editor comment popover; a rejected admission or a response with no run leaves it and its sent-text cue unchanged. The cue remains the fact about the linked run's submit-time envelope, never the currently active buffer. Equal comment IDs in another document or project do not share a link. This settles the lifecycle left open by the 2026-09-30 collapsed-Timeline amendment and J13/§4/§5 below; it changes no generated appearance rule.
**Amendment — J9 in-flight Dismiss and untitled comments, ruled by CJ 2026-10-01:** `Dismiss` stays available while a project-open request or retry is in flight. Dismissing hides the status line without cancelling the request; the line returns with the terminal outcome. An untitled document admits comments before Save As, including anchors, gutter icons, the comment context group at ≥1 comment, the open-comment count at ≥1, and `Comment` in its selection popover. A collapsed comments column shows `No comments in this document` after a successful empty load on either file-backed or untitled documents. `Ask agent` remains absent on a pathless selection until Save As. This supersedes the earlier in-flight-dismiss and untitled-comment exclusions below; appearance is `docs/design/` refresh (20), notably `9g` and `13a`–`13f`.
**Amendment — UXR-Q13 and UXR-Q14.3 collapsed Timeline, ruled by CJ 2026-09-30:** a collapsed Timeline keeps the comments column at its established width for every open document and keeps that preference across tab switches in the window. Every open document, including an untitled one, says `No comments in this document` only after its comments load confirms no threads. The composer, provider switcher, mic, and J13 standing disclosure remain in expanded Timeline, reachable through its chip. Addressing a comment with AI while collapsed keeps the column visible and gives its card a link to the run; if that run carried unsaved document text, the card also says `Unsaved text sent with this run`. §4 owns the rail behavior, J13 owns the disclosure condition, and §5 records both questions as answered. Appearance is drawn in generated `docs/design/` refresh (20), options `4c`–`4g`.

**Supplement — UXR-Q16.4 collapsed run-start routes, ruled by CJ 2026-10-01:** a run started while Timeline is collapsed from outside a persistent comment card expands Timeline to present the new run and the applicable J13 sent-context state on its owning submitted-turn card (user chat card if there is one, otherwise run card); a clean document has no disclosure line. This includes `Address all…` and file-backed selection `Ask agent`, and keeps the comment-card `[Address with AI]` exception above. If the active facet excludes the new run, select `All` and reveal that run; if it already includes the run, preserve it. This is a scoped exception to UXR-Q2's normal no-facet-change submission rule. §4, J13, and §5 carry the same boundary; generated `docs/design/` refresh (20) draws these states in `4g`.
**Amendment — UXR-Q14.1 editor dictation placement, ruled by CJ 2026-09-30:** the editor toolbar carries a mic on file-backed and pathless authoring panes. This reconciles §4's omitted control with FR-ED-5 and §2's three-surface voice inventory. The toolbar remains one row under UXR-Q7.2; controls that do not fit use its existing measured overflow. §4 and §5 record the behavior; the Windows/macOS appearance and voice states are drawn in generated `docs/design/` refresh (20), options `3c`/`3d`.
**Amendment — #698 / TD-18 confinement capability, ratified by CJ 2026-07-30 (DH-7):** capability-gate the existing Missing-on-disk `[Recreate]` recovery as specified below.
**Amendment — #759 project-open routing, ratified by CJ 2026-08-09 (DH-7):** **J9**, §2 "Project-open routing states", the §4 shared status-region clause, and **UXR-Q8** bind the project-open experience against D26 and FR-X-10/11/12, merged in PR #1150.
**Amendment — #1752 project-open status and run-blocked-close behavior, ratified by CJ 2026-09-14 (DH-7):** J9 and §2 now make the existing status-region outcome executable: an in-flight request was not dismissible under that ruling (superseded by the 2026-10-01 amendment above); a failed outcome carries the required `failureRoute` discriminator and one of seven reason strings; `[Show the run]` conditionally selects the `All` facet, reveals and focuses the exact run card without moving the document, and leaves the refusal mounted while the run remains active; terminal clearing restores focus only when it would otherwise remove the focused status control and never resumes the refused close. Failures stay one line and the existing backend reason taxonomy is retained. §4 records the matching dismissibility exception, surface reachability, reviewer exclusion, and visual authority in `docs/design/` refresh (16), options `13a`–`13h`. CJ ratified all eight dispositions after an independent design review reported no unrecorded contradiction or gap.
**Amendment — #932 displaced documents, ruled by CJ 2026-08-11, wording ratified 2026-08-12 (DH-7):** J2's correlated-move clause now says what happens when a move's destination is a path another *open* document occupies — the moved document becomes the sole document there, and a displaced document's unsaved work becomes an **untitled recovery document** rather than being discarded or written to the destination.
**Amendment — #1421 untitled-recovery over-cap lifecycle, ratified by CJ 2026-08-27 and revised/re-ratified 2026-08-28 (DH-7, PR #1833):** J2 distinguishes live over-cap work from a restored omission, persists the omission/hidden facts explicitly, gives close five truthful guard variants, conditions quit on authoritative session-persistence proof, and records omission-aware Save As adoption through a durable event with a D15 projection. This supersedes the withdrawn 2026-08-26 read-only wording; appearance is `docs/design/` 9a–9f.
**Amendment — #1125 comment correction, ratified by CJ 2026-08-09 at reviewed head `145c655c` (DH-7):** **J10**, *correct your own comment*, gives D24 / FR-CMT-8..13 their behavioral home — §2 gains `Withdrawn`, `Editing`, and an `edited` provenance dimension; J6 and §4 gain the reviewer's own-comment correction verb; §5 resolves **UXR-Q9** through `docs/design/` options `5a`/`5b`/`5c` (confirmation, withdrawn placeholder, refusal states, edited provenance, and cross-surface reuse).
**Superseded amendment — #1125 cascade-hide (CJ 2026-08-18/20):** the former rule hid an author's own replies when a private root was withdrawn. CJ replaced it with child-first withdrawal on 2026-09-30; its third confirmation variant and cascade presentation no longer apply.

**Amendment — J10 child-first withdrawal, ruled by CJ 2026-09-30 (D24 / FR-CMT-8..11):** no parent comment or nested reply can be deleted until every direct and descendant reply is already withdrawn, regardless of author. Only each comment's own author can withdraw it in v1; an owner's ability to moderate others awaits a future permissions model. J10 keeps the historical-placeholder and append-only guarantees, and records the exceptional late-offline-reply convergence case. The child-present refusal and eligible confirmation are drawn in generated `docs/design/` refresh (20), option `5d`; the former cascade confirmation is withdrawn.
**Amendment — #1720 rail document types and the Documents/Skills split, ruled by CJ 2026-08-18 (DH-7):** J2's project rail becomes **two labelled sections inside one keyboard tree**. *Documents* lists only FR-ED-9 document types, hides every dot-prefixed path, and yields any discovered skill/workflow-owned path to *Skills* so one project-relative path has one row and one active marker. *Skills* renders the FR-SKILL-5 discovery result rather than a location-rooted project tree: a skill expands to its owned directory material, while a workflow is a file-backed leaf that opens its own portable Markdown body. Workflow-file ownership takes precedence over containing skill directories, and the deepest matching skill owns all remaining nested material. The two data sources load, update, fail, and recover independently. Governed by **D27**/FR-ED-9's two gates: whole-file CriticMarkup safety plus an explicitly supported editing/projection contract. CJ's 2026-08-20 `docs/design/` refresh closes the appearance gap in options `1b`, `1c`, `2a`, `3a`, `3b`, `4a`, `4b`, and `6a`–`6c`; implementation remains a separate pending card.
**Proposal — #1162 dismissal, parked for CJ at the DH-7 gate (proposed 2026-08-19; conformed 2026-08-20 to the requirements CJ ratified that day — *the delta itself is still not ratified*):** **J11**, *dismiss a comment or finding*, gives D25 / FR-CMT-14..18 their behavioral home — §2 gains `Dismissed`, the two-outcome closure rule, and dismissal's rows in the run-exit table; J1's triage, J3, J4's single and batch flows, J5, J6, J10 and §4 gain their consequences. **CJ ruled UXR-Q12.1 / .3 / .4 on 2026-08-19 (raised and answered under the number **UXR-Q11**, renumbered on merge — #1125’s cascade-hide question holds UXR-Q11 on `main`)** — the optional note is offered **after** the act, both closed cards carry `Reopen`, and the verb is **`Dismiss`** — and on **2026-08-20 ratified the requirements those rulings needed: D28, D29 and FR-CMT-19/20/21 (DH-7, PR #1792; its own file-level merge still pending)**. This document is conformed to them: **reopen is a user act with a requirement of its own** rather than a UX invention (FR-CMT-19/20), *Reopened* is **one condition with two causes**, and **closure availability is one rule for both outcomes** (FR-CMT-21) — refused by hand while any linked tracked change is undecided, available to both while a run is in flight, so FR-CMT-16's in-flight-run limb is withdrawn. The same ratification **widens correction's** bind the other way (FR-CMT-10: from the moment a live *or queued* run targets the thread), so §2's acknowledgment ladder gains correction copy at its first two rungs and J10's refusal bullet is conformed — on one thread at one rung, correction is refused and closure is not, which this document now states in three places rather than leaving to inference. That closes **UXR-Q12.2**, and it makes `Mark as resolved` **inert in a state where it is live today** — a state `docs/design/` has never drawn. Still routed elsewhere: whether a manual reopen is distinguishable from a machine-written one in the record, and what the two causes mean to the fold and to re-delegation, are the **Architect's** (Q24(b), TD-1, P5). `docs/design/` draws no dismissed or reopened treatment; the design layer noticed the gap and deferred it pending ratification (`docs/design/CHANGELOG.md`, 2026-08-09), so a brief goes to CJ for the design-project refresh rather than a mock being inherited.
**Amendment — #1515 reviewer-mode boundary, ratified by CJ 2026-08-14 (DH-7, PR #1547):** J6 gains an explicit whole-window mode boundary, reviewer-workspace states, an externally ended-review transition, and a reviewer status region. The review set is the reviewer's only document namespace; J2's project rail and every author-only local-path recovery control remain unmounted and unreachable.
**Amendment — #1404 J7 comment-lane stop outcome, ruled by CJ 2026-08-20 at PR #1712 head `c11692e1` (DH-7):** Under J7, the existing STOPPED — partial delivery outcome includes any completed TD-1 §3.2 delivery-lane action, including add_comments and reply_to_comment. No third stop state is intended; the existing two-outcome taxonomy stands.
**Amendment — #1727 timeline-facet empty states, ratified by CJ 2026-08-20 (DH-7):** §2 records the five facet-specific completed-empty messages and their quiet muted-text treatment, resolving `docs/design/specs/state.md`'s listed timeline empty-state gap. An empty claim appears only after the current project's initial transcript load has settled; loading remains silent rather than briefly asserting that a non-empty history has no activity.

**Amendment — #1379 untitled/scratch document lifecycle (D12, FR-ED-8), ratified by CJ 2026-08-21 at reviewed head `7114b02a39bdc23417a7aba1be26c93fc9b74897` (DH-7/DH-30) — partial delivery:** the untitled recovery document becomes editable, with autosave off by construction, and Save As is the chosen product route for adopting it into the project. FR-ED-8 says that a scratch document saved into the project adopts accumulated comments and tracked changes; it does not itself prescribe Save As. **At that point D12 and FR-ED-8 were incomplete** pending #1803; untitled comment creation and adoption have since shipped (see `e2e/acceptance.spec.mjs`), and the comment capability is retained by the 2026-10-01 ruling above. #1804 remains the separate rich-text/Markdown parity follow-up; #1379 and #1803 have since closed.

**Ruling — #2515 D27 dirty legacy unsupported-session recovery (CJ, 2026-10-01):** a restored legacy tab whose file type FR-ED-9 now refuses but whose session retains an unsaved buffer automatically becomes J2/J12's editable untitled recovery document, labeled by its former project path. The unsupported path is neither opened nor written; Save As is the existing adoption route. A clean unsupported tab still closes after a visible refusal. An omitted buffer is not reconstructed from its marker. **Follow-up ruling (CJ, 2026-10-01, #2555):** an omission-only unsupported tab keeps its tab and omission marker after the visible refusal, because no retained bytes can justify conversion and the loss fact must remain protected. Refresh (20) `9h.2` drew that tab closing; generated `docs/design/` refresh (21) corrects it. J2 owns the conversion and durability guard; J12 owns the pathless editing surface. Refresh (21) also draws the unsupported-source label and explanation in `9h`; `docs/d27-legacy-unsupported-recovery-visual-refresh-brief.md` is the source brief.

**Amendment — #1804/#2111 document-notice slot ("Delta B"), ratified by CJ 2026-08-28 (DH-7):** §3's document integrity vocabulary now names the **document-notice slot** — immediately below the toolbar, above the standing-conditions strip (above the document-info row on a file-backed pane) — binding on every pane, carrying the FR-ED-7 integrity banner, an untitled pane's amber loss notice, a Save As failure line and the adoption-provenance pending line, and closing with the prohibition against anything sitting between the tab strip and the toolbar. §4's review bar is confirmed as **workspace-level chrome, full width above the tab strip** — not in that slot and not subject to its prohibition, which governs document-scoped notices only — and J5 gains a **fixed-height** constraint on the bar for the review's duration; J5's own placement wording is conformed to the same ruling (CJ, 2026-08-28), replacing its prior "on the editor" phrasing so the two sections no longer describe the bar differently. Traces FR-ED-7, J5, D18; appearance in `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md`, "The document-notice slot." Raised by #1804, drawn by PR #2111. **This ratifies independently of #1804's own J12/"Delta A" proposal**; Delta B fixes the ordinary file-backed pane and does not depend on #1804 shipping.

**Amendment — #1804 J12 delta ("Delta A"), ratified by CJ 2026-08-28 (DH-7):** mints **J12**, *write in an untitled document (the pathless authoring surface)*, extending J2's adoption clause and the new cross-journey section § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus* at three named points. That cross-journey section is added here too, giving the single-flight save-as latch, the session-write finalization freeze, and the focus-recording guarantee behind it a behavioral home binding on every document pane, file-backed and untitled alike. §2's "Tracked-change states" inventory gains the recorded/unrecorded population rule, and a new "Untitled document surface states" sub-inventory is added. J2's untitled/scratch lifecycle paragraph gains a pointer to J12. Traces D12, FR-ED-8, TD-16, TD-24, J2, J5; appearance `docs/design/` `9a`–`9f`. **Depends on PR #2111 landing before or with this delta** — J12's body cites `docs/design/` sections that exist only on that branch. Ratifies independently of, and cites, the document-notice slot delta ("Delta B", #2123): Delta B fixes the ordinary file-backed pane and does not depend on #1804; J12 states only where *this pane's* notices sit and defers the general rule to it.

**Ruling — UXR-Q14.4 pathless selection (CJ, 2026-09-30):** J12 keeps `Comment` and `Quote to chat` in the untitled selection popover and omits `Ask agent` entirely until successful Save As gives the document a project path. The file-backed selection action then follows its ordinary rule, using J12 Phase F's preserved selection. This closes Q14.4 without changing FR-CHAT-4's ambient reference-data channel or the path-keyed agent write boundary. `docs/design/` refresh (20) draws the pathless selection popover in `9g`; generated mocks are not hand-edited in the repo.

**Amendment — #2102 unattributed internal failure, ratified by CJ 2026-08-28 (DH-7):** §2 gains an **Unattributed internal failure** state for the shared status region (FR-X-5) — a failure that reaches no part of the app that can name it, distinct from the ratified requested-action sentence, which stays exactly as it is for a request the author made. The line reads `Something inside Emend didn’t finish. If something doesn’t look right, this may be why. <detail>`, occupies one line on every surface, and clips rather than wraps in a fixed order (`<detail>` gives way first, then the second sentence, then the first) — the discipline of the band §4's shared status region and J6's reviewer status region establish (J5's fixed-height constraint and UXR-Q14.6 — #1804/#2111). It takes no focus and causes no scroll, carries no `[Try again]` and no reassurance clause, omits `<detail>` in reviewer mode (Principle 4/7), yields without queuing to any message about something the user asked for, and is dismissible in both author and reviewer mode. **§4 gains a paragraph immediately after the ratified "Status region" sentence, carving this state's own copy rule out at the site that owns the region** — the ratified sentence is not edited, but a failure nobody asked for now uses §2's wording, bound and rules instead of it, and author mode's occurrence is named the first this band admits with no user act before it, against reviewer mode's `Review ended` (#1515) precedent. Resolves **UXR-Q15** (§5): the band's existing one-line reflow may fire in author mode with no user act, bounded exactly as this state bounds it. Traces FR-X-5; §4 Status region; J5's fixed-height constraint and UXR-Q14.6; Principle 2/UXR-Q2; Principle 4; Principle 7; J6's reviewer status region and `Review ended` (#1515); `docs/test-plan.md` §9b. The reporting mechanism is TD-25's.

**Amendment — UXR-Q numbering convention, ratified by CJ 2026-08-30 (DH-7):** §5 gains a preamble stating how a UXR-Q number is claimed — next free at the time of writing, confirmed at ratification against every number the queue already carries on `main` (ratified, still open, or retired) plus any number confirmed at an earlier sign-off whose delta has not yet merged. Complements, and does not compete with, UXR-Q14's numbering note, which governs a number only after it is claimed.

**Amendment — #2327 refused-close reconciliation, ratified by CJ 2026-09-04 (DH-7):** J2's quit sequence records the finalization write landing and the finalization write failing. It did not record the third case — the write lands and the window then refuses to close — which `core:window:allow-destroy` being absent from the capability manifest made the *only* case in every build ever produced (#2327, PR #2328). Because the queue's closes are carried out by the window going away and by nothing else, a landed write followed by a refused close leaves the durable session describing tabs that are still on screen, including an untitled document the author chose to discard. **Emend re-writes the live session through the ordinary lane on a refused close, un-committing the queue's staged closes**, and says the window did not close and that nothing was closed or discarded. This is a scoped exception to *"in a single write it waits for"*, confined to the refused-close branch. **Four things are named and deliberately not ratified here, carried as UXR-Q20.** One is CJ's own, left open when he ratified the second write: whether a refused close should instead carry the queue's closes out in the UI, destroying the discarded buffer in a window that stayed open. Three were raised at this entry's independent review on 2026-09-05 and **CJ has not ruled on them** — the same refusal after `Quit anyway`, which J2's write-failure paragraph still describes as closing; the failure text this disclosure interpolates, which §2 bounds to one interpolation and calls precedent for nothing else; and where focus lands when a close is refused. Traces J2, FR-ED-3, FR-X-5, TD-25, §2; #2330 records it.

**Amendment — UXR-Q20's four refused-close rulings, ratified by CJ 2026-09-05 (DH-7):** the amendment above recorded the refused-close branch and named four things it did not settle; CJ ruled all four. **(1)** A refused close never carries the queue's closes out in the UI — the shipped behaviour is the answer, because the authorisation to discard an untitled buffer was given as part of quitting and does not survive the quit not happening. **(2)** J2's `[Quit anyway]` sentence said that branch *closes*; its tail is amended, its substance untouched. **(3)** §2's `<detail>` bound moves from *"this one interpolation"* to the class it was the first instance of — a failure Emend **can attribute** may carry the failure's own text, under the constraints §2 already records; what stays forbidden is Emend authoring developer-shaped words of its own. **(4)** A refused close returns focus to the author's recorded position, the destination cancelling already uses, filling the one case § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus* did not name. Amends J2's quit sequence, §2's *Unattributed internal failure*, that cross-journey section, and closes **UXR-Q20**. Question 1 was CJ's own of 2026-09-04; questions 2-4 were raised 2026-09-05 at the independent review of PR #2331. Traces J2, FR-ED-3, FR-X-5, TD-25, §2, Principle 2; #2334 records it, and the focus ruling ships with it.

**Amendment — #2388 format-group behavior, ratified by CJ 2026-09-06 (DH-7), with applicability and interim appearance supplement ratified 2026-09-07:** mints **J14** and a §2 format-group state inventory. The existing four controls remain live on every editable pane and in both editor projections; they operate on the source-model Markdown, reflect semantic state, and preserve the author's target and input-origin focus. `Mod-B` and `Mod-I` join the tranche; structural shortcuts and the real icon set do not. **UXR-Q21** records the six original rulings. The 2026-09-07 supplement makes applicability atomic and fail-closed, specifies mixed structural normalization, and authorizes implementation from J14's exact pressed/focus brief until the next generated `docs/design/` refresh carries it. Appearance otherwise remains `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md`; its `B`/`I`/`H1`/`≔` glyphs remain placeholders pending CJ's design refresh. Traces FR-ED-1, FR-X-1, TD-2, J12, Principle 2, #2388.

**Amendment — UXR-Q22 exact-target and block-edge behavior, ratified by CJ 2026-09-07 (DH-7; #2388):** preserves UXR-Q21's six rulings and fills four cases they did not decide. A format control now answers `active`, `inactive`, or `pending` for the exact document revision and selection; pending is a temporary, per-control unavailable state rather than a stale semantic answer, and a shortcut against it is refused without queuing or replay. Removing a task item's bullet preserves its `[ ]`/`[x]` marker as authored text. Heading 1 and Bulleted list refuse a whole target that touches a table or raw HTML, using the same reason-bearing refusal vocabulary as YAML frontmatter and code. A multiline soft-line paragraph becomes one Setext H1 so its line breaks survive, while a single-line paragraph remains an ATX `# ` heading. The literal format glyphs and deferred production icon set are unchanged. Amends §2, J14, §4 and §5. J14 carries the exact active/pending/focus brief for the next generated `docs/design/` refresh; CJ authorized implementation from it in the meantime. Traces FR-ED-1, FR-X-4, TD-2, TD-13, Principle 2, Principle 7, #2388.

**Amendment — #2429 filtered permission attention, ratified by CJ 2026-09-07 (DH-7), with clear-focus supplement ratified 2026-09-07:** J7 and §4 now specify one in-app attention cue whenever the current project has a pending permission request, including when its run card is excluded by the active timeline facet. A nonnumeric pause badge marks the existing Timeline host without changing the facet, rail mode, editor or focused review; the author's explicit activation selects Chat, reveals the matching run, and moves focus to the oldest actionable request's heading. The live announcement, stacked-request progression, clearing and focus-recovery rules are recorded below. The supplement gives the expanded host a stable level-2 `Timeline` heading and makes it the clear-time fallback until the generated adjacent collapse toggle has a production owner; it neither implements collapse nor settles UXR-Q13. The badge does not reuse UXR-Q2's submitted-card-only `N new in Chat`, does not duplicate permission decisions or Stop, and does not replace J7's separate OS-notification requirement. The 2026-10-02 #2540 supplement above supersedes the pending-only Windows overflow exception; the generated design folder remains unchanged pending its compact-state refresh. Resolves **UXR-Q23**. Traces D14, FR-AGENT-3, Principles 2/3/5/7, J1, J7, §4, UXR-Q2, #2399, #2429.

**Amendment — #2237 outbound turn-context disclosure (FR-CHAT-4 / D30), ratified by CJ 2026-09-05 (DH-7); proposed 2026-09-02, PR #2242:** mints **J13**, *what leaves with a turn*, and a new §2 inventory, **Outbound turn-context disclosure states**. FR-CHAT-4 (ratified by CJ 2026-08-27 at #1793, reaffirmed 2026-09-02, recorded 2026-09-03) makes every submitted agent run carry the `open_documents` envelope `@anchor:timeline-open-documents-block`, including **the exact submit-time contents of the active buffer while it is dirty**, and an untitled document's contents whenever it is active — in both cases within D31's size bound, and with an honest marker over it. FR-CHAT-3's quoted selection has the visible quote bar as its disclosure surface (UX Principle 6); ambient open-document context has none, so text that exists nowhere but the author's screen leaves the machine on every turn and the author is never shown it. The PM wrote no J# deliberately — appearance and flow are not the PM's — and escalated it here (D30 Notes, Q25, #2237). **The delta in one line: disclosure is keyed to the author's text being *in play* — not to the envelope, and not to whether the text in fact went; it is standing rather than per-turn; it is visibility without a new control; and it names *which* document and *whether* its text went, never *how much*.** It amends **J12** at three sites — Phase A's *Travels / Stays* table, Phase E's `Quote to chat` bullet, and Phase E's D21/agent-reach bullet — because the untitled pane's "no agent can read this buffer" premise is false under FR-CHAT-4, while "no agent can *address* it" remains true; §2's *Untitled document surface states* `Agent reach` row follows them. §4's right-rail inventory gains the composer's standing line. **UXR-Q16** carries six numbered items. **CJ answered four of them on 2026-09-03** — *visibility only, no opt-out* (Q16.1, as proposed); *the over-bound composer lines carry the route out* (Q16.3, **against** this delta's draft — and then, on the same day and *with* it, the wording of that remedy: `save it so the agent can read it` in place of `save it to send the text`, because a saved document is clean and its text does not ride the turn, and `Save As…` for a document that has no file, whose `and there is no file` clause stays displaced because naming the act carries it); *the standing line alone, with no onboarding moment* (Q16.5, as proposed, recorded so it reopens on evidence rather than on taste); and *the disclosure survives the run card's default collapse* (Q16.6, as this delta argued it must). One was withdrawn at the independent design review because a ratified ruling had already foreclosed it (Q16.2, struck in place), and **one was answered by two later rulings** — Q16.4, UXR-Q13.3's inherited disclosure limb: CJ settled the comment-card route on 2026-09-30 and auto-expansion with its active-facet exception outside a persistent comment card on 2026-10-01. **Q16.6 amends a ruled string** and is therefore recorded at the section that owns it, §2's *Completed-run summary* (#1725), rather than as a clause inside J13. **Two ruling dates run through this delta and are not interchangeable:** D31's size bound, its untitled limb and its marker wording are CJ's of **2026-09-02**; these four answers are his of **2026-09-03**. **Sequencing:** CJ's 2026-09-02 ruling on **#2238** — the snapshot bounded at 256 KiB, sent whole or not at all, with an honest marker and no waiver for a pathless document — is recorded as **D31** and reaches the constitution as an FR-CHAT-4 amendment under its own card (**PR #2248**, open at drafting; since merged). §2's inventory carries **two** over-bound rows written against that then-proposed text, so **this delta could not be ratified before D31 merged**, or the sign-off would have been against superseded requirement text and against a `“<label>” goes in full` that the bound falsifies. **That bar is discharged:** PR #2248 merged, and CJ ratified on 2026-09-05. **No opt-out is proposed:** FR-CHAT-4 is a MUST with no opt-out, so a control to suppress the context would be a requirements change and returns to the PM, not to this document. Appearance is a **brief for CJ's design project**, not a repo edit — `docs/design/` draws no such surface and its `specs/state.md` "Known gaps" does not list one, so it is unrecorded rather than deliberately open, which CLAUDE.md's "silence is not permission" treats identically. Traces FR-CHAT-4, FR-CHAT-3, D30, D21, D4, D12, FR-ED-3, FR-ED-7, FR-ED-8, FR-AGENT-7, Principles 1/2/5/6/7, J1, J2, J4, J12, §4; `docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md` (composer, agent-message footer); #1724, #1793, #2237, #2238.

**Ruling — J13 is ratified, and this document is made to say so at every site (CJ, 2026-09-05; #2237, UXR-Q16.6):** this document contradicted itself about J13's status. §2's *Completed-run summary* amendment recorded the collapsed line's outbound disclosure as **ratified by CJ 2026-09-03 (UXR-Q16.6)**, while the §2 inventory header two lines below it, the entry above, J13's own banner and §4's composer line all still read **proposed, not ratified**. The question is not a typo either way, which is why it was escalated rather than tidied: answering questions raised *about* a parked proposal is not the same act as ratifying the proposal, and DH-7 keeps those separate — one site read Q16.6 as having done both, three read it as having done only the first. Escalated to CJ under CLAUDE.md's rule that a constitution document contradicting itself goes to him rather than being settled by whoever found it. **CJ ruled for the ratified reading: his answers of 2026-09-03 ratified the J13 delta itself, not merely the questions raised about it, so the *proposed* markers are stale.** In his words, *"Ratify in every location."* **The sites this ruling corrects**, recorded so a later reader sees why several changed at once rather than inferring it: the entry above; §2's *Outbound turn-context disclosure states* header and its D31 sequencing bar; §2's *Untitled document surface states* `Agent reach` row; §3's journey index row for J13; J12's Phase A *Travels / Stays* row and its two Phase E bullets; J13's own status banner; §4's composer standing line; §5's UXR-Q16 entry, whose number is confirmed as 16 at this ratification under §5's numbering preamble; and the J13 cross-reference inside §5's UXR-Q19 entry, whose own parked status is deliberately untouched — the ratification corrects what that entry says about J13, not what that entry asks. **Nothing about J13's substance changes** — the state inventory, the composer's standing line, the J12 amendments and UXR-Q16's answers stand exactly as ratified; this entry corrects *status*, not content. **One item was not ratified there but is now answered:** UXR-Q16.4, UXR-Q13.3's inherited disclosure limb. CJ answered the comment-card route on 2026-09-30 and auto-expansion, including the scoped UXR-Q2 facet exception, for other collapsed run starts on 2026-10-01. §5's UXR-Q16 is now closed. The same discharge is recorded downstream at `docs/functional-spec.md`'s D30 *Escalated, not decided here* clause and its Q25 entry, and at `docs/test-matrix.md`'s FR-CHAT-4 *Not built, filed instead* note — where J13 is now ratified but still unbuilt and untested. Traces J13, J12, UXR-Q16 (esp. Q16.6), FR-CHAT-4, D30, D31, §2, §4; #2237, PR #2242.

**Amendment — #2389 reveal-at-cursor preference, ratified by CJ 2026-09-06 (DH-7):** §2 gains the *Markup reveal preference*: `Reveal markup at cursor` lives in **Settings → General**, is app-global and durable, and defaults **off**. In Rich text, **Display: All** is the only display mode in which turning it on permits TD-2's established cursor reveal for both structural Markdown markers and CriticMarkup delimiters; Simple, None and Original keep both delimiter families hidden without changing the stored preference, and returning to All restores that preference. Markdown view always shows the raw source and is unaffected. Reviewer mode remains fully rich. The `Rich text | Markdown` segment is the complete fallback for seeing and directly editing delimiters while reveal is off, so #2388's format group improves the rich-text path but does not gate this change. The setting is a pure P1 projection: it changes no document bytes, dirty state, save, or undo history. **No new visual exploration is required:** `docs/design/` wireframe `1m` already binds General-pane on/off rows, while UXR-Q7.2's one-row toolbar budget keeps this durable preference out of authoring chrome. Traces TD-2, FR-ED-1, FR-ED-4, P1, J1, J12, UXR-Q7.2, `docs/design/specs/wireframes.md` `1m` and `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md` “Toolbar.”

**Proposal — #2130 render-failure fallback: CJ's rulings recorded, the remainder parked at the DH-7 gate (#2130 / #2432):** §2 gains **Render-failure fallback states** and §3 gains **J15**, *recover from a render failure* — the whole-window surface left after an uncaught error during React's render destroys the product tree, so #2102's shared status line has nowhere to appear (FR-X-5). **CJ resolved the behavioural choice on 2026-09-04 at 12:24Z, in three numbered items**, closing *"This resolves the behavioral choice. PR #2301 / issue #2130 remain at the design gate only for the two platform artifacts and any resulting prose alignment."* (1) Proceed with the whole-window fallback, **accepting the narrow Principle 3 / J7 exception** — while the fallback owns the window, live-run activity, permission UI and `[Stop]` are unavailable until reload/remount. (2) **The four mode-safe copy variants and `Reload window` as the sole action are approved**, including the rejected / non-completing retry state. (3) Implementation waits for refreshed Windows and macOS artifacts, which turn 12 now supplies. **CJ ruled four more things on 2026-09-07:** native close while the fallback is mounted; that the close refusal is an **assembly** — fixed opener, one clause per holding guard, fixed closer — rather than a set of whole strings, with `An untitled document is open` as the untitled clause; that its clauses take **ordinary English list punctuation**, two joined with `, and` and three separated by `, ` with `, and` before the last; and that **a queued reviewer event is not a close guard**, since J6 and FR-COLLAB-9 keep it recoverable. **None of those four is in the PR thread or any other artifact in this repository at the time of writing** — CJ gave them directly in a working session, which is the same one-disposable-place failure this entry exists to correct. §2 carries that provenance in full at their signposts, and they are recorded as rulings rather than softened into proposals to make them checkable. Each ruling is transcribed under its own signpost so it cannot be mistaken for a proposal, and — the failure this entry exists to prevent — so that a proposal cannot be mistaken for it: **`docs/design/` is regenerated wholesale from CJ's design project, so a ruling recorded only there is destroyed on the next refresh, and its `specs/render-failure-fallback.md` §6 marks absences *"absent by ruling"* naming no ruling at all.** What stays **proposed** is the prose alignment CJ’s own closing line anticipated, and it is now **three items**: the close refusal’s three drawing decisions (**UXR-Q24.1**); the mount/focus/announcement contract, whose only open question is whether Principle 2 needs a carve-out, and none is proposed (**UXR-Q24.2**); and three of the eight §6 absences (**UXR-Q24.3**). They are carried in **UXR-Q24** (§5), and none of them re-opens the behavioural choice. **Two former items are no longer proposed and are not in that list:** the close-refusal strings and the reviewer row’s durable clause were **closed by CJ on 2026-09-07** and are recorded in §2; and the reload control’s silence about what a remount does not preserve, together with the status region’s higher rank and dismissal memory across a remount, were **split to #2437** as design defects rather than prose alignment. **Two bounded `docs/design/` regenerations are owed** and are CJ’s to commission, both recorded in §5: per-item provenance in place of §6’s *"absent by ruling"*, and reconciliation of the `closeGuards` row and §5a with the narrowing and the assembly above. This document **declines to propose a Principle 2 carve-out** the design folder asserts, because Principle 2's focus limb is scoped to an agent delivery or an arriving permission request and reaches neither this case nor a node that has ceased to exist. Appearance arrives alongside as `docs/design/` turn 12 (`12a`–`12f`), generated rather than hand-written. The render-boundary signal, structured logging that does not consume the runtime signal, mode and guard establishment outside the failed tree, and the remount-result contract are the **Architect's**; they do not inherit TD-25 by analogy. Traces FR-X-5, D12, D15, Principles 2/3/4/7, J6, J7, J12, §2's *Unattributed internal failure* and its precedence rule (#2102), `docs/test-plan.md` §9b; #2130, #2432.

## 1. Design Principles

Derived from the functional spec's decisions; every screen should be checkable against these.

1. **Provenance always visible.** Human, agent, reviewer, or skill-finding — you can tell at a glance, everywhere a change or comment appears (D6, D8, D19).
2. **Propose, don't surprise.** Nothing changes a protected document without an accept. The UI never applies, auto-scrolls, or steals focus on the agent's behalf (D3, D21). **The three verbs share one test — did the user ask for this? (UXR-Q2)** *Applies* — never to a **protected** document without an accept, whatever the actor; an unprotected document stays direct-writable (§2, D21). *Auto-scrolls* — never for motion the user did not ask for, covering an agent delivery and a remote collaborator's edit alike (J6); the user's own deliberate act may scroll, and a reader sitting at the tail has by that choice asked to be carried by what arrives there. *Steals focus* — never taken **unasked**: an agent delivery or an arriving permission request never pulls focus from where you are typing (J1, J7); a user deliberately entering a focused pass is *granted* focus, not robbed of it — J1's Review 1-by-1 keyboard model depends on it.
3. **The agent is visible, never blocking.** You keep writing while it works. Its activity (reading X, editing Y) is observable in real time; it asks permission through non-modal affordances (FR-X-3, FR-AGENT-3).
4. **Git is invisible.** No branch names, commits, or push buttons in either authoring or reviewer mode. Versioning surfaces only as human concepts: "start review," "accept review," "history" (D9, D18).
5. **One timeline.** Chat, comments, tracked changes, and review findings are one chronological stream with filters — not four competing panels (baseline carry-forward, D6).
6. **The document is the center of gravity.** Everything in the panel deep-links into the text; everything in the text deep-links back (baseline: anchors, quote-bar).
7. **Degrade honestly.** No provider → editor still excellent. No git → local features intact. Offline → clear, not broken (FR-X-5, D12). In reviewer mode, app-managed checkout or sync failure is stated in review terms; it never leaks a local project path or offers author-only filesystem repair.

---

## 2. State Inventories

The vocabulary all wireframes reuse. (Mechanically derived from the spec.)

### Comment / thread states (D8, D17, D19)
| State | Meaning | Visual signal (proposal) |
|---|---|---|
| Open | Awaiting action | Solid accent border |
| Working | An Address-with-AI run has **started work on this thread** and delivered nothing yet — §2's Agent-states `Working` projected onto one thread (FR-CMT-2, FR-X-3, Principle 3) | The `5a.5`/`5b.5` active-run treatment: `◔` + `Addressing with AI — you can edit or delete this comment once the run finishes.`, never the pulsing/half-filled dot. The card asserts **no proposal of its own**: no agent summary, no linked-change count, no `[Go to →]`, no accept/reject **for this run**. The run's clock, activity feed, and `[Stop]` remain on its timeline card (J10, UXR-Q9); changes an earlier delivery left undecided stay visible and decidable, each named by its delivery |
| Addressed — pending | AI/author fixed; linked changes undecided (FR-CMT-7) | Pulsing/half-filled indicator. **The thread cannot be closed by hand here** — `Mark as resolved` and `Dismiss` are both inert until the changes are decided (FR-CMT-21(a)); deciding them is the route, and accepting closes the thread for you (FR-CMT-3/7) |
| Resolved | All linked changes decided, ≥1 accepted, or manually resolved — the manual act refused while any linked change is undecided (FR-CMT-21(a)) | Dimmed, collapsed, and carrying `Reopen` like any closed thread (FR-CMT-19) |
| Reopened | The thread was closed and is open again — **one condition, two causes** (FR-CMT-20): a linked change was rejected (FR-CMT-3/7), or the author reopened it by hand (FR-CMT-19) | Open + "reopened" badge. **The closure-history rule binds the hand reopen only** (FR-CMT-20, scoped at #1792): where the reopen inverts a **recorded** closure, that closure stays legible on the card — no Emend surface may show the author less of a thread's own closure history than the external reviewer's copy of that thread shows. A **rejection-driven** reopen inverts no closure and gains no closure line: FR-CMT-7 resolves a thread only once every linked change is decided *and* one is accepted, so a thread carrying a rejection travels *addressed — pending → reopened* with nothing closed in between, and a closure line there would state a closure that never happened. It reads exactly what D8 already gives it — the per-change outcomes. One condition, two causes; the causes decide the content. What the thread carries to the agent depends on what closed it — per-change outcomes after a rejection, the dismissal and its note after a dismissal (FR-CMT-18), and nothing after a hand-reopened resolution, which is an ordinary open thread again |
| Orphaned | Anchor text gone; original quote preserved | Warning chip: "original text changed" |
| Suggestion | Carries reviewer's proposed replacement text (D19) | Diff preview + Apply button |
| Editing | Its author is revising the body in place (FR-CMT-12) — transient, per card | Body becomes a composer; the timestamp reads `Editing`; the action cluster hides (`docs/design/specs/comments.md` "Editing state") |
| Withdrawn | Its author deleted it after every descendant reply was already withdrawn (FR-CMT-8/9) | **Absent** where it was never published, has no historical reply by another actor, and has no live descendant; otherwise a bodyless placeholder line carrying author + time. No live child is hidden or deleted as a side effect. A later-arriving offline reply by any author survives beneath the placeholder (J10 Phase C, FR-COLLAB-9). A withdrawn nested reply is not a live blocker, though its existing `5a.3b` provenance line may still appear when the containing thread is shown. A withdrawn placeholder offers **no `Reopen`**: withdrawal is terminal and a placeholder is not a closed thread (FR-CMT-19, FR-CMT-13) |
| Dismissed | Its reader closed it as *considered, and not accepted; nothing was changed* (FR-CMT-14) — Resolved's **peer**, never its variant | The closed mechanics Resolved uses (dimmed, collapsed, hidden) and **never** its claim: no `✓ Resolved` pill, a distinct `⊘ Dismissed` label, the optional note where one was given, and `Reopen` (FR-CMT-19). **Closed, not gone** — unlike the `Withdrawn` row above, nothing beneath a dismissed thread leaves the working view: every reply, the agent's defence included, stays on the card and reveals with it (FR-CMT-17). Appearance is `docs/design/`'s and is **undrawn** — UXR-Q12 |

**Working is not a variant of Addressed — pending; it is its opposite.** *Addressed — pending* asserts a proposal exists and waits on the user. *Working* asserts the agent is still running and the user owes nothing. Conflating them produces a card that claims a proposal and offers no route to it (#1122). The row is the **Agent-states `Working` row projected onto a thread** — same fact, thread scope, not a second definition; where they could differ, the Agent-states row governs the run and this row governs only what the thread card says about itself. Its progressive `Addressing with AI` status cannot be mistaken for the completed `Addressed — pending review` claim.

**Distinguishing the two, and not by animation.** Both states move, so motion cannot carry the distinction and does not survive `prefers-reduced-motion`. Three non-animated carriers do:

1. **Different words.** *Working* reads `Addressing with AI — you can edit or delete this comment once the run finishes.` The completed-state strings `Addressed`, `pending review`, and any count claimed for this run are forbidden while this run has delivered nothing.
2. **Different actions, and every action names whose it is.** *Working* adds no proposal controls for the current run and, per J10/UXR-Q9, duplicates no run control from the timeline card. Where an earlier delivery's changes are still undecided, their controls stay — **named by that delivery** the way J1's cards name one (`↳ 2 changes from Agent · 12m ago · [Go to →] [Accept both] [Reject]`), never "the earlier run": FR-CMT-7 preserves per-change outcomes across reopenings, so a twice-re-delegated thread can carry undecided changes from more than one delivery, and each is listed under its own. No count or control on the card is unattributed (Principle 1).
3. **No proposal content for this run.** No agent summary, no new count. What may appear is history, attributed as above.

**Where the carriers narrow, and what holds instead.** On a thread with no earlier delivery — the ordinary case, and #1122's — all three carriers hold. On a re-delegated thread that still carries undecided changes, carriers 2 and 3 hold only in **attributed** form: the words still differ, and every element the card shows names the delivery it belongs to. Attribution is the fallback carrier, and it is Principle 1 doing the work in the one place confusion is likeliest.

Stated as a rule, in the shape of §2's severity-encoding rule: **run state is never conveyed by animation alone** — always motion **plus** the words in the card-level status treatment.

**One clock and one control set per run, on the run's card.** Elapsed time, the activity feed, `[Stop]`, and any permission decision live on the run card in the timeline (J1 Phase A, J3 Phase A, J10), not on each thread: no existing surface puts a clock on the object being worked, and §4 records **one** stream, so per-thread timers and duplicate run controls would put synchronized accounts in it. Principle 3's "observable in real time" is discharged by that card; the thread card's job is to stop lying about itself and preserve the same active-run status treatment `5a.5`/`5b.5` already binds.

**The active-run status is not governed by state precedence.** Which state name a card displays when several are true follows the presentation order **Orphaned → Working → Reopened → Resolved / Dismissed → Addressed — pending → Open** — the two closure names share one rank, because a thread has exactly one current closure outcome (the closure rule below, D25). But *whenever a run is in flight for a thread*, that card keeps the `5a.5`/`5b.5` status line **regardless of which name it displays** — an anchor lost mid-run (D17 drift, J3's mid-run edge) makes the card read *Orphaned* without making the run silently disappear. The run's `[Stop]` stays on the timeline card in every case (J10, UXR-Q9).

**Card contents while Working.** The thread shows its original comment text and the status line, and stays usable — `[Reply]` and **both closure verbs**, `Mark as resolved` and `Dismiss`, remain live (Principle 3, never blocking; FR-CMT-21(b)) **as long as no linked change on this thread is undecided** — an earlier delivery that left one makes both inert, run or no run (FR-CMT-21(a), the closure rule below); `✎` and `Delete` do not, and have been inert since the run first targeted this thread (FR-CMT-10, the ladder below). The document stays editable. Provenance stays visible (Principle 1): the status line names the actor and, in a batch, its run.

**Manual resolve during a run is acknowledged, not swallowed.** *Working* outranks *Resolved* in the presentation order, so a resolve pressed mid-run would otherwise change nothing visible — the silent no-op FR-X-5 forbids, at the point of action. The card therefore states the fact in place: `✓ Resolved — the agent is still working; anything it delivers will bring this thread back.` A resolve pressed **before** the run reaches this thread (rung 2 below) displays as an ordinary *Resolved* thread and picks up that same line at the moment the run starts work on it — the transition where *Working* begins outranking it. Either way the card keeps naming *Working* while the run lives, and the resolve is real: it is what the thread recomputes to if the run ends without delivering.

**Both closure outcomes stay available while a run is in flight — so long as no linked change is undecided — and one acknowledgment covers both (D29, FR-CMT-21).** Dismissal behaves here exactly as the manual resolve above does: the card states the fact in place — `⊘ Dismissed — the agent is still working; anything it delivers will bring this thread back.` — keeps naming *Working* while the run lives, and the closure is real, being what the thread recomputes to if the run ends without delivering. **FR-CMT-16's in-flight-run limb is withdrawn**, so no closure availability turns on which rung of the ladder below a thread sits at: in-flight status by itself adds no closure refusal, because closure changes no text and no anchor — the hazard correction's refusal exists for (an agent delivering against text that moved under it, FR-CMT-10) is not this one. A live run is **not** evidence that nothing has been delivered: J7 records `STOPPED — partial delivery` as a legal outcome, and FR-AGENT-6's atomicity is about one delivery rather than about the run. Anything already delivered and still undecided is caught by the refusal that survives. The refusal that survives is **not rung-shaped**: while any linked tracked change is undecided, neither outcome may be taken by hand — the closure rule below. This is where closure and correction part company on one card: during a run `✎` and `Delete` are inert (J10) while `✓` and `Dismiss` stay live, which is exactly what `5a.5` already draws for the first three.

**When the run pauses for permission (J7).** A run that hits a gated action stops working and waits. The thread must not keep claiming activity: its status line becomes `⏸ Waiting on permission`. The permission block and its decision controls remain on the run card, never duplicated onto the thread. The thread remains *Working* by name (the run has not ended) but asserts waiting, not progress.

**No run control is duplicated onto a thread.** A permission request is a decision carrying content, and one request must have exactly one place it is decided (J7, non-modal and singular). J10/UXR-Q9 applies the same one-home rule to `[Stop]`: the thread states the active or waiting fact; the run card owns the controls and accounting.

**Batch (J4 "Address all… ▾") — Working means started, not targeted.** A batch run puts a thread into *Working* only once it has **started work on that thread**. Targeted threads it has not reached stay in their prior state — J4's ratified "unstarted threads untouched", and J7's Completed / **Not started** distinction, which a run-wide flip to *Working* would flatten. If a provider reports only run-level progress, **no** thread shows *Working* and the run card is the only progress surface: Emend never invents per-thread activity it cannot observe (Principle 7, FR-X-5). Stopping is **run-scoped** — there is no per-thread cancel inside a run — and remains on the batch run card.

**One acknowledgment ladder, so the request surface is never silent (UXR-Q1, §4).** A thread's own state changes only at the *Working* rung; the rest are acknowledgments on the card, in UXR-Q1's and §4's existing vocabulary. Every rung uses the same card-level status-line slot `5a.5`/`5b.5` defines; only its words change, and run controls remain on run cards. The ladder binds **two** classes of thread-local control, not one: it replaces every **run-starting** affordance with the rung, and it renders **correction** (`✎`, `Delete`) inert at every rung (FR-CMT-10 as amended; the copy is below). Closure is bound at no rung and stays live throughout (FR-CMT-21(b)) unless a linked change on the thread is undecided — that refusal is not rung-shaped and binds during a run exactly as it does outside one (FR-CMT-21(a)):

| Situation | What the thread shows in place of its run-starting affordances | Thread state |
|---|---|---|
| Request queued behind an active run (UXR-Q1) — single or batch | `⏳ Queued behind an agent run` — cancellation remains on the queued run card | unchanged |
| Run live; this thread targeted but not started, **or** per-thread progress not observable | `In an agent run` — no `◔`, no claim of activity; `[Stop]` remains on the run card | unchanged |
| Run has started work on this thread | The full *Working* presentation above | **Working** |
| A thread already in a live run that the author has deliberately added to a queued run as well (J4's opt-in) | That rung's presentation **plus** `⏳ also queued behind another agent run`; both runs' cards carry their own controls and accounting | as that rung |

**Correction is refused across the whole ladder; closure is refused at no rung of it (FR-CMT-10 as amended and FR-CMT-21, both ratified by CJ 2026-08-20).** The two requirements carried the same phrase — *"while a thread has an in-flight … run"* — and bind differently on purpose, so this document states the difference rather than leaving an implementer to infer it from identical wording.

- **Correction binds from rung 1**, the moment any live **or queued** run targets the thread, and holds until that run ends. The reason is mechanical, not cautionary: an address run captures the comment body and the anchor's quoted text **when it is submitted** (`src/comments/addressPrompt.ts` `@anchor:address-prompt-thread-summary`, single and batch paths alike), so a queued run already holds your comment as written, and revising or withdrawing it after that leaves the agent working from text that changed under it — the exact hazard FR-CMT-10 is named for. Rung 2 is defined here as *targeted but not started **or** per-thread progress not observable*, so a bind at *Working* alone would lapse entirely in the ordinary batch case, and a rule that fails closed cannot rest on a distinction the app admits it may not be able to draw.
- **Closure binds at no rung.** FR-CMT-16's in-flight limb is withdrawn (D29): `Mark as resolved` and `Dismiss` stay live at every rung, refused only while a linked tracked change is undecided. Closure changes no text and no anchor, so the capture that creates correction's hazard creates none for it.
- **So on one thread, at one rung, correction is refused and closure is not**, deliberately. It is the same shape as the other place these two features look alike and say opposite things (the closure rule below: J10's `✎`/`Delete` are *absent* on another actor's card, while `Dismiss` is *present* there). An implementer who reads one rule off the other gets it backwards in both places. The test in both is one word: correction touches **text**, closure does not.

**The copy at rungs 1 and 2, in the one status slot the ladder already uses.** `✎` and `Delete` render inert exactly as `5a.5` draws them for an active run — muted, never removed, one reason at card level because one cause blocks two controls — and the rung's existing line gains the reason rather than growing a second block:

| Rung | The line |
|---|---|
| Queued (rung 1) | `⏳ Queued behind an agent run — it already has this comment as written; you can edit or delete it once the run finishes.` |
| Targeted, or progress not observable (rung 2) | `In an agent run — it already has this comment as written; you can edit or delete it once the run finishes.` |
| Started (rung 3, *Working*) | J10's ratified string is unchanged: `Addressing with AI — you can edit or delete this comment once the run finishes.` |

**Neither new line claims activity**, which is UXR-Q10's constraint: *"it already has this comment as written"* states what was handed over, not what the agent does with it, and it stays true where per-thread progress cannot be observed. The second clause is J10's, verbatim.

**And neither line names the run, because nothing in this app can (UXR-Q17, ratified by CJ 2026-09-02).** These rungs were first written as `⏳ Queued behind sermon-review` and `In sermon-review`, against a run display name that was never built: `start_run` `@anchor:start-run-command` takes no name and no run-carrying wire shape holds one, so the name was not merely unplumbed — there was nothing upstream to plumb. CJ's ruling words all four naming sites without one rather than mint a mechanism for a label an ad-hoc batch would have nothing to put in: rungs 1 and 2 above, the run card's `[Stop]` in the ladder table (which is what it already renders), and the also-queued modifier. The acknowledgment's substance is unchanged — the clause that carries it is J10's, and the rest never depended on which run it was, only on *that* a run holds the comment. **Do not read this as licence to identify the run by whatever string is to hand:** `skill` and `workflow_run` `@anchor:thread-birth-provenance` are written at thread birth and name the review pass that *raised* the comment, so a rung rendering either would name the wrong run, and `providerMeta.ts`'s `displayName` `@anchor:provider-display-name` names the provider rather than a run.

**The rule is over the class of thread-local affordances, not a list of buttons.** J4 records three ways to start an address run for a thread — `[Address with AI]` on the thread card, the same action in the editor's comment popover, and `[Try again — add guidance…]` on a reopened thread — and the rule binds the class: **while a live or queued run targets a thread, every thread-local affordance that would start another run is replaced in place by that thread's current rung, on whichever surface it appears.** The comment popover shows the rung exactly as the timeline card does — a sentence the **not-yet-ratified** proposal immediately below asks CJ to qualify for a surface that does not read the run queue, and which until he rules stands unqualified. J4's batch sheet `[include them too]` is the sole exception: a deliberate collection-level opt-in, never a re-delegation button on the thread itself. Any future thread-local run-starting affordance inherits the prohibition without amendment.

**Ruling — #2258 / UXR-Q19 (CJ, 2026-10-04): one thread shows the same acknowledgment rung in Timeline and the editor popover.** The earlier proposal allowing a queue-blind popover to show rung 2 while Timeline showed rung 1 is withdrawn. When the one queued-run projection positively contains an exact run ID holding the thread, both surfaces show rung 1. If that ID is absent or its queue membership is unknown, a held thread shows rung 2 unless its own started-work fact puts it on rung 3; neither surface guesses that a run is queued. The ladder table's “Run live” row includes a queued request for this honest rung-2 fallback, and its line still claims only that a run holds the comment. This changes no thread state, control availability, or run control placement. TD-15's 2026-10-04 revisit records the chosen App-prop route from Timeline's queued cards to the popover; there is no second run-queue reader.


**The ladder never withdraws a live acknowledgment.** While a request on this thread is live — queued or running — the card never falls silent, never reverts to a run-starting affordance, and its acknowledgment only ever **gains** information: a rung is never traded down, and the fourth row above is a modifier on rungs 2–3 rather than a step past them. When the request **ends**, the run-starting affordances return and the thread's landing is the exit table below: a thread the run reached carries its note; a thread it never reached is untouched and carries none, because the note is owed only where the user was shown work in progress. The run card holds the account either way.

**Collapse and scale (§4 "Timeline at scale").** §4 collapses *resolved* threads, *fully decided* change-sets, and *successfully completed run cards* by default, expand-on-click; it records no affordance for collapsing an arbitrary card. A thread with a live run is in none of those sets — *Working* is not resolved, and its run card is not completed — so no collapsed *Working* rendering is specified, and none is needed. Stopped and failed run cards likewise stay expanded, because J7's outcome and recovery context must not hide behind a disclosure. This is derived from §4 as amended by #1725, not an independent thread-state rule.

**Exit transitions — Working is a transition, never a resting place.** *Prior state* means the thread's state **recomputed from current facts** when the run ends — Open, Reopened, Addressed — pending, **Resolved or Dismissed** (a closure taken mid-run stands, whichever outcome it was — FR-CMT-21(b)), or Orphaned; the list is illustrative, the recompute is the rule. This guarantee rests on **FR-X-5** and **Principle 7** (degrade visibly, never silently) and **J7**'s "resolves honestly" — *not* on FR-AGENT-6, which is document-scoped and says nothing about thread state; FR-AGENT-6 is cited below only where document state is at stake.

| Run outcome for this thread | Thread lands in | What the card says |
|---|---|---|
| Change(s) delivered | **Addressed — pending** | Today's behaviour: agent summary, linked-change count, `[Go to →]`, accept/reject (FR-CMT-2/7). Delivery is atomic (FR-AGENT-6) |
| Delivered into a thread the user resolved mid-run | **Addressed — pending** | The manual resolve is cleared and the card notes the sequence: `You resolved this while the agent was working — 2 changes arrived after.` A proposal is never hidden under a collapsed *Resolved* line |
| Delivered into a thread the user dismissed mid-run | **Addressed — pending** | The same rule as the row above, and now the requirement's own words: a delivery into a closed thread clears the closure and lands it here, **whichever outcome had closed it** (FR-CMT-21(b)). The card notes the sequence: `You dismissed this while the agent was working — 2 changes arrived after.` A proposal is never hidden under a closed thread |
| Agent replied without proposing a change (FR-CMT-4) | **Prior state** — **Open**, labelled *"defended — no change proposed"*, on a thread that was open; a thread the user closed mid-run **stays closed**, with the defence readable beneath the closed card | J4's existing push-back path — no new state. Only a **delivery** clears a closure (FR-CMT-21(b)): a defence proposes nothing, so nothing is hidden under a closed card and there is nothing to clear. Reopening a thread the user deliberately closed, in order to show a reply that asks for nothing, would be motion nobody asked for (Principle 2) — and the defence you end up agreeing with is J11's clearest case, so this is the row that would undo the feature's own best moment. **Where the user learns of it:** the closed card collapses with the hidden set, so the run's own card in the timeline reports the defence as it reports every other outcome, naming the thread (§4's one stream, J7's honest accounting); revealing the hidden set shows the defence on the closed card itself. The thread surface is quiet because the thread is closed, not because nothing happened |
| Anchor lost mid-run (D17 drift) | Card names **Orphaned** and keeps the active-run status line; `[Stop]` remains on the run card | If the run then delivers, the delivery follows J1 Phase B (and UXR-Q3 for a dirty tab) and the thread follows D17 re-anchor/orphan handling; the thread records the outcome |
| Run stopped by the user from the run card | **Prior state** | `Address run stopped — nothing proposed for this comment.` — persistent until the user's next action on the thread |
| Run failed or crashed | **Prior state** | `Address run failed — nothing proposed for this comment. [Try again]`; the reason and J7's `[Retry from here]` live on the run card |
| Batch ended part-way, per-thread progress observable | Per thread, by its own outcome | Delivered → Addressed — pending; a thread that **was** *Working* → prior state + the stopped note; threads never started are **untouched and carry no note** (J4 edge state) |
| Batch ended part-way, per-thread progress **not** observable | Per thread: delivered, or untouched | No thread was ever *Working*, so **no thread carries a note** — the note is owed only where the user was shown work in progress. The run card reports what it can honestly know in this mode: **delivered vs. not delivered**, not J7's started / not-started shape, whose premise this mode denies |
| An in-flight marker outlives its run (restart, lost completion) | **Prior state** + the failed note | A thread whose run is not live must never present as *Working* indefinitely |

**How failure is visible without surprising anyone (Principle 2).** No modal, no toast, no focus steal, no auto-scroll. The note appears in place on the card the user already associates with their request, and the state moves **back** to where they left it. A terminal failure additionally raises the OS notification J7 already defaults on for *blocked or broken*. Returning to Open silently is not an option — that is the silent no-op FR-X-5 forbids.

The last row is a UX guarantee, so implementation must clear the in-flight marker on cancel, failure and crash. **These are UX guarantees; the Architect owns the run-progress signal, the marker's lifecycle, whether per-thread start is reported, how a thread deep-links to its run card, and how a run's target set is known to the comment surface.**

Provenance dimensions on every thread: author (you / agent / reviewer-name / skill-name), source (local / review "R", skill-finding with severity chip), publish state (local-only vs synced-to-review), revision state (unedited / **edited**, carrying the revision time — shown only where the comment was published or another actor has replied, FR-CMT-13).

**Correction is confined to your own words, and its refusals are a closed list (D24).** Only the actor who authored a comment or reply may revise or withdraw it, in author and reviewer mode alike; nothing in this app removes or rewrites another actor's comment. Both corrections are refused **from the moment any live or queued "Address with AI" run targets the thread until that run ends** (FR-CMT-10 as amended 2026-08-20 — the whole acknowledgment ladder above, not only its *Working* rung), and withdrawal alone is additionally refused while any linked tracked change is undecided — revising a body orphans nothing, so the two refusals are deliberately **asymmetric** (FR-CMT-10). Those are the only two refusals: a thread that is resolved, reopened, orphaned, or published is still correctable by its author. Journey: J10.

**Closure has two outcomes, and no surface may state the wrong one (D25).** A thread closes as **Resolved** — *this was dealt with* — or as **Dismissed** — *considered, and not accepted; nothing was changed*. They are peers on one axis and never coexist (FR-CMT-15). Nothing else about closure changes: a dismissed thread leaves the open count, hides with the resolved ones, is excluded from `Address all…` and from the comments the agent is offered (TD-1), and raises no completion warning. **Reopening returns it to every one of them** (FR-CMT-19/20) — the list reads the same in both directions, which is what makes closure reversible rather than merely undoable in principle. Journey: **J11**.

- **Presentation order becomes Orphaned → Working → Reopened → Resolved / Dismissed → Addressed — pending → Open.** The two closure names share one rank because a thread has exactly one current closure outcome; *which* of them a card displays is a fact read off the thread, never a precedence decision.
- **The outcome is never carried by geometry or color alone — always the word.** This is §2's severity-encoding rule applied to closure, and it is the whole point of the feature: Dismissed reuses the resolved *mechanics* and never its *claim*. No `✓ Resolved` pill, no *resolved* string, and no count of addressed or fixed findings may include it (FR-CMT-15, FR-REVIEW-3). A reader looking at a muted, collapsed card must still be told which of the two happened.
  - **Where there is no room for a word, the rule becomes negative and absolute: never the *other* outcome's mark.** The gutter icon is 24×20px and `docs/design/specs/comments.md` draws exactly three states for it — Open (`#B5871F`/`#FBF3E1`), Selected (`#2F5D8C`/`#E4EBF4`), Resolved (`#6E9179`/`#EDF4EF`, `✓`). A dismissed thread is anchored and hides with the resolved set, so it has *some* gutter treatment, and if it inherits the green `✓` the **document** surface asserts *resolved* for a dismissal — precisely what FR-CMT-15 forbids, on the surface the reader looks at most. So the requirement at that size is stated the other way round: a dismissed anchor **may not carry resolved's glyph or fill**, must be tellable from both Open and Resolved without resting on hue (a distinct glyph, as severity uses a letter beside its color), and carries the word one click away on the card its icon selects. What the mark *is* belongs to `docs/design/`, is undrawn today, and is in the brief (UXR-Q12).
  - **The anchor highlight follows resolved's precedent rather than inventing one.** Closed is closed: a dismissed thread's anchor drops the open-state highlight (`#FBF3E1`) for the same reason a resolved one does, and `docs/design/specs/comments.md` draws no closed-state highlight to inherit. The `#E4EBF4` **selected** treatment is about selection, not outcome, so it is unchanged when a dismissed thread is selected — selection asserts nothing about closure.
- **A closed thread states its outcome wherever it is legible at all** — the card, its collapsed line, and the external reviewer's copy of it (FR-CMT-17, D20). Where a hidden set contains a dismissal, the reveal control takes the neutral `N hidden threads` form for the same reason a withdrawn placeholder already forces it (J10): `N resolved threads hidden` is a claim about every item it counts.
- **Closure availability is one rule for both outcomes (D29, FR-CMT-21), and it constrains the *manual* act only.** **(a) While any linked tracked change is undecided, neither `Mark as resolved` nor `Dismiss` is available** — inert, stated in place, with the route named rather than only the obstacle: accept the changes and the thread resolves itself (FR-CMT-3/7); reject them and it reopens, and may then be dismissed. No comment action decides, discards, or orphans agent work in a protected document (D21), and a hand-pressed resolve over an undecided proposal would additionally assert what FR-CMT-7 denies. **(b) While an addressing run is in flight, both stay available**, acknowledged rather than swallowed (above). FR-CMT-3/7's *automatic* resolution once the changes are decided is a different path and is untouched, and neither condition binds `Reopen`, which writes no closure. The property to check the surfaces against: **no closed thread ever holds an undecided linked tracked change.** Two consequences implementers meet: `Mark as resolved` becomes **inert in a state where it is live today**, which no mock draws (UXR-Q12); and closure's rule is no longer correction's, so one card during a run carries inert `✎`/`Delete` beside live `✓`/`Dismiss`.
