- **UXR-Q12.2 is closed (D29, ratified by CJ 2026-08-20).** The two conditions are judged separately, not by one symmetric rule: refuse **both** closure verbs while a linked change is undecided, and allow **both** while a run is in flight (D29, FR-CMT-21). The refusal copy still names the honest route, and no surface leaves `Mark as resolved` live beside an inert `Dismiss`.
- **Dismissal is offered on another actor's card; correction is not.** J10's `✎` and `Delete` are absent on words you did not write, because the per-author log makes moderating them a different feature. Dismissal is the opposite case — it records *your* judgment of what you read and moves nothing another actor wrote (D25: triage, not moderation) — so it is offered on every thread whatever its provenance. The two rules look alike and say opposite things; a surface that copies J10's absence rule onto dismissal has the feature backwards.
- **Dismissal closes a thread and nothing smaller (FR-CMT-17).** There is no dismissal of an individual reply: the reply block's own `⋯ ✎` cluster (`docs/design/specs/comments.md`) gains no `Dismiss` item, and dismissing a thread that carries an agent defense or another actor's reply closes the thread with every reply still readable beneath it. This is the second place dismissal and correction differ in *shape* rather than in degree — J10 acts at reply level and is confined to your own words; dismissal acts at thread level and is confined to no one's. **And it hides nothing beneath it**: a dismissed thread is **closed**, so its replies, the agent's defence among them, stay readable on the closed card and reveal with it. J10's current child-first rule also prevents deletion of a parent while any reply remains live (CJ 2026-09-30).
- **Both closure outcomes are reversible by hand, and the requirement now says so (D28, FR-CMT-19/20; the UX half was CJ's UXR-Q12.3 ruling of 2026-08-19).** `Reopen` is offered on a **closed** thread and nowhere else — an open thread has no closure to invert, and a **withdrawn** placeholder is *not* a closed thread: withdrawal is terminal, so it offers no reopen and no reopen resurrects it, whatever the arrival order of records (FR-CMT-19, FR-CMT-13, J10, P5). It is the author's act, on a thread of any provenance, **author mode only** (reviewers close nothing and therefore reopen nothing, FR-COLLAB-2); it is on the closed card itself — to hand while a just-closed card still holds its place in the column (J11), and found by revealing the hidden set once the card has joined it, since there reaching the card *is* revealing the set; it is **not confirmed**, because closing again is its inverse exactly as it is closure's; it **decides no agent work**, so a thread auto-resolved by accepted changes reopens as an ordinary open thread while those changes stand on their own in the change sidecar (D21); and in v1 it carries **no reason or note of its own** (FR-CMT-20). Where it lands is the *Reopened* condition above — the requirement's answer, not a recompute this document invents.

**Reply collapse (UXR-Q7.3, ratified by CJ 2026-08-05).** A thread with **2 or more replies** collapses its middle replies behind a `▸ N more replies` row; the newest reply always stays visible below that row, so the thread never hides its most recent state. A thread with one reply renders it inline with no collapse row. Expansion is per-thread and **not persisted** (`expandedThreads`). When a closed card — **Resolved or Dismissed** — is revealed, its replies remain readable and this same reply-level rule applies. This is distinct from §4's "Timeline at scale," which collapses a whole closed thread to one line in the chronological stream by default — the two compose. Appearance for reply collapse is binding in `docs/design/specs/comments.md`; dismissal appearance remains undrawn (UXR-Q12).

*Revisit from dogfooding:* at exactly two replies the rule hides one reply and spends a collapse row to do it, so it nets roughly nothing and costs a click; it starts paying at about four. The threshold is captured as drawn because matching the appearance doc beats guessing a different number without usage data — but it is the first thing to re-examine once real threads exist.

### Tracked-change states (D5, D8, D21)
Pending → Accepted / Rejected. Attributes: author + author-type icon, timestamp, group (substitution pair, section group, **change-set** = one agent delivery), optional agent note ("why"), optional linked comment.

**Group closure is not bounded by the change set** (P4b, CJ 2026-07-21 / #416). Deciding any member decides its whole group all-or-none, and a group may straddle two change sets — so a change-set-scoped decision can decide changes that arrived in a *different* delivery. P4b carries no scope qualifier; this is P4b applied, not amended. The user-facing consequence is the decision-provenance inventory below.

**Two populations, distinguished by construction (TD-16; #1804).** A **recorded** change joins a durable change record: it has an author, an author-type, a timestamp, and the group the backend asserted. An **unrecorded** marker — human-typed CriticMarkup, an imported or pre-Emend file, a marker whose record no longer joins, or any marker in a document with no identity to join against — has none of those. It is still a tracked change: it renders, navigates, and can be accepted or rejected, and group closure applies among unrecorded markers only. **What it never acquires is attribution**, and no surface may supply one it does not have. Where a redline is wholly unrecorded, the surface says so rather than showing an unexplained accept/reject pair (J12 Phase C). This distinction is not untitled-specific — imported and hand-typed CriticMarkup on an ordinary file-backed document are unrecorded too.

### Change-set decision provenance (UXR-Q5 — ratified by CJ 2026-07-22)

A change's *decided* state and *who decided it* are two different facts, and a change-set card is not always the surface that decided its own changes. Five states, per card:

| State | Meaning | Signal on the card |
|---|---|---|
| Self-decided | Every id this card retired, it decided itself | Nothing extra — the existing counts are the whole story |
| Reached beyond the set | This card's own action decided ids **outside** its change set (group closure, P4b) | `↳ also decided N linked changes outside this set` — a non-interactive accounting line in the card body, stated **after** the action |
| Decided elsewhere | Ids of **this card's own** changes were decided by another surface (in-editor triage, a document-scope bulk op, or another card's sweep) | `↳ N changes decided elsewhere` — a persistent status line, not an event |
| Reversed | A decision this card counted was undone — the editor returned those changes to Pending (§3.3 undo policy; `invertedEffects` restores markup *and* attribution) | The card **returns to its prior state and leaves no residue**: the ids go back to pending, the counts restate, any reach or decided-elsewhere note that named them is withdrawn. No "was decided" badge, no ceremony — an undo is an ordinary editor action, not an event in the record |
| Unverifiable | The card cannot prove a decision report addresses its own parse — its `doc`/generation do not match the reporting one | **No count and no note at all.** The existing UXR-Q4 mismatch affordance stands unchanged and owns the exit |

The **Reversed** row is a transition, not a resting place: it restores `Pending` in the per-change table above, and returns the card to Self-decided (or to nothing decided at all). The timeline reversal path shipped in #680; the row remains listed because it defines that path's required no-residue result — see rule 1.

Two rules bind this inventory:

1. **Honesty is post-action, not pre-action — and it is only honest if reversal reaches the card.** Over-reach is reported once it has happened; nothing warns before a bulk op. A pre-action prediction would need the timeline to model group structure it deliberately does not own (tech-design §3.3 — the timeline never writes text itself), and putting a confirmation in front of a routine triage action would make this the one place the app blocks the user on their own deliberate act — against the inline, never-modal pattern J7 set for the one interruption this app *does* stage.

   **What undo covers, precisely.** A decision and its swept-in co-members are one transaction and therefore one history event, and `invertedEffects` `@anchor:invert-tracked-change-effects` restores the markup *and* its attribution together (tech-design §3.3 undo policy, finding M9). Ctrl+Z therefore reverses the **document** whole. Since #680, the same restoration transaction also reverses the timeline's **account** of it: each restored id leaves the decided accumulator and returns to Pending on its card, withdrawing any assertion tied to that decision.

   **So the reach note was conditional, and this was a sequencing constraint, not a footnote.** Post-action honesty is sufficient *only* once reversal reaches the card — i.e. once the **Reversed** row above is real. #680 satisfies that prerequisite: the note may now ship, provided it withdraws with the ids it names. This is the price of rejecting a pre-action confirmation, and it is stated rather than assumed. It is **not** an argument for adopting the confirmation: that rejection stands on §3.3 and J7 independently of undo.

2. **A card never asserts a count it cannot verify** — the UXR-Q4 gate applied symmetrically. UXR-Q4 already refuses to let a mismatched card *decide*; the same ordinal-collision reasoning refuses to let it *absorb* a decision report, because a bare change id means nothing across parses. A mismatched card therefore stays frozen at its last verifiable state rather than reconciling on a collision.

   The rule is **self-applying, and has two instances here** — a card absorbing a report it cannot verify (the collision), and a card retaining a claim after the fact under it has been undone (rule 1). Both are the same shape: the card stating a decided state that is not a fact.

   Cross-document and stale-generation reconciliation become *routable* — rather than refused — on the condition UXR-Q4 named: stable change identity (TD-4). **#493 is the home of that fix**, and CJ folded the report-direction defect itself into it on 2026-07-21; this delta states the rule the fix must satisfy and does not propose the mechanism. The same condition is what would reopen the "links nowhere" question for **every** row of this table, not just the last two.

### Agent states (FR-X-3, FR-AGENT-3/6)
| State | UI |
|---|---|
| Idle | Input ready |
| Working | Live activity feed (verb + file), elapsed time, Stop button |
| Needs permission | Inline permission card (allow once / always / deny) — never a modal over the editor |
| Delivering | Change-set/comments materialize as a timeline card |
| Completed | The successful run card collapses by default to `▸ ■ Completed 0:17 · 5 files read · 2 changes`; expanding restores the full persisted activity feed unchanged |
| Continuity recovered | Treat the run as a successful completion, then append one ordinary agent chat card before its reply: `Couldn’t resume the previous agent session. A new session was started; your conversation history is still visible.` The notice persists with the transcript, introduces no modal/toast/focus move, and repeated terminal updates never duplicate it |
| Interrupted / failed | Timeline card states what completed vs. rolled back (FR-AGENT-6) |

**Completed-run summary (#1725, CJ 2026-08-18).** The collapsed line is grounding plus outcome, never merely elapsed time. It derives both counts from the run card's own persisted `activity` `@anchor:run-status-activity-field`, so the same data seeds off-screen virtualization and a rehydrated card without a second read: `Reading` contributes file reads; `Writing`, `Editing`, and `Delivering` contribute changes; every count is over distinct normalized paths, not activity lines. A known zero reads `no files read` and a known-zero change count is omitted (`▸ ■ Completed 0:09 · no files read`). A counted activity whose path is absent makes that metric explicitly unknown (`files read unknown` / `changes unknown`) — only a complete read-free feed may claim `no files read` (Principle 7, FR-X-5). Running, queued, cancelled, Stopped, and Failed cards remain expanded; the latter two retain J7's failure reason and recovery context in view. Appearance reuses `docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md`'s existing collapsed-row pattern; the `·`-separated completed-run content is a note for the next generated visual refresh, never a hand edit to `docs/design/`.

**Amendment — the collapsed line also carries J13's outbound disclosure (ratified by CJ 2026-09-03, UXR-Q16.6; #2237).** A run started from any of the composer-less routes has the run card as its **full** record of what document context rode with it (J13 Phase B), and this rule collapses that card by default — so without this amendment the sole disclosure for those runs left view at the moment the run succeeded, and the transcript could no longer be asked what a past turn carried. CJ ruled that it survives the collapse. **The constraint that governs how, ratified with it:** the outbound line sits beside `5 files read`, which is an **inbound** count, and the two must stay legible as **opposite directions rather than one list** — the rule J13 Phase B already binds from Principles 1 and 5, now binding here too. **The treatment is not specified here and must not be**: the run card has no hi-fi appearance in `docs/design/` at all, and #1391 holds a prior claim on that same undrawn card, so *how* the two directions are kept apart in one collapsed row is a brief for CJ's design project (J13, *Appearance is a brief*). This amends the content of a ruled string, so it is recorded at this section — the one that owns it — rather than only inside J13, and J13 cites it rather than restating it.

### Outbound turn-context disclosure states (J13; FR-CHAT-4, D30, #2237) — **ratified by CJ 2026-09-05 (DH-7)**

What the author is shown about the document context a **submitted agent run** carries out of the app. FR-CHAT-4 sends an `open_documents` envelope `@anchor:timeline-open-documents-block` on every submitted run; this inventory is about **disclosure**, and disclosure is keyed to **the author's own text being in play** — not to the envelope, and not to whether the text in fact went.

| Active document at submit | What the envelope carries | Composer standing line | The submitted turn's card |
|---|---|---|---|
| Nothing open (workshop, J2) | `active: null`, empty `also_open` — no entry, so **no statement** | absent | absent |
| Path-bearing, `unsaved: false` — including FR-ED-7's tab whose file changed underneath it | identity only, and **no statement**: the entry offers a readable `path`, so (b) does not reach it and no `content` clause applies | absent | absent |
| Path-bearing, `unsaved: true` (FR-ED-3's dirty flag), **within the size bound** | identity + `content` + statement **(a)** — the `content` is the document's current text and the file at its `path` is behind it | `<name> goes as edited, not as saved` | `carried <name> as edited` |
| Path-bearing, `unsaved: true`, **over the size bound** (D31, #2238) | identity + statement **(c)** — the unsaved changes are not in the file at its `path`; **no `content`** | `<name> goes by name only — over Emend's size limit; save it so the agent can read it` | `carried <name> by name only — over Emend's size limit` |
| Untitled / scratch (D12, FR-ED-8) — `unsaved: true` by construction, whatever `TabRecord.dirty` `@anchor:tab-record-dirty` says — **within the size bound** | `label` + `content` + statement **(a-pathless)** — the `content` is the document's whole text and it exists nowhere on disk, so there is no file to read for it | `“<label>” goes in full — it has no file to read` | `carried all of “<label>”` |
| Untitled / scratch, **over the size bound** (D31) | `label`, `path: null`, `unsaved: true` + statement **(c-pathless)** — the text was too large to include and exists nowhere on disk, so this entry itself supplies no route to it; **no `content`** | `“<label>” goes by name only — over Emend's size limit; Save As… so the agent can read it` | `carried “<label>” by name only — over Emend's size limit` |

**Collapsed run-start routes (UXR-Q13.3 and UXR-Q16.4; CJ 2026-09-30, 2026-10-01, and 2026-10-02):** if `[Address with AI]` starts a run from a comment while Timeline is collapsed, that comment card links to the run without replacing the column. It also says `Unsaved text sent with this run` **only** for the two rows above whose envelope carries `content` (dirty path-bearing within bound, or active untitled within bound). The card keeps one link to its latest accepted run after completion and across tab switches; a later accepted run for that same comment replaces the link and its submit-time cue together, while an admission with no run changes neither. The link remains for an over-bound run, but the cue is absent because no text was sent; the run card retains the table's full by-name-only disclosure. This cue is neither the standing composer line nor a second transcript record. A run-starting action **outside a persistent comment card** instead expands Timeline to expose the new run and this table's applicable disclosure on the submitted-turn card that owns it (J13 Phase B). The rule includes `Address all…` and file-backed selection `Ask agent`; it does not invent a sent-text claim for a clean or D31-withheld turn. If the current facet excludes the run, select `All` and reveal it; otherwise preserve that facet. Only this auto-expanded path overrides UXR-Q2's normal no-facet-change submit rule.

**The two over-bound rows were written against a requirements amendment that had not yet landed, and this delta could not be ratified ahead of it — a bar now discharged.** FR-CHAT-4 **as it then read** said `content` is sent "neither normalized nor truncated" and that an active untitled document *always* carried it. CJ's ruling of 2026-09-02 on **#2238** bounds the snapshot at 256 KiB — sent whole or not at all, never truncated — and is recorded as **D31**, reaching the constitution as an FR-CHAT-4 amendment under its own card (PR #2248, open at drafting; since merged). The two rows were written against that then-proposed text, including its statements **(c)** and **(c-pathless)** and its ruling that **the bound is not waived for a pathless document**, because waiving it would make the exceptional path the unbounded one. Until D31 merged, these two rows described a payload the *binding* text did not yet carry, which is the whole reason for the bar above; **D31 has since merged, and CJ ratified this delta on 2026-09-05**. **Their copy is ruled** (UXR-Q16.3, CJ 2026-09-03); the *facts* they state are D31's.

**Why these are rows rather than an omission.** An earlier revision of this delta left the untitled-over-bound case out on the ground that its payload was undetermined — correct when it was written, and wrong the moment the presence rule below was restated: a pathless document renders a line *whether or not the bound withheld its text*, so with only one untitled row an implementer would render `“<label>” goes in full` in the exact state where nothing went. A state the rule makes reachable must be a row; leaving it implicit is how a deliberate omission turns into specified behaviour by accident.

Two conditions change neither surface: a **dirty background tab** (identity and dirty state only — none of its text leaves; a *pathless* one carries statement **(b)**, which is the fifth of #2248's five and the only one this table's *active*-document rows never reach) and an **attached quote** (FR-CHAT-3's own bar is its own disclosure, and the two are independent).

**All five statements are named above rather than inferred, because #2248 asks for exactly that.** It mints (a-pathless) as a second condition alongside (a) "so a downstream surface can key off the second directly rather than inferring it from a rule about the first," and this table is that downstream surface. Its closure rules are exhaustive — exactly one of (a) and (a-pathless) with any entry carrying `content`, exactly one of (b), (c) and (c-pathless) with any entry whose text was withheld — so every row states which it carries, including the two that carry none. **A note on #2248's own downstream note**, so the mismatch does not surprise anyone at sign-off: it asks this delta for "a fifth row" while itself minting two over-bound statements. Two statements need two rows; the table has six, and the count in that note is one short rather than this table being one long.

**This is a genuinely new category of state in this section, and that is deliberate rather than an oversight.** It maps onto none of the existing tables — comment/thread, tracked-change, agent, review, protection, voice — because it is keyed to a **document's relationship to a turn**, not to the agent's state or the document's own lifecycle; extending *Agent states* would have put an outbound fact about the author's text inside an inventory of what the agent is doing. The `free`/`finalizing` precedent later in this section (*Untitled document surface states*) sets the convention of saying so out loud.

The rules beneath the table, each argued in J13:

- **The author's text being in play is the trigger, not the envelope.** Identity *is* new information — D30 records that before FR-CHAT-4 "the agent is never told which document the user is looking at," and clause (2) exists precisely to tell it. What is true, and what carries the ruling, is narrower: **naming an open document exposes no text a clean turn withheld**, and the two facts are not proportionate — identity is metadata about attention, `content` is the author's unsaved words. Add the practical half: a line on every turn would habituate the surface into invisibility and spend its whole signal on the ordinary case, which is the opposite of what Principle 7 keeps a surface for.
- **Silence means one thing only: *your text is not in play this turn*.** The presence rule is therefore **the active document is one whose text FR-CHAT-4 would send — dirty, or pathless — whether or not D31's size bound then withheld it**. Keying presence to `content` actually shipping would make the over-bound state silent, and silence there would be the one genuinely false claim in the table: the author's document is dirty, the ordinary case sends it, and this time it did not.
- **Standing, not per-turn.** The condition is ambient and unasked-for, so the statement is a standing condition of the composer, not an object attached to a pending message. That is what makes it true for the run-starting routes that never touch the composer, which are the great majority — J13 states the **rule** rather than a census (Phase A): it is over **every route that submits an agent run, of which the composer is one**.
- **Which and whether, never how much.** The line names the document the way the author already names it — its name for a path-bearing document, and for a pathless one the tab's own disambiguated label, which is the string FR-CHAT-4 sends as `label` precisely so the same words identify it to the author and to the agent. It states **no byte, word or character count**, including in the two over-bound rows: the disclosable fact there is *whether* the text went, not how much of it there was. **The two over-bound composer lines also carry the route out — `save it so the agent can read it`, and `Save As… so the agent can read it` where the document has no file — and they are the only lines that carry a remedy at all** (ratified by CJ 2026-09-03, UXR-Q16.3). That is not an exception to *which and whether*: it is Principle 7's own distinction between degrading **visibly** and degrading **usefully**, applied at the one state where the author has an act that actually helps. Every other row either sends the text or has nothing to remedy, so no other line has a remedy to offer. A count would spend the surface on a number the author cannot act on. **And it never says what the agent will do with the payload** — the remedy names what the agent *can* read, which is a capability of the app's own doing rather than a prediction of the agent's behaviour, and that is the line the third constraint at UXR-Q16.3 draws; which FR-CHAT-4 gives the app no standing to claim; *over Emend's size limit* names a bound the product chose, where *too large to send* would blame the document for a limit stdin delivery was designed to avoid.
- **Absent is absent, never zero — and the two over-bound rows are not an exception.** No `carried: 0`, no "nothing carried", no empty row holding the space, the same rule and the same reason as the agent card's grounding indicator (`docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md`). The absent treatment would say *this turn carried nothing*, which is true of an over-bound turn as far as it goes — and that is the trouble, because it is **true and radically incomplete**: it reads as *there was nothing to carry* where the fact is *there was, and it did not go*. Absence cannot carry that distinction, so the over-bound state gets a line rather than the absence, and absence keeps meaning the one thing bullet 2 gives it.
- **True at the instant of submit.** The composer line derives from the same state, at the same boundary, that FR-CHAT-4 captures the envelope at. What the line says when the author presses Send is what the envelope carries — including for a run that is queued and dequeued later (FR-AGENT-7).
- **Visibility, not control.** Neither surface carries a dismiss, an ✕, or an opt-out. FR-CHAT-4 is a MUST with no opt-out; a control would be a requirements change, not a journey (J13, *what this journey does not admit*).

### Timeline facet empty states (§4; #1727)

Once the current project's initial transcript load has **succeeded**, an active facet whose filtered stream is empty states that completed result instead of leaving an unexplained blank panel. It does **not** show an empty message while transcript loading is still in flight; that interval keeps the prior silent treatment rather than making a false claim about the project's history. A facet with one or more matching cards shows its ordinary stream and no empty message.

| Active facet | Completed-empty message |
|---|---|
| All | `No activity yet` |
| Chat | `No chat yet` |
| Comments | `No comments yet` |
| Changes | `No changes yet` |
| Findings | `No findings yet` |

The message is one quiet line with zero margin, `12px 14px` padding, the muted-text color token, and `12.5px` type. CJ approved this copy and treatment on 2026-08-20, resolving the explicit `docs/design/specs/state.md` Known gap; the next generated visual-layer refresh should carry the same disposition rather than reopening it.

**Transcript unavailable (#1813, ratified by CJ 2026-08-26).** A rejected transcript rehydrate is neither completed-empty nor still loading: preserve every activity card already in memory, show **Activity unavailable** with `Earlier activity could not be loaded.` and `[Try again]`, and never show any completed-empty message for that failed load. Retry re-runs transcript rehydration only; it neither clears retained cards nor restarts another activity. A successful retry returns to the ordinary stream or, only when it genuinely loads no matching history, the facet's completed-empty message above. This uses the established compact unavailable/error treatment and its existing tokens rather than introducing a new visual contract; no modal, toast, focus move, or auto-scroll accompanies it.

### Unattributed internal failure (§4; FR-X-5; #2102)

Some failures inside Emend reach no part of the app that can name them: nothing reported them, and the failure carries no record of what was doing the work. Emend therefore knows only that something did not finish, plus whatever text the failure carried. It does not know which feature, whether the author asked for it, what was affected, whether it recurs, or how serious it is — and the message it shows claims none of those.

| State | Meaning | User-facing behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Unattributed internal failure | Something inside Emend did not finish and no part of the app reported it | The §4 status region reads `Something inside Emend didn’t finish. If something doesn’t look right, this may be why. <detail>` — **one line**. `<detail>` is the failure's own message text, placed **last**, inserted verbatim, and omitted with its preceding space when there is none; there is no `Try again.` fallback, because there is nothing to try again. Emend adds no prefix, terminator, count, timestamp, or other per-occurrence text of its own, so one failure always produces one set of words. The line names no document, project, review, or path of its own, and offers the region's ordinary `Dismiss` and no other action |

**One line, and the band's height is never what gives way.** This state occupies one line of the status region on every surface it appears on, and never wraps to a second. What does not fit is **clipped, not wrapped**, in a fixed order: `<detail>` gives way first, then the second sentence, and the first sentence last. So the reader loses the failure's own trailing text before they lose the words that say what the line is for — which is the whole reason `<detail>` is placed last. The full detail text remains present for selection and for assistive technology; only its display is clipped. This holds at every window width the app allows, without depending on any particular one. The bound follows the discipline of the band this region establishes and the review bar joins (J5's fixed-height constraint and UXR-Q14.6 — #1804/#2111, ratified by CJ 2026-08-28).

**It takes no focus and causes no scroll.** The region is one polite live region: the line is announced once on arrival without moving keyboard focus, without scrolling either pane, and without changing what is selected. An author mid-sentence keeps their caret and their place. The line and its `Dismiss` are reachable in normal tab order.

**`<detail>` here is a knowing exception to the plain-language rule, and the exception is a class rather than this one site (CJ, 2026-09-05, UXR-Q20.3).** The ratified requested-action sentence interpolates text the backend authored for a user; this one interpolates an arbitrary internal failure's own text, which will routinely be developer-shaped. It is still shown, because a reason a reader must decipher is more use than no reason at all, and FR-X-5 asks for the reason. Every word Emend itself writes on this surface stays plain.

**The class: a failure Emend *can* attribute may carry the failure's own text too.** J2's write-failure branch already promises *"the actual reason"*, and its refused-close disclosures carry one, so the earlier bound — *"this one interpolation"*, *"precedent for nothing else"* — was being read as a cap on a rule it was only ever the first instance of. It is replaced by the constraints, which are this state's own and now bind every site: the failure's text is placed **last** and inserted **verbatim**; it is what gives way **first** when the line is clipped, before any word Emend wrote; the sentence around it names a subject the author actually acted on; no severity is encoded; and in reviewer mode the text is omitted. What stays forbidden is Emend writing developer-shaped words **of its own** — the exception covers text it is quoting, never text it is authoring. No such disclosure is reachable in reviewer mode today, because a reviewer window mounts no document region at all, so that limb binds the next site rather than describing a live one.

**Message identity governs repetition, in both directions.** The region holds one message at a time. A failure that would produce the words already showing changes nothing on screen and is not announced again — the line already says the only true thing there is to say, and repeating it adds no information. A failure producing *different* words replaces the line and is announced. Dismissal completes that same rule rather than contradicting it: **a dismissed message does not return while it would say the same words.** That memory is per window, is never persisted, and is cleared when **a new request starts or a different message occupies the region** — so §4's guarantee that starting a new request lets a repeated failure be announced again holds exactly as written. The author is told once per distinct failure, is never nagged by a repeating one, and is never silently left without a *new* one.

**A message about something the user asked for outranks this line.** While the region reports a user-initiated request — in progress or its outcome, including J9's project-open states, an out-of-project refusal, and reviewer mode's `Opening review…`, `Review unavailable` and `Review ended` — an unattributed failure neither replaces it nor queues behind it to surface later, stale. A single-slot region cannot report two failures at once, and of the three things that could give way — the specific actionable message, this unactionable one, or the band's height — this one is the right loss: no action is forfeited either way, because this line has none. **The cost is stated rather than implied:** a failure that occurs while such a message is showing and never recurs is not separately reported. What occupies the region in that window is itself a failure notice the reader is already reading, carrying its own actions, so the interval is not silence. A message that was never shown is not remembered as dismissed, so a recurrence once the region is free is reported normally. This line fills the region only when the region is otherwise clear.

**Severity is not encoded, because Emend has not got it.** The line reuses the status region's single existing treatment. Colouring it would assert a seriousness the app cannot know, which is the same falseness in pixels that *the requested action* would be in words. §2's severity encoding rule governs findings, not this region.

**Reviewer mode (J6).** A reviewer sees the same line in the reviewer status region, and **`<detail>` is omitted there.** The failure's own text is outside Emend's control and can name a local filesystem path, which Principle 7 forbids showing a reviewer and which Principle 4 forbids for a second reason — it leaks the existence of managed machinery a reviewer is never meant to know about. The reviewer also has no use for it: no project, no repair surface, no author-side action. Principle 7 requires a reviewer's failures to be stated in review terms; Emend cannot restate a failure it cannot attribute, so it does not pretend to — the wording is deliberately mode-neutral and identical in both modes.

**This line is dismissible in reviewer mode, exactly as in author mode.** J6's non-dismissible rule names two persistent states — `Review unavailable` and `Review ended` — and each ends at an exit: a successful retry or `[Back to reviews]`. This line has no terminal condition of its own — no retry that can succeed, and no `[Back to reviews]` to route to — so a non-dismissible version would be permanent furniture no reviewer could ever clear, which is worse than the silence it replaces. `Dismiss` therefore behaves identically in both modes, governed by the same message-identity and dismissal-memory rules stated above.

### Render-failure fallback states (J15; FR-X-5; #2130) — **CJ's rulings recorded; the remainder proposed**

An uncaught error during React's render discards the tree that owned the app header, shared status region, panes, rails, focus and scroll positions. Nothing survives in which to report it — which is exactly what separates this state from *Unattributed internal failure* above. That line reports an escaped rejection **while the shell is still alive**; this is the surface after rendering destroyed the shell. The two are complements by mechanism and by consequence, and neither substitutes for the other. The fallback states only facts available **outside** the failed tree, and offers only an act that starts the display again; it never treats a pane, buffer, run or review operation as surviving merely because its last pixels were on screen.

**Status of this section — what is ruled, and what is not.** CJ ruled on this design once in the PR thread, **2026-09-04 at 12:24Z**, in three numbered items, closing *"This resolves the behavioral choice. PR #2301 / issue #2130 remain at the design gate only for the two platform artifacts and any resulting prose alignment."* Items 1 and 2 are transcribed below under their own signposts, and item 3 — *implementation waits for refreshed Windows and macOS visual artifacts, which CJ commissions and binds here* — is discharged by turn 12. **He ruled four more things on 2026-09-07:** native close; the close refusal as an **assembly** rather than a set of whole strings; the **ordinary English list punctuation** of its clauses; and that **a queued reviewer event is not a close guard**. Each carries its own signpost below, and all four share one provenance note there, because none of them is in this thread or in any other artifact in this repository. **What is left proposed is narrower than the behavioural choice CJ resolved, and it is three items:** the close refusal’s three drawing decisions (**UXR-Q24.1**); the mount/focus/announcement contract, whose only open question is whether Principle 2 needs a carve-out, and none is proposed (**UXR-Q24.2**); and three of the eight §6 absences (**UXR-Q24.3**). **Two former items are not in that list:** the close-refusal strings and the reviewer row’s durable clause were **closed** on 2026-09-07 and are recorded below, and the reload control’s silence about what a remount does not preserve was **split to #2437** together with the status region’s higher rank and dismissal memory across a remount. `docs/design/specs/render-failure-fallback.md` §3 says its strings are *"`../ux-design.md` §2's, verbatim … If they differ, §2 wins"* — a forward reference this section now supplies.

**Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 2), verbatim:** *“The four mode-safe copy variants and **Reload window** as the sole action are approved, including the specified rejected/non-completing reload retry state.”* The rows below are that approved set, unchanged since he approved them.

| Last established context | User-facing behavior |
|---|---|
| Author mode, project established | Heading: `Emend can’t show this window right now.` Body: `Your saved documents are still in the project folder. Emend can’t confirm from this screen whether every unsaved edit was kept.` **Only where a run was working or waiting for permission**, append: `If an agent run was working or waiting for permission, it may still be active; reloading does not stop it or decide the permission request.` Then, always: `Reloading starts this window again from the last state Emend can restore.` Action: `[Reload window]` |
| Author mode, no project established | Heading: `Emend can’t show this window right now.` Body: `No project is open in this window. Reloading starts Emend again.` Action: `[Reload window]` |
| Reviewer mode established | Heading: `Emend can’t show this review right now.` Body: `A comment or suggestion that was in progress may not have been sent. Reloading starts this window again from the last review state Emend can restore.` Action: `[Reload window]` |
| Context not established | Heading: `Emend can’t show this window right now.` Body: `Reloading starts Emend again.` Action: `[Reload window]` |

**Context is established without consulting the failed tree, and an unestablished context is not guessed.** Where the last mode cannot be established from state outside the failed tree, the neutral row wins rather than an author or reviewer guess. Reviewer copy names the *review*, and names no project, path, managed checkout, branch, sync detail or author-side recovery control (Principles 4 and 7).

**The author copy separates durable files from session state, and asserts neither more nor less.** D12's file on disk is the source of truth, so `Your saved documents are still in the project folder` says where durable text lives without claiming a save completed; `Emend can’t confirm … whether every unsaved edit was kept` refuses the opposite overclaim, because this surface cannot inspect the tree that held those edits. There is no reassurance clause — never `Your work is safe`, `Nothing was lost` or `All changes were saved`.

**The reviewer row states the limit and no durable half, and after CJ's ruling of 2026-09-07 that is exactly right.** The author row is built on a durable/uncertain split; the reviewer row carries only the uncertain half. That read as under-claiming while `closeGuards` implied queued review work was at risk — J6 promises a queued event survives this class of failure, becomes `Not sent — review ended`, and *“remains in the local review record with `[Copy]`”* (FR-COLLAB-9). CJ closed it by narrowing the guard rather than by adding words to approved copy: a queued event is no longer a close guard (below), so the only reviewer work this surface speaks about is a composer draft that really may be lost. Principle 7 is satisfied in both directions — nothing is over-claimed, and nothing durable is described as at risk. **This narrowing puts `docs/design/` out of date, and that is recorded rather than left to be discovered:** `specs/state.md`’s `closeGuards` row and `specs/render-failure-fallback.md` §5a both still enumerate *"unsent reviewer comment or suggestion"* without the draft/queued distinction, and §5a still presents two whole refusal strings rather than the assembly. The folder is not wrong about appearance; it now describes a behaviour contract this document has changed. A bounded regeneration is owed, and it is CJ’s to commission — recorded with the other regeneration ask in §5.

**The agent sentence is conditional, and its absence is a state rather than a truncation.** It renders only where state held outside the failed tree establishes that a run was working or waiting for permission; otherwise the author-project body is the same row with that one sentence absent. No stale `working` or `waiting` row, elapsed clock, activity item, run card or permission control renders in either case.

**Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 1) — the whole-window fallback, and the narrow Principle 3 / J7 exception.** CJ approved proceeding with the whole-window React render-failure fallback, *"accepting the narrow Principle 3/J7 exception: while the fallback owns the window, live-run activity, permission UI, and Stop are unavailable until reload/remount."* This is a named exception to a ratified Principle, not an omission and not a gap, and it is scoped to that interval and nothing else. The conditional sentence above is the only acknowledgement that a run may exist, and it is deliberately an acknowledgement rather than a control — reloading neither stops the run nor decides its pending permission request, and the copy says so. **What makes the interval safe is already ratified in J7:** *"Ignored requests just wait — card shows `⏸ waiting 4m`; the agent never times out into acting."* An interval with no permission UI therefore cannot cause an unasked act, independently of anything this surface does. *This delta adds one requirement of its own, which is not CJ's:* on a successful remount the ordinary run card must show the current backend-owned state, including a still-pending permission request, rather than replaying the snapshot it last rendered — J7 territory, and proposed rather than ruled.

**Provenance of the four rulings dated 2026-09-07 — stated plainly, because a reader cannot verify them from this repository.** Four signposts in this section carry that date: **native close** (immediately below), the close refusal as an **assembly** rather than a set of whole strings, the **serial-comma punctuation** of three clauses, and **a queued reviewer event not being a close guard**. **None of them appears in the PR thread, in #2130, #2432 or #2437, or in any other artifact in this repository at the time of writing.** CJ gave them directly in a working session. That is the same durability failure this entry exists to correct — a ruling living in exactly one disposable place — and naming it is better than letting a later reader discover it. They are recorded as rulings because they are rulings; they are deliberately **not** softened into proposals to make them checkable, since that would misstate what CJ decided in order to make this document easier to audit. CJ has been asked to post them so an artifact exists beside this note; until that lands, this paragraph is their only record here, and a reviewer should treat them as an unverifiable recorded-decision delta rather than as text they can confirm. **CJ's 2026-09-04 rulings are not in this position:** they are verbatim in the PR thread at 12:24Z, and are quoted as such above.

**Ruled by CJ, confirmed 2026-09-07 — native close while the fallback is mounted.** **Provenance, stated because a reader will look for it and not find it:** this ruling is **not in the PR thread.** CJ's only dated comment there (2026-09-04, 12:24Z) contains no native-close ruling, and the question was first raised by an independent design review posted at 14:11Z the same day, two hours *after* it. `docs/design/` records the acceptance as *"Ruled 2026-09-04, and accepted as recommended"*; CJ confirmed directly on 2026-09-07 that it was accepted. It is ruled, and it was accepted outside this thread. The platform's own close affordance (Windows `✕`, the macOS red light) stays available, and J15 records a disposition where it previously defined none:

| Guard situation | Native close does |
|---|---|
| **No guard applies** — nothing unsaved, no untitled buffer, no active or awaiting run, no unsent reviewer composer draft | **Closes the window**, exactly as from the ordinary shell. Nothing is drawn; there is no fallback-specific confirmation |
| **Any guard would normally hold** — dirty or untitled author work, an active or awaiting run, an unsent reviewer composer draft | **Refuses.** The fallback stays mounted, the protected work stays protected, a mode-safe reason appears, and `[Reload window]` remains the only action |

Those two bullets are the whole of the ruling. `docs/design/`'s own primary records agree: CHANGELOG (10) §1 states it as exactly these two under *"Ruled 2026-09-04, and accepted as recommended"*, and the `12f` annotation block headed *THE RULING DRAWN HERE — Accepted 2026-09-04* carries only these two.

**Refusal, not a confirm-and-discard, and the reason is the copy's.** The dirty-close guard, the untitled-close guard, the run-refusal dialog and the reviewer send confirmation all lived in the failed tree. A `Close anyway` offered here would assert loss semantics the body has just declined to assert, directly above a sentence saying Emend cannot confirm whether every unsaved edit was kept. Recovering the real guards is what the one button is for. No `Close anyway`, `Discard`, `Stop` or `Send` appears — those are ordinary controls, and only a successful remount returns them.

**Three things the drawing decided, which the ruling did not — proposed, not ruled (UXR-Q24.1).** `docs/design/` files these separately from the acceptance, under its own heading *"Three decisions the drawing had to make"*, and this document keeps that line where the folder's mock and its ruling paragraph put it: (a) the refusal **reuses the one status slot** below the button rather than adding a second region; (b) that slot is **mutually exclusive** with the reload-failure line — one slot, one polite atomic `role=status`, whichever reason is current; and (c) **a refusal moves no focus**, leaving it where the user left it, with the platform's close affordance keeping its ordinary highlight. Each is sound and each is proposed. What *is* inside the ruling is that no document, project, path, branch, checkout or run is named in either mode, and that where only one guard holds only that clause renders.

**The refusal is assembled, not chosen from a set of whole strings. Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-07** (provenance above, with the other three rulings of that date)**.** A fixed opener, one clause per guard actually holding, and a fixed closer — which is what the composition rule above already requires and what a set of two whole strings could never supply. **Nothing CJ approved is rewritten:** the opener and closer are unchanged and appear in every rendering, and his approved author string is now the dirty-plus-run rendering rather than the only rendering.

| Position | Author | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|
| Opener | `Emend can’t close this window yet.` | `Emend can’t close this review yet.` |
| Middle | the clauses for the guards actually holding, in the order below, punctuated as an ordinary English list (see the rule) | `A comment or suggestion may not have been sent.` |
| Closer | `Reload the window to get the ordinary controls back.` | Same |

The author clauses, one per `closeGuards` `@anchor:render-recovery-close-guards` member, each true when that guard alone holds:

| Guard | Clause |
|---|---|
| Dirty buffer | `There’s unsaved work` |
| Untitled buffer | `An untitled document is open` |
| Active or awaiting run | `An agent run may still be active` |

**`An untitled document is open` states existence and claims no loss.** That is what makes it true in J2's `preserved-empty` and `restored-omitted-empty` cases, where closing discards nothing at all, and it stays clear of *dirty* and *unsaved* — the vocabulary J12 withholds from a pane that *"is never dirty and never shows a dirty dot"* and whose only dot means earlier unsaved work was not preserved.

**The clauses are punctuated as an ordinary English list. Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-07** (provenance above)**.** One clause stands alone. **Two are joined with `, and`.** **Three are separated by `, ` with `, and` before the last** — the serial comma. The first clause keeps its capital, later ones are lowercased, and the sentence ends with a full stop before the closer. That is the whole rule, and it generates all seven author renderings from the three clauses; it is stated as a rule rather than a table because an implementer can apply a rule, while a table only tells them what to match.

The two renderings worth naming:

| Guards holding | Rendering |
|---|---|
| **Dirty + run — CJ's approved string, byte-unchanged** | `Emend can’t close this window yet. There’s unsaved work, and an agent run may still be active. Reload the window to get the ordinary controls back.` |
| All three — the serial form | `Emend can’t close this window yet. There’s unsaved work, an untitled document is open, and an agent run may still be active. Reload the window to get the ordinary controls back.` |

The first is the form CJ approved and is what the rule must keep producing unchanged; the second is the form he ruled on 2026-09-07, and it is the only rendering where the choice between `, and` twice and one serial comma was ever visible.

**This closes a defect raised on this work before the `12f` wording was adopted, and adopted anyway.** An independent design review found that `closeGuards` *"includes any untitled buffer, while its only author work clause is `There’s unsaved work`"*, that J2 and `specs/recovered-document.md` retain close guards for `preserved-empty` and `restored-omitted-empty` *"where closing discards no current content"*, and that *"with either as the sole guard, 'There’s unsaved work' is false."* The refusal disposition was sound and the wording was not; the clause above is what that finding asked for.

**Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-07 (provenance above) — a queued reviewer event is not a close guard, so the reviewer refusal has one rendering.** `closeGuards` `@anchor:render-recovery-close-guards` previously held *"unsent reviewer comment or suggestion"* without separating a **composer draft**, which lived in the failed tree and is genuinely at risk, from a **queued append-only event**, which is not. J6 and FR-COLLAB-9 guarantee the queued event survives exactly this class of failure: it becomes `Not sent — review ended`, stops retrying, and *"remains in the local review record with `[Copy]`"*. Refusing a close to protect it would protect nothing. Only a composer draft holds the guard — and **that is what makes CJ's approved reviewer string true with no word changed**, because the only thing that can now trigger it is work that really may be lost:

- Reviewer, composer draft holding — `Emend can’t close this review yet. A comment or suggestion may not have been sent. Reload the window to get the ordinary controls back.`

**The same ruling is why the reviewer copy row no longer under-claims.** The author row states durable file text and then the limit; the reviewer row states only the limit, and that reads as under-claiming while the guard set implies queued work is at risk. Once queued events are outside the guard set, the row is describing the one thing that genuinely is — so Principle 7 is satisfied by narrowing what the surface protects, not by adding a durable clause to copy CJ approved.

**Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 2) — the one control and its outcomes.** `[Reload window]` is the sole in-surface action, and the rejected / non-completing retry state is approved with it. It requests **one** display remount and rehydrates only what the existing project, review and session contracts can restore. That activation authorizes nothing else — no save, submit, accept or reject, comment publication, permission decision, agent request or run stop — and the render failure attributes no outcome to any of them. There is no automatic retry: a loop could discard transient state before the reader has read the sentence explaining it. While a request is pending the control reads `Reloading window…`, stays present, focusable and focused, and is activation-inert; a second activation **joins** the pending request rather than starting another. On rejection or bounded noncompletion the fallback stays, the control returns to `[Reload window]` with focus retained, and the status slot says `Emend couldn’t reload this window. Try again.` — inserted **once** for that request's outcome, absent while a request is pending, and cleared by a later deliberate request before pending begins. Nothing counts attempts or claims progress. A remount that begins and reaches a new render failure produces a **new** fallback instance, not a mutation of this one.

**The one action is not gated against the work the close refusal protects. Split out to #2437 (CJ, 2026-09-07), as a design defect rather than prose alignment.** A remount discards whatever the session write does not hold, and J12's pathless pane has *"Autosave and its scheduler (**off by construction** — no path)"*, so its only durability is that write. A native close is **refused** while that same untitled work holds a guard, on the reasoning that protected work must stay protected — while the sole in-surface action proceeds against it with no guard, no confirmation, and no sentence saying so. The guard set is this journey's own, so the asymmetry is this journey's, and it is put to CJ rather than settled by adding a gate to a control he has approved as the sole action.

**Mount, focus, and the one announcement — proposed.** The fallback replaces the failed tree immediately, with no animation and no automatic retry. Focus moves **exactly once**, to the fallback's single `main` root — programmatically focusable, named by the heading, described by the body. That root is **not** `role=alert` and carries **no** `aria-live`, and there is no nested announcer: the focus move *is* the announcement. The next Tab stop is `[Reload window]`, the only other focusable thing in the window. Re-rendering the mounted instance moves focus nowhere and announces nothing again. The fallback owns a fresh viewport beginning at its top; it scrolls no surviving document or rail, because there is none, and claims no restored scroll position. **This is not an exception to Principle 2, and none is proposed (UXR-Q24.2).** `docs/design/` §4 calls it *"J15's narrow, named exception to Principle 2"*, but Principle 2's focus limb is scoped as written — *"never taken **unasked**: an agent delivery or an arriving permission request never pulls focus from where you are typing"* — and a render failure is neither of those, while the node focus previously sat on has ceased to exist. Recording a permanent carve-out in §1 for a case §1 never reached would cost more than it buys, so this document declines to propose one and asks CJ only to confirm the reading.

**Deliberately absent.** Two of these are CJ's under item 2's *sole action*: no `Dismiss` and no in-surface close or discard — dismissal would recreate the blank window this state exists to prevent, and closing stays the platform frame's job under the ruling above — and no second action, alternate route or safe-mode entry. **Three are proposed and unruled (UXR-Q24.3):** no raw error detail; no toast, standing status band or fifth region; and no reassurance clause as a standing prohibition. On the first, the ratified `<detail>` class (UXR-Q20.3) reaches a failure Emend *can attribute* under five constraints, one of which is that **the sentence around it names a subject the author actually acted on** — and this fallback arises from no user act at all, so that constraint cannot be satisfied and the class does not extend here. Reviewer mode is not open at all: the class already omits the text there, and Principles 4 and 7 bind independently. **The asymmetry is stated rather than hidden:** the sibling *Unattributed internal failure* shows `<detail>` in author mode for a recorded reason — *"a reason a reader must decipher is more use than no reason at all, and FR-X-5 asks for the reason"* — and this surface cites the same FR-X-5 and withholds it. Whether that asymmetry is right is CJ's, not this document's. On the third, the four approved copy variants already contain no reassurance clause; what is unruled is elevating that into a standing prohibition. Appearance absences — no alarm iconography, no responsive rule of its own — are `docs/design/`'s under the split of authority and are neither ruled nor proposed here.

### Review states — author view (D18, D20)
None → Preparing (branch/worktree/PR setup, progress) → **Live** (reviewer avatars, open-comment count, sync status dot) → Completing (decide-all-changes gate) → Completed / Cancelled.

### Review workspace states — reviewer view (J6; D9, D19, FR-COLLAB-2/2b/9)

Reviewer mode is a whole-window state selected before review content mounts, not author mode with controls hidden after render. The review set is its complete document namespace; no state below introduces a user-visible project root, an author tab/buffer lifecycle, or a route into J2.

| State | Meaning | User-facing behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Reviews waiting | No review is open | Show invited reviews and GitHub-account status; there is no project-home strip or project rail |
| Opening review | The selected review's managed checkout and first trustworthy snapshot are not ready | Keep reviewer chrome from the first frame and state `Opening review…`; never flash the author shell or expose clone, branch, folder, or filesystem controls (D9, Principle 4) |
| Ready | A trustworthy review-set snapshot and selected document are available | Show the selected document, review-only timeline, and doc-set dropdown. The dropdown lists only review-set documents; Comment / Suggest / Reply and correction of the reviewer's own comments are the only document verbs |
| Updating | A newer review snapshot is being received | Retain the last trustworthy document without moving the reader; the top bar states the update, and content changes land without a mid-read jump (Principle 2) |
| Offline — queued | The last trustworthy snapshot remains readable but sync cannot complete | Keep reading and locally authored comment/suggestion events available; mark them queued with the existing `⚠ will sync` status until convergence (FR-COLLAB-9) |
| Review unavailable | No trustworthy selected document can be shown, or a refresh cannot retain one | In §4's reviewer status region, state that the review or document cannot be loaded or updated and offer `[Try again]` and `[Back to reviews]`. Never translate the failure into `Missing on disk`, a project tree, or a local-file recovery choice (Principle 7) |
| Review ended | The author completed or cancelled the review while this reviewer was Opening, Ready, Updating, Offline — queued, or Review unavailable | Name the outcome and author (`CJ completed this review` or `CJ ended this review without completing it`) in the reviewer status region and offer `[Back to reviews]`. Completion, or cancellation with **Keep**, retains the last trustworthy workspace read-only and its synced events as ordinary history. Cancellation with **Discard** removes the synced review-comment history from that workspace and replaces the review rail with `The review history was discarded by CJ`; it never promises that shared history remains. In every variant, each locally queued event becomes `Not sent — review ended`, stops retrying, and remains in the local review record with `[Copy]`; returning to Reviews waiting never discards it. From Opening or Review unavailable with no trustworthy snapshot, show the ended shell and notice without inventing document content |

### Document protection states (D21)
Unprotected (agent may direct-write) · Protected: open / in-review / operation-target. Subtle editor badge only when protection derives from review ("In review · R1").

### Markup reveal preference (TD-2, FR-ED-1, FR-ED-4, P1; #2389) — **ratified by CJ 2026-09-06 (DH-7)**

**`Reveal markup at cursor` is an app-global, machine-local setting in Settings → General. It defaults off and persists across projects, windows, and restarts.** It is not document state, window state, display-mode state, or session state. A committed change applies immediately to every mounted author document pane, including an untitled pane (J12), and every pane mounted later starts from the persisted value. The setting uses the ordinary on/off row already drawn in `docs/design/` wireframe `1m`; it adds no toolbar or status-bar control.

The two view axes keep their existing ownership and precedence:

| Surface | `Reveal markup at cursor` off | `Reveal markup at cursor` on |
|---|---|---|
| **Rich text · Display: All** | Structural Markdown markers and CriticMarkup delimiters stay hidden regardless of caret position — **CJ's ruled default, 2026-09-06** | TD-2's established reveal-at-cursor behavior applies to both delimiter families |
| **Rich text · Display: Simple / None / Original** | Both delimiter families stay hidden | Both delimiter families stay hidden; the stored preference is retained, not rewritten. Returning to All restores its effect |
| **Markdown** | Raw Markdown and CriticMarkup are visible and editable | Raw Markdown and CriticMarkup are visible and editable; the setting has no effect |
| **Reviewer mode** | Fully rich, as TD-2 requires | Fully rich; the setting has no effect |

Changing the setting is a **view operation only** (P1). It never edits the Markdown string, marks a document dirty, saves, or enters the document's undo history, and applying it to an open pane does not move the caret, selection, scroll position, or focus (Principle 2). It controls delimiter reveal only: whether tracked-change content and redline styling appear remains the four-mode projection's job (FR-ED-4, J1). Display mode remains a separate window-scoped, unpersisted axis (UXR-Q7.2; J12); switching it may suppress reveal but never changes the durable preference.

**The Markdown segment is the fallback, and it is sufficient to ship independently.** With reveal off, `Rich text | Markdown` is the one-click route to see and directly edit structural Markdown and CriticMarkup delimiters; switching back to Rich text hides them again. #2388's format group reduces how often an author needs that route, but this preference does not depend on it and does not change the format group's scope.

### Untitled document surface states (J12; D12, FR-ED-8; #1804)

The untitled pane's editing surface, as distinct from the untitled *document's* lifecycle states, which are J2's.

| Dimension | Values on an untitled document | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Editing surface | `mounted` — always, from pane mount | Never a read-only variant |
| Save route | `save-as-only` | Three entry points, one latched act — § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus* |
| Dirty state | **none — the tab is never dirty** | No dirty dot, no saved-state slot, either platform |
| `Rich text \| Markdown` | both segments live; window-scoped | Not keyed to a document identity |
| Display mode (FR-ED-4) | all four live; window-scoped; control in the status bar | Computed from the buffer's own CriticMarkup |
| Tracked changes | wholly `unrecorded` — never `recorded` | § *Tracked-change states*; the count states the degraded fact, never silence |
| Redline toolbar cluster | absent at 0 changes, present at ≥ 1 | Never visible-but-disabled; unmounting hands focus to the document |
| Comments | **present** — anchored on the buffer and adopted with it on Save As | CJ, 2026-10-01; FR-ED-8 |
| Selection popover (UXR-Q14.4) | `Comment` and `Quote to chat` present; `Ask agent` absent | CJ, 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01: comments need no project path, but a selection-scoped agent run does. Save As restores `Ask agent` without recreating the editor or losing the selection (J12 Phase F). Appearance is `docs/design/` `9g` |
| Agent reach | **protected; unaddressable, but its text is supplied — within D31's bound** | Inside D21's set as an open tab; no channel *addresses* it — TD-1 and D3 are both path-keyed. **Amended by #2237 (ratified by CJ 2026-09-05):** under FR-CHAT-4 an *active* untitled document's whole text rides every submitted run as reference data, so *unreachable* is now true of the write channel alone. See J12 Phase E and § *Outbound turn-context disclosure states* |
| Dictation (UXR-Q14.1) | Present in the editor toolbar, with no project path needed | Text-shaped, so it travels with the buffer |
| Freeze | `free` or `finalizing` | Owned by § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*, which binds every pane — this row records only what it means here |

**`free`/`finalizing` is a genuinely new category of state in this section** — it describes a *window-level attempt* projected onto a document surface, and it maps onto none of the existing tables (comment, tracked-change, agent, review, protection). That is deliberate, not an oversight: the freeze is the first cross-cutting state this document has needed to record.

### Format-group states (J14; FR-ED-1, FR-X-1, TD-2; #2388)

| Dimension | Rule |
|---|---|
| Availability | Present on every editable document pane, file-backed or pathless, in Rich text and Markdown. A control is live while its exact query is `active` or `inactive`; `pending` makes only that control temporarily unavailable. J12's finalization freeze makes the whole toolbar inert and remains the separate pane-wide exception. |
| Exact query | Per control: `active`, `inactive`, or `pending`, for the exact document revision and selection the command would act on. `active` means the whole inline target (Bold/Italic) or every touched eligible nonblank block (Heading 1/Bulleted list) has the format. `inactive` includes a mixed target. Blank and excluded blocks assert no structural state. A collapsed target reflects what typed text would inherit (Bold/Italic) or the containing block (Heading 1/Bulleted list). `pending` means the authoritative parse cannot yet answer for that exact target; it says nothing about applicability. |
| Projection | Active state and command meaning come from the same Markdown source in both views. Visible or hidden delimiters never decide them, and changing view changes neither. |
| Pending | Remove `aria-pressed`; set `aria-disabled="true"` and `aria-busy="true"`; expose `Formatting state is still loading.` Keep the control focusable so existing keyboard focus stays put. The control is unavailable and unpressed. When the exact parse arrives it becomes `active` or `inactive` in place, without a selection transaction, focus move, scroll, announcement, or editor-mode change. |
| Applicability | A format act is atomic over its semantic target. YAML frontmatter and fenced or indented code are outside every format target; Bold and Italic also exclude inline code. Heading 1 and Bulleted list may cross inline code because they edit only block structure, but tables and raw HTML are excluded from both block commands. If a target touches a region excluded for that command, Emend changes nothing and reports ``<Control> isn't available for this selection because it includes <reason>.`` The control is the invoked **Bold**, **Italic**, **Heading 1**, or **Bulleted list**; the reason is the first excluded region in document order: `YAML frontmatter`, `code`, `a table`, or `HTML`. Focus and selection do not change. This refusal never describes `pending`. |
| Block normalization | Among eligible blocks, a mixed structural selection normalizes every touched nonblank block to the chosen structure; when every touched block already has it, the same act removes that structure from all. A task item counts as a Bulleted-list item; removing its bullet preserves `[ ]` or `[x]` as authored literal text. A soft-line continuation belongs to one semantic block. A multiline paragraph becomes one Setext H1, preserving its line breaks; a single-line paragraph uses ATX `# `. Toggling either H1 form off removes only its heading structure. |
| Target | Toolbar focus does not replace the editor selection. The stored target follows document transactions and is mapped through the format transform. Inline markers remain outside the resulting selection; an empty inline target leaves the caret inside the inserted pair. Block-prefix edits retain the same logical block/text target. |
| Focus | Pointer activation from the editor leaves or returns focus there; `Enter`/`Space` on a focused toolbar button keeps focus on that button; a direct shortcut leaves focus in the editor. Every path preserves the transformed editor target. |
| Shortcuts | `Mod-B` and `Mod-I` are in scope and resolve through the platform keymap. Heading 1 and Bulleted list have no shortcut in this tranche. A shortcut whose control is `pending` makes no edit, remains focused in the editor, preserves the selection, is never queued or replayed, and reports `Formatting is still loading for this part of the document. Try again in a moment.` The line clears when that exact target resolves if it still owns the shared status region. |

The controls expose their full names — **Bold**, **Italic**, **Heading 1**, and **Bulleted list** — and expose the same pressed truth visually and to assistive technology. The generated glyphs are placeholders; this ruling chooses no icon.

### Project rail states (J2; #698)

The project rail is an **author-mode navigator**, present in both workshop and document-authoring layouts. It contains two labelled sections — **Documents** and **Skills** — that answer different questions: *what can I open as an Emend document?* and *what repo-owned grounding can I inspect?* Neither is a literal disk view (Principle 4). The Architect owns enumeration and discovery; this section owns visible membership, state, and interaction.

**Documents** preserves the folder hierarchy of user-visible FR-ED-9 document paths. Every other type is absent rather than greyed or inert. Git machinery (`.git` and its contents), Emend sidecar/state paths, other generated/tool/cache internals, and every dot-prefixed path are absent. A folder with no Documents-owned document at any depth is absent too. The heading says it lists documents, and the empty state names that the project may contain other files, so filtering never claims the project itself is empty (Principle 7).

**Skills** renders the FR-SKILL-5 discovery result, not a project-rooted file tree. A discovered **skill** is a disclosure row showing the repo-authored `SKILL.md` name and description; expanding it shows that skill's owned directory material (`SKILL.md`, document references, and other FR-ED-9 files) without inventing rows for non-documents. The description is the skill format's when-to-use signal; Emend synthesizes none. A discovered **workflow** is a leaf row, not a directory disclosure: it shows discovery's filename-derived name plus its project-relative path, and activation opens that Markdown workflow. The workflow body is the portable description and when-to-use authority; Emend does not fabricate metadata the workflow format does not carry (D1, D7, FR-SKILL-1/5).

The sections are mutually exclusive by canonical project-relative path. Ownership precedence is: a discovered workflow file; otherwise the deepest matching discovered skill root; otherwise Documents. A workflow file inside a discovered skill root therefore appears only as its workflow leaf and is omitted from every containing skill disclosure. Every workflow-owned path and every remaining skill-owned path is suppressed from Documents even when it is a non-dot Markdown path such as `claude_skills/example/SKILL.md`. If discovered skill roots nest, the parent entry omits the nested skill's subtree and the nested skill remains its own discovery row. Thus a path appears at most once, and a successful open marks exactly that one row active. Documents' dot-prefix rule does not apply to Skills: a discovered skill under `.claude/` remains visible there.

The two headings label groups inside **one ARIA tree**, not two widgets. The tree keeps one roving tab stop across all visible rows. Up/Down cross the section boundary; Home/End address the whole rail; Right expands and Left collapses or returns to the parent for folders and skill disclosures; Enter/Space activates a document/workflow or toggles the focused disclosure. Section labels and inline status messages add no second tab sequence. Collapsing the rail hides the whole tree, closes no tab, preserves both sections' expansion and the active document, and leaves one labelled keyboard-reachable restore control.

Documents enumeration and Skills discovery transition independently. A completed section renders immediately while the other is loading, updating, empty, or failed; one failure never erases or relabels the other section's trustworthy result. Automatic refresh and the header's `[Refresh]` request both sources, but each section retains and annotates only its own last trustworthy rows until its new result completes. `Project unavailable` is rail-wide only when the root itself cannot be read.

| State | Meaning | User-facing behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Loading — Documents | The first document enumeration has not completed | In Documents, show `Loading documents…`; Skills may already be usable, and workshop conversation, restored tabs, and editing remain usable |
| Loading — Skills | The first grounding discovery has not completed | In Skills, show `Finding skills and workflows…`; Documents may already be usable |
| Updating — Documents | A later document enumeration has not completed | Retain Documents' last-known rows and active marker with `Updating documents…`; do not present them as the new snapshot |
| Updating — Skills | A later grounding discovery has not completed | Retain Skills' last-known entries with `Updating skills…`; do not present them as the new discovery result |
| Update failed — Documents | Enumeration failed but the project root is still available | Retain only Documents' last-known rows, mark `Couldn't update documents`, and offer `[Try again]`; Skills keeps its own state |
| Discovery failed — Skills | Skill/workflow discovery failed | Retain any last-known Skills entries, mark `Couldn't update skills`, and offer `[Try again]`; with no trustworthy prior result, show the failure rather than `No skills found`. Documents keeps its own state (Principle 7, FR-X-5) |
| Ready — Documents | Enumeration completed with at least one Documents-owned path | Preserve folder hierarchy; document rows are actionable, folders disclose children, and the sole active path is marked |
| Ready — Skills | Discovery completed with at least one skill or workflow | Show skill disclosure rows and workflow leaf rows under the ownership rule above |
| No documents | Enumeration completed with no Documents-owned document | Keep the section with `No documents yet — this project may contain other file types`; workshop conversation remains usable and can produce a draft (D12, J2) |
| No skills | Discovery completed successfully with no skill or workflow | Keep the section with `No skills found`; never use this state for a failed or incomplete discovery (Principle 7, FR-SKILL-1) |
| Collapsed | The author hid the rail to reclaim width | Show only the labelled restore control; preserve section states, expansion, tabs, and active document |
| Project unavailable | The project root cannot be read or no longer exists | Show `Project files unavailable` with available detail and `[Try again]`; preserve workshop use, open tabs, and local buffers (Principle 7, FR-X-5) |
| Non-document path | The path is not an FR-ED-9 document type | Show no row in either section. A stale link, restored session, or other document-open route refuses visibly with the reason; never a blank editor or silent no-op. **Restored legacy exception (#2515):** a tab with an explicitly retained unsaved buffer becomes an editable *untitled* recovery document labeled by its former path, while opening that unsupported path is still refused; a clean tab closes. An omitted buffer is never invented. This does not prevent D3 direct agent writes outside the document boundary |
| Deep nesting | A visible owned path exceeds the rail width | Preserve hierarchy; truncate only the visual label while hover/focus and the accessible name expose the full project-relative path |
| Unreadable path | An owned file or directory cannot be read | Keep the known row with a warning, no open action, and `[Try again]`; an unreadable directory discloses no stale or invented children. A later successful section refresh restores it |
| Missing on disk | A previously listed path is absent in a completed update and no in-project move was confidently correlated | Remove its sole row. If activation races with disappearance, leave the current document unchanged and report through the shared status region (FR-X-5). If open, keep its tab/buffer and mark no rail row active; enter the specialized **Missing on disk** lifecycle—never offer Reload for an absent file, and offer Recreate only when confinement capability permits |
### Project-open routing states (J9; D26, FR-X-10/11/12; #759)

Every project-open entry point resolves against **which instance holds the chosen project**, never against what the window the user clicked in happens to contain, and the resolution is the same whether or not a run is active (FR-X-10). The states below are the *choosing* instance's; the arriving instance has no states of its own, because it is an ordinary instance (FR-X-12, J9 Phase D).
