The subject of every outcome is the project the user chose, not the window they chose it from — three of the four successful outcomes leave the choosing window unchanged, which is exactly why each one says so (FR-X-11).

| State | Meaning | User-facing behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Requesting | The chosen project's owning instance is being resolved; the launch or raise has not completed | Status region reads `Opening <name>…`. The choosing instance stays fully usable and nothing in it is torn down in advance — no tab closes, no run pauses or migrates, no timeline clears, the protected set is untouched (D21). A repeat request for the **same** project while one is in flight joins it; it never starts a second one. A request for a **different** project queues in request order behind the active one; the region adds `· N more queued`, and states each request's outcome in order before advancing to the next. Every request's FR-X-11 deadline starts when the user asks, not when its queue turn begins; exhausting that deadline fails that request visibly without an effect. Dismiss remains available while the request or a retry is in flight; dismissing hides the line without cancelling the request, and the line returns with the terminal outcome |
| Adopted here | The choosing instance held no project | The existing path, unchanged: J8 Screen 2 — grounding card, then workshop mode (J2) or restored tabs (FR-ED-3). The status clears; the opened project is the outcome |
| Already open here | The choosing instance already holds that project | Nothing reopens: no tab closes and reopens, no timeline reload, no scroll, no focus change (Principle 2). Status region: `<name> is already open in this window.` |
| Open in another window | Another live instance holds that project | That window is brought to the front; the choosing instance is unchanged. Status region: `<name> is open in another window — brought it to the front.` The raise disturbs nothing in the raised instance — its run, queue, timeline, tabs, and protected set are as they were |
| Opened in a new window | No live instance holds that project | A new ordinary instance opens on it (Phase D); the choosing instance is unchanged. Status region: `Opened <name> in a new window.` |
| Failed | The bounded wait elapsed, or the launch/raise could not be performed | Status region states the failure **and its reason**, with `[Try again]`, on one line. The failed outcome requires `failureRoute: "undetermined" \| "raise" \| "launch"` `@anchor:project-failure-route-values`; the renderer selects its leading sentence from that field alone and never infers it from `reason`: launch → `Emend couldn’t open <name> in a new window.`, raise → `Emend couldn’t bring <name> to the front.`, undetermined → `Emend couldn’t open <name>.` The reason tail is one of the seven exact J9 strings below. Both the choosing instance and the target project are exactly as they were, with no partial ownership anywhere. Never a lock-conflict message, a "project is in use" prompt, or a force-unlock affordance — leftover machinery from an instance that is gone is never the user's problem to resolve (FR-X-10; Principle 4's *invisible machinery* applied beyond git) |
| Close blocked by a run | A close was requested while a run is active | The project stays open and the refusal is stated with its reason: `Emend can’t close <name> while a run is in progress. [Show the run]`. The action preserves a facet that already contains the run, otherwise selects `All`; reveals the exact card with 12px top clearance (whole only when `cardHeight + 12px <= timelineViewportHeight`, otherwise top-aligned); focuses its container, never `[Stop]`; and leaves document scroll and caret untouched. The refusal survives activation and clears automatically when the blocking run completes, but the refused close never resumes. If that clear removes a focused band control, focus returns to the recorded stable authoring target with `preventScroll`, falling back to the connected shell close-attempt target; focus already outside the band is not moved. There is no stop-and-close shortcut in v1 — the user stops the run through the existing controls (FR-X-3) and closes again (FR-X-11) |

The welcome surface can host Requesting, Open in another window, and Failed; Adopted here clears the region there. It cannot host Already open here, Opened in a new window, or Close blocked by a run. Authoring and workshop can host every state except Adopted here, whose successful path replaces those surfaces. Reviewer mode has no project-open entry point, so none of these states reaches it and J6's separate persistence rules are unchanged.

The seven exact failure reasons are `@anchor:route-failure-reasons`: `project_unavailable` → `That folder isn’t available to Emend.`; `open_failed` → `Emend couldn’t finish setting up that project.`; `deadline_expired` → `It took too long, so Emend stopped waiting.`; `registry_unavailable` → `Emend couldn’t keep track of its open windows.`; `launch_failed` → `The new window didn’t start.`; `owner_unavailable` → `The window that has it isn’t responding.`; `activation_refused` → `The system wouldn’t bring that window to the front.` Each sentence is deliberately broad enough to remain true for every condition its existing reason code covers; the reason taxonomy is not narrowed.

### Voice / dictation states (FR-ED-5)
Idle → (first use: Downloading model, progress) → Recording (live partial as ghost text) → Transcribing → back to Idle · Error states: no device / mic permission denied / model missing. The mic button appears on **all three surfaces** — editor toolbar, chat input, comment composer — with identical states.

**Editor dictation target (CJ, 2026-10-02):** accepting dictation fixes the ghost and Final to the original target. At a collapsed caret, later caret or selection moves do not retarget them; ordinary document edits map that original point with the text. If dictation starts with a nonempty selection, Final replaces the original selected range even if the caret later moves; #2549 implements that selected-range behavior. In Rich text Display None or Original, the mic stays disabled.
**Chat and comment draft placement (CJ, 2026-10-02):** a Final transcript replaces the selection captured when recording starts, or inserts at that original caret if the selection was empty. Later caret or selection moves do not retarget it. Partial text stays provisional outside the editable draft; Final changes the draft only and never submits it.
**Device and permission recovery (CJ, 2026-10-02):** on any of the three mic surfaces, activating a no-device, no-default-device, selected-device-unavailable, or permission-denied error opens Settings at Voice. It does not retry Start. The Voice heading takes focus; closing Settings returns focus to the initiating mic if it remains visible and available, otherwise to the Settings trigger. Backend-unavailable, model/download and transcription errors keep their existing retry actions. This resolves the named error-click behavior left open by generated `docs/design/` refresh (20); its appearance remains for the designer to draw.

### Severity encoding rule (accessibility)
Severity is never conveyed by color alone: always **color + letter chip** (`● C` `● H` `● M` `● L`) — in margin marks, timeline dots, and report cards alike.

### Agent concurrency (UXR-Q1)
**One run at a time per project.** New requests while a run is active become visible **queued cards** (`⏳ queued behind an agent run · [Cancel]`, name-free per UXR-Q18, ratified by CJ 2026-09-03), executed in order. Parallelism *within* a run (the agent's own subagents) is invisible and allowed. Queued cards can be cancelled before they start; the active run's `[Stop]` never touches the queue.

Order chosen so later journeys reuse earlier vocabulary.

| J2 | Find/open a document or brainstorm → draft materializes (repo-level chat) | **Ratified; project-rail amendment #698 ratified by CJ 2026-07-30** |
| J3 | Skill/workflow review → findings triage | **Strawman below — under review** |
| J4 | Address comment with AI (single + batch) | **Strawman below — under review** |
| J5 | Start a review (author side) | **Strawman below — under review** |
| J6 | Reviewer's first session (invite → redline → suggest) | **Ratified by CJ 2026-08-14 (DH-7), #1515 / PR #1547** |
| J7 | Agent permission / interruption | **Strawman below — under review** |
| J8 | First launch / onboarding | **Strawman below — under review** |
| J9 | Open another project (routing across instances) | **Ratified by CJ 2026-08-09 (DH-7), #759** |
| J10 | Correct your own comment (revise or withdraw) | **Ratified by CJ 2026-08-09 (DH-7), #1125** (D24, FR-CMT-8..13); **child-first amendment ruled by CJ 2026-09-30, superseding the 2026-08-18/20 cascade-hide amendment** |

| J15 | Recover from a render failure (the shell cannot render its own error surface) | **Behavioural choice ruled by CJ 2026-09-04; native close confirmed 2026-09-07. Prose alignment proposed — parked at the DH-7 gate, #2130 / #2432** (FR-X-5, D12, D15; appearance `docs/design/` turn 12) |

---

### J1 — Revision review (the heart loop)

**Trigger:** In chat: *"Revise the opening two sections based on our discussion — tighten the hook."* Agent works; a revision lands as tracked changes (FR-REV-1).

**Phase A — agent working (you keep writing):**

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
│  the-life-you-lay-down.md            [In rev.] │  TIMELINE All Chat Cmt Chg Fnd│
│                                                │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │
│  # The Life You Lay Down Every Day             │ │ You: Revise the opening…  │ │
│                                                │ └───────────────────────────┘ │
│  Paul did not say "I die daily" as a...        │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │
│  (cursor here — editing freely)                │ │ ◔ Agent working…    0:42  │ │
│                                                │ │   ✓ read claude.md        │ │
│                                                │ │   ✓ read grammar/SKILL.md │ │
│                                                │ │   ◔ drafting revision…    │ │
│                                                │ │              [ Stop ]     │ │
│                                                │ └───────────────────────────┘ │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘
```

**Phase B — delivery.** The editor does NOT jump or redline mid-keystroke (Principle 2). A **change-set card** arrives in the timeline; the doc shows redline only after the set exists in full (FR-AGENT-6 atomicity):

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
│  {--For most of us,--}{++We++} rarely think    │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │
│  of surrender as {~~daily~>a daily act~~}...   │ │ ⬒ REVISION · Agent · now  │ │
│   ▲ green underline = insert                   │ │ "Tightened hook; fixed 2  │ │
│   ▲ red strike = delete (Word-style render,    │ │  citations."              │ │
│     not raw CriticMarkup braces)               │ │  14 changes · 2 sections  │ │
│                                                │ │  ▸ §1 Opening (9)         │ │
│  Toolbar: [✓ Accept][✗ Reject] ◀ 3/14 ▶       │ │  ▸ §2 Exposition (5)      │ │
│  Display: All ▾   Track: ON                    │ │ [Review 1-by-1][Accept all]│ │
│                                                │ │ [Reject all]              │ │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘
```

**Phase B edge — delivery onto unsaved edits (UXR-Q3).** If the target tab holds unsaved user edits when the delivery lands, the buffer is **not** touched and neither editor baseline moves: the FR-ED-7 external-change banner + Compare fires ("Keep mine" the highlighted default) — the recorded M11 answer for disk-diverged-from-dirty-buffer. An agent-attributed write is a tracked change only for a **clean** tab; to a dirty tab it is an outside edit until the user chooses. The change-set card still arrives in the timeline either way.

**Phase C — triage.** Entry paths from the card:
- **Review 1-by-1:** enters focused pass — selects first pending change, editor scrolls, popover shows old→new + agent's note; `Accept & next` / `Reject & next` (keyboard: **Cmd/Ctrl+Enter accept, Cmd/Ctrl+Backspace reject** — platform-aware per FR-X-1); progress "3 of 14". Exit anytime; partial triage is fine.
- **Accept all / Reject all** on the card (or per-section via the ▸ groups).
- **Type-filtered bulk ops** (CJ, J1-Q1): "Accept all insertions" / "Accept all deletions" (and reject equivalents) — available from the card's overflow menu and the toolbar's track-changes dropdown, scoped to the change set or the whole doc.
- Clicking any redline directly in the text opens the same popover — no mode required.
- **(P2)** "Changes as a list" — a readable diff-style list view of all pending changes for linear reading and triage outside the document flow.
- **Triage closes threads, and a closed thread can be reopened by hand.** Accepting a change's group resolves its linked thread and rejecting reopens it (FR-CMT-3/7); since D28 those are two causes of **one** *Reopened* condition (FR-CMT-20), and the auto-resolved card carries `Reopen` like any other closed thread (J11 Phase D, FR-CMT-19). The card a **rejection** reopens reads as it always did — per-change outcomes, no closure line, because none was recorded on the way (FR-CMT-20 as scoped at #1792); only a card reopened out of a **recorded** closure keeps that closure legible. Nothing else about the sweep changes: this card decides changes, and deciding changes is the only thing on it that closes anything — no sweep dismisses (J4).

**Phase C — decision reach (UXR-Q5, ratified by CJ 2026-07-22).** A card-scoped decision can reach past the card. Group closure is all-or-none and unbounded by the change set (P4b, §2), so "Accept all insertions" on set A also decides a grouped co-member delivered in set B. The reach is correct; what it must not be is *invisible* (Principle 1 — provenance always visible; Principle 7 / FR-X-5 — degrade honestly, never silently). Two ends of the same fact, two different affordances (§2's inventory):

```
The acting card, after the sweep:          A card whose changes were decided elsewhere:
┌───────────────────────────────┐          ┌───────────────────────────────┐
│ ⬒ REVISION · Agent · 4m ago   │          │ ⬒ REVISION · Agent · 12m ago  │
│ "Tightened hook…"             │          │ "Second pass on §2."          │
│  3 changes · 1 section        │          │  1 change · 1 section         │
│  ▸ §1 Opening (3)             │          │  ▸ §2 Exposition (1)          │
│  ↳ also decided 2 linked      │          │  ↳ 2 changes decided elsewhere│
│    changes outside this set   │          │                               │
│ [Review 1-by-1][Accept all]   │          │ [Review 1-by-1][Accept all]   │
└───────────────────────────────┘          └───────────────────────────────┘
```

- **The reach note is an outcome, not a warning.** It appears after the action, states the count, and offers no control — J7's honest-accounting block (`Completed: … / Not started: …`) is its precedent, and `↳` is J4's existing linked-change vocabulary. Nothing is confirmed beforehand; §2's rule 1 gives the reasoning.
- **Undo withdraws it.** Ctrl+Z reverses a decision and its swept-in co-members together — one transaction, one history event, markup and attribution restored as a unit (tech-design §3.3, M9). The note names that decision, so it must go when the decision does, and the changes return to pending. #680 supplies that reversal path and satisfies §2's sequencing condition; an accounting line that outlives its fact remains the same defect as a count the card cannot verify.
- **It does not link anywhere.** A decided change has no redline left to deep-link to (Principle 6's exception, not its violation), and the timeline has no card→card linkage to navigate (`TimelinePanel`'s own recorded limitation). An accounting line that promised navigation it cannot deliver would be the silent no-op FR-X-5 forbids. This is contingent, not permanent — it reopens for the whole inventory when change identity is stable (TD-4, #493).
- **It lives in the card body, not the collapsed line.** A fully-decided set still collapses to `N changes decided` counting only its **own** changes (P2b-1b, §4 "Timeline at scale"); expanding reveals the reach. Reach is provenance, not a headline.
- **A gated card says nothing.** A card whose document/parse generation does not match the live field neither absorbs a decision report nor renders either note — §2's rule 2. It keeps showing the UXR-Q4 mismatch affordance, which already names the action that resolves it. This closes the asymmetry where a stale card could be silently decided *for* while being unable to decide itself: it is now neither.

**Edge states:** you edit inside a pending change (allowed — your edit wins; change remains attributed); revision arrives while you're mid-sentence (card appears and the toolbar's pending-change counter updates — nothing else moves: no scroll, no focus steal, no animation on the text); Stop pressed mid-run (FR-AGENT-6: card reports "no changes applied" or complete partial set).

**Open UX questions (J1):** see interview queue.

---

### J2 — Find/open a document or brainstorm → draft materializes

**Trigger:** Project open. You either choose an existing document from the project rail, or—with no relevant document open—start talking. (FR-CHAT-1: chat is repo-level; a new doc can be *born from* the conversation.)

**Entry path — find and open an existing document (#698).** In workshop mode, activate a document in **Documents**, or a skill-owned document/workflow in **Skills**; the ownership rule guarantees the same project-relative path never appears in both sections. Emend opens it in a new tab, or focuses its existing tab, and marks the one owning row active only after the open succeeds. The repo-level conversation then slides from center stage to the right timeline, preserving its history (D4, Principle 5). In author mode, the same action switches/focuses tabs without changing the timeline. A failed open leaves the current workshop or document exactly where it was and reports through the shared status region.

Opening a *project* is not this journey (J9, #759): the rail and strip navigate inside the project this instance holds, and neither ever replaces it.

The rail and project-home strip have different jobs. The rail is the primary navigator for user-visible project paths. The strip is a workshop-only summary of recent documents and active reviews: choosing a recent document performs the same open/focus action, but the strip never mirrors the tree or replaces it. Collapsing the rail leaves the strip in place; opening a document replaces the workshop strip with the standard tabbed authoring center.

**Rail lifecycle (#698).** A path appears only after its create/rename succeeds or a completed automatic/user-requested refresh observes it; the rail never predicts success. During updating, the last-known rows and active-row marker remain. A successful result applies externally observed path changes together: an addition appears under its current ancestors; an uncorrelated disappearance removes its closed old row; and an unreadable path takes the warning state above. A failed result changes none of those path facts and exposes retry.

When Emend can confidently correlate old and new in-project paths as the same document, the completed update presents an external rename/move—not a deletion (FR-ED-8). The row moves to the new path; document identity and history, open tab/buffer, and active-row marker all follow it. The tab never enters **Missing on disk**, and externally moved ancestors keep their existing expansion state so the update steals no focus. The Architect owns how correlation is established.

A correlated move can land on a path another *open* document occupies (an external `mv` over an existing file). The moved document then becomes the **sole document at that path** (CJ, 2026-08-11): its identity, buffer, and active marker arrive there exactly as above, and the destination's previous document is no longer at any project path. If that displaced document has nothing unsaved, its tab closes. If it has unsaved work, that work stays open as an **untitled recovery document** — its own tab, named for the path it was displaced from, holding the buffer. It is never written to the destination (which would overwrite the moved document) and never silently discarded: it persists with the session across quit and relaunch, and only an explicit, **confirmed** close by the author discards it. Because that close is the one action that destroys the only copy of the buffer, it is guarded — the author is offered `[Save As…]`, `[Discard]`, and `[Cancel]`; cancellation or a failed Save As keeps the guard and buffer intact, while only explicit Discard destroys the work. While it is the active tab no rail row is marked active, because it is at no project path.

**Legacy unsupported-session recovery (D27 / FR-ED-3/9; #2515, CJ 2026-10-01).** At restore, a legacy tab can name a file type Emend now refuses while still carrying unsaved text in its session snapshot. Emend refuses to open or write that former path, then **automatically converts the exact retained buffer** into an editable untitled recovery document. Its tab label uses the existing recovered-from pattern and names the former project path; it does not claim that path as a backing file, and no project-rail row becomes active for the untitled tab. The conversion replaces the legacy tab in its restored tab order and active selection; it does not create a second tab for the same buffer. The author sees why the original path could not open as an Emend document and that the retained text is in the untitled tab, with Save As as the route to a supported project-document path. This is a second entry route into the same J12 pathless surface, not an extension of D27's supported file types. Its existing no-autosave rule, session persistence, guarded close, and Save As adoption apply. A cancelled or failed Save As keeps the buffer and tab unchanged; successful adoption writes the chosen supported path, never the unsupported former one.

**No false recovery.** A clean legacy unsupported tab has no retained buffer to convert: it closes after the visible refusal. An explicitly present empty unsaved buffer is different from no buffer and is preserved as empty text in an untitled tab. An over-cap omission marker without retained text cannot recreate the missing bytes and does not enter this retained-buffer conversion; its tab and recorded loss marker remain after the visible refusal (CJ, 2026-10-01, #2555). If an earlier omission fact coexists with retained current text, only that current text converts and the loss fact remains, so Save As never claims to restore earlier omitted work. The original legacy snapshot remains the durable copy until the converted untitled snapshot is safely persisted. If conversion fails, the author sees the failure and the retained buffer stays in the session; Emend never mounts an unsupported editor to work around it. Generated `docs/design/` `9h` draws this origin and its visible explanation; see the source brief `docs/d27-legacy-unsupported-recovery-visual-refresh-brief.md`.

**Untitled/scratch document lifecycle (D12, FR-ED-8, #1379) — interim, partial; ratified by CJ 2026-08-21 at reviewed head `7114b02a39bdc23417a7aba1be26c93fc9b74897`:** the recovery document is editable — the author's copy of what CJ's 2026-08-11 ruling preserves, on screen and changeable, with **autosave off by construction**: there is no project path yet for autosave to write to, so no autosave clause applies here and the tab is never marked dirty. Its whole buffer, not a divergence from a file, persists durably with the session through the existing debounced path and its blur, tab-set, and close-request flush points (see the `toSessionPayload` `@anchor:tabs-to-session-payload` clause above). **Save As is the ratified product decision for adoption; FR-ED-8 does not itself prescribe Save As.** `[Save As…]` opens the existing confined native save route, writes the buffer with its line endings preserved verbatim, and turns the untitled tab into an ordinary project document at the chosen path. Cancelling or failing Save As changes nothing: the buffer, untitled tab, and any open destructive-close guard stay intact. A successful Save As from that guard adopts and keeps the document open; `[Discard]` is the only choice that destroys the buffer. **At this interim point D12 and FR-ED-8 were incomplete** pending #1803. That follow-up has since closed, and untitled comment creation and adoption are exercised in `e2e/acceptance.spec.mjs` (2026-10-01 comment ruling above). #1804 remains the separate rich-text/Markdown parity follow-up; #1379 and #1803 have since closed.

**#1804 is captured as J12**, which owns the untitled pane's editing surface — manual save, display modes, unrecorded tracked changes, the freeze, and comments admitted on the untitled buffer under the 2026-10-01 ruling. J2 keeps the document's lifecycle; J12 keeps its surface, and extends this paragraph's adoption clause at one named point (J12 Phase F). The save-as latch and the finalization freeze this paragraph's quit sequence relies on are recorded at § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*.

Recovery content is held inline in the session record, subject to a per-tab limit (2 MiB). The limit governs what the session can keep, not what the author has: a buffer above it is still present and editable in the window that holds it, and Emend says so — that the work is here, that the session cannot keep it, and that saving it into the project is the only way to keep it. Only after a relaunch the session could not carry is the work gone, and only then does Emend say it could not be preserved, that it exceeded the per-tab limit, that it cannot be restored, and that the document is not in the project yet. These two are never told as one thing; where both are true of one document, the still-actionable risk is stated first and the earlier loss separately, and neither is written so that saving reads as recovering the other.

The document is editable and adoptable exactly as any other untitled document is, and nothing in either state presents editing or saving as recovery of work that was not kept, or offers a retry, a compare, or any other affordance implying it can be reached (Principle 7).

A restored omission is a standing fact about the tab for as long as the tab is untitled. Editing does not clear it, and while the document is untitled there is no save that could. The author may hide the explanation; hiding collapses it to a persistent statement, never presents the state as resolved, and returns as hidden after a relaunch. The live warning is not hidden at all: it is a risk the author can still act on.

Two facts are persisted with the session and survive quit and relaunch: that a tab's recovery content was omitted for exceeding the per-tab limit, and whether the author has hidden the explanation. Neither can be reconstructed from the recovery path and the absence of content, so the outcome is recorded explicitly.

When the same path is displaced more than once, each colliding untitled document receives a stable creation-order label ordinal. It is carried in the session, appears identically in the tab, accessible name, and any affected-documents list, and is never renumbered when a sibling closes; the opaque recovery identity is never parsed to reconstruct it (`9d.3`).

Closing an untitled document always asks. What it asks depends on what closing would actually destroy: work that is only here, work the session cannot keep, work written since an earlier loss, or nothing at all — and where it would destroy nothing, it says so rather than warning about work that no longer exists, and discarding is not presented as destructive. Every one of these offers saving, discarding and cancelling, in that order, with cancelling the default and dismissal cancelling.

Quitting does not ask about an untitled document only when Emend has been told, authoritatively, that every remaining untitled document was kept in full. A write reporting success while having dropped a buffer is not that assurance; where any buffer was not kept, Emend asks about it rather than closing. A run still in progress refuses the close before any of this begins — nothing is decided, staged, or written — and the author closes again once it has stopped.

Quitting resolves the window's dirty project documents first, one at a time. Those decisions take effect only when the session is written: a document saved during the queue is saved, but its tab stays open until the window does; a document the author chose to discard is recorded and nothing is thrown away yet. Emend writes the session it would have after all of those closes, in a single write it waits for, and only then are the closes carried out. Where the write fails, none of them are, and the plan is kept so the author can try again or quit anyway. Where the session Emend would write is not meaningfully different from the one it last saved, it does not write again, and it never says anything is at risk.

Where the write fails, Emend stays open with every tab intact, gives the actual reason, says no new session was committed, and says what would come back if the author quit anyway — the previous saved session, none at all, or something it could not verify. Quitting anyway abandons the session changes and closes — unless the window refuses to close, and then Emend says that it did not close and that the session changes are still unsaved, which is the whole of what changed: nothing was written and nothing was carried out (CJ, 2026-09-05, UXR-Q20.2). Where it does close, it does not pretend to have carried out the closes durably, and it discloses that the older session, including tabs the author closed or earlier versions of open documents, may reopen. Remaining open is the default. Where more than one untitled document is at risk, Emend says how many and names them all.

Where the write lands and the window then refuses to close, none of the closes have been carried out either — they are carried out by the window going away and by nothing else — so the session just committed describes tabs that are still on screen, including any document the author chose to discard. Emend puts that session back, writing the one that describes the window the author is still looking at, and says the window did not close and that nothing was closed or discarded. This is the one place a second write follows the single write above, and it is confined to this branch: where the session was not meaningfully different and Emend therefore proved it rather than writing it, nothing was committed to put back and it still does not write again. The author's staged decisions are dropped, which is narrower than it sounds: a document saved during the queue stays saved, and what quitting again asks about afresh is whatever is still dirty or still discardable.

**What CJ ruled on this branch (2026-09-05, UXR-Q20).** **A refused close never carries the queue's closes out in the UI.** What is recorded above is the answer, not merely the implementation: the window keeps everything it had. The authorisation to discard an untitled buffer was given as part of quitting, and where the quit does not happen its premise is gone — it is also the one irreversible act in the sequence, which J2 elsewhere guards behind an explicit, confirmed close. **Focus returns to where the author was**, the destination cancelling already uses; the reasoning and the case it fills are at § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*. **The disclosure carries the failure's own text**, as the write-failure branch does, under the constraints §2 records for every such sentence. And **`[Quit anyway]` is described correctly above**, its tail amended rather than its substance. Nothing here is settled by silence: UXR-Q20 carries all four answers, and the sibling that was never in question — whether any of this is reachable in a healthy build — is not, since the grant #2328 added is what makes a refused close the defensive path it now is.

When an untitled document is adopted into the project, the fact that earlier work was not part of what was saved is recorded durably before the adoption is made, and shown afterwards in the conversation record. Where it is saved but that record has not yet been written, Emend says so plainly, and completes the record later — once, however many times it tries.

These guarantees are for one window's close request. Quitting the application asks each participating window, any of which may refuse. Nothing is guaranteed where the application is terminated rather than asked to quit.

When Emend cannot establish that identity, it never labels the transition a rename: the old path becomes **Missing on disk** and the new path is an unrelated addition. While the active document's path is absent from a completed result, no row is marked active. Once that path appears, its ancestors expand and its row becomes active when the path change came from a user-requested open, document birth, scratch adoption, or inline rename/move. This reveal changes no keyboard focus and causes no center/timeline scroll (Principle 2). Background additions do not expand ancestors or change the active document.

For J2 Phase B, the new draft path appears only after creation succeeds; because the created document then opens, its ancestor chain expands and its row becomes active. Saving a scratch document into the project follows the same rule after Save As succeeds. A successful inline rename/path move updates the existing row in place, preserves its active-row marker when that document is active, and expands a newly required ancestor chain; until success, the old path and marker remain. Any failed create, adoption, or rename leaves the rail and active document unchanged and reports through the shared status region (Principle 7).

**Phase A — workshop mode.** With no document open, chat isn't a sidebar — it's the main event. Center stage is the conversation; the left rail still shows Documents and discovery-shaped Skills; a slim project-home strip sits above (recent docs, active reviews). Appearance is `docs/design/` options `1b`, `6a`, `6b`, and `6c`:

```
┌──────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ biblical_wri…│  biblical_writing        Recent: life-you-lay-down.md · …   │
│ DOCUMENTS    │ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
│  ▸ drafts    │ │ You: Thinking about a sermonette on Philippians 2 —    │ │
│  readme.md   │ │      the kenosis passage, but for daily life.          │ │
│ SKILLS       │ │                                                         │ │
│  ▸ Grammar…  │ │ Agent: Strong angle. You touched adjacent ground in    │ │
│  ▸ Sermon…   │ │  "The Life You Lay Down" (2026-03) — surrender as      │ │
│ WORKFLOWS    │ │  daily act, not crisis moment. Philippians 2 lets you  │ │
│  weekly-br…  │ │  root that christologically…      [refs: 2 files read] │ │
│              │ │                                                         │ │
│              │ │ You: Draft it as a sermonette.                          │ │
│              │ └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
│              │  [ mic ] [ type a message…                        ] [Send] │
└──────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Phase B — the birth moment.** Agent writes the draft file (direct write — it's not open yet, D3), and—after that write succeeds—a **document-birth card** appears; the doc opens as a tab, the rail reveals its new active row, chat slides to the right panel, and we're in the standard authoring layout (J1's chrome):

```
┌───────────────────────────┐
│ ⬒ DRAFT CREATED · Agent   │
│ my_writing/drafts/        │
│   kenosis-daily.md        │
│ Sermonette · 1,480 words  │
│ via skill: sermon-writer  │
│ [Opened in editor]        │
└───────────────────────────┘
```

The conversation *continues* — same thread, now visibly associated with the doc (its future revisions, comments, findings all join this one timeline; Principle 5).

**Notes:** draft location is the agent's choice following repo convention (visible on the card — no save dialog interrupts the flow); "refs: N files read" on agent replies keeps grounding observable (Principle 3) without cluttering the prose.

**Edge states:** you keep brainstorming and ask for a second variant → second birth card, second tab; you say "draft it" with a doc already open → new file still (a revision of the open doc requires "revise", J1 — never overwrites); agent asks a clarifying question instead of drafting (normal chat, no special UI).

---

### J3 — Skill/workflow review → findings triage

**Trigger:** In chat: *"Run the sermon review workflow on this."* (D7: agent interprets `workflows/sermon-review-workflow.md`; app enforces nothing.)

**Phase A — stage running.** A **workflow-run card** tracks live progress; findings tick in as they're found:

```
┌───────────────────────────────┐        Editor margin, meanwhile:
│ ⬒ WORKFLOW · sermon-review    │        ┃ red mark    = Critical/High
│ Stage 1/3 · biblical-accuracy │        ┃ amber mark  = Medium
│ ◔ checking citations…   1:12  │        ┃ gray mark   = Low
│ Findings so far: 3            │        (severity-colored comment
│  ● 1 High  ● 2 Medium         │         highlights on anchored text)
│                      [ Stop ] │
└───────────────────────────────┘
```

**Phase B — stage report card + gate.** At stage end, the card becomes a report; the gate (workflow's "fix Critical before proceeding") is conversational per D7, but the card carries the obvious buttons:

```
┌────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ■ STAGE 1 · biblical-accuracy DONE │
│ Verdict: Needs Minor Revision      │
│ ● 0 Critical ● 1 High ● 2 Med ● 1 Low │
│ ▸ H: Eph 4:22 quote conflates NKJV/ │
│      ESV (§2)                       │
│ ▸ M: "kenosis" definition — root    │
│      fallacy risk (§1) …            │
│ [Address findings] [Continue → 2/3] │
└────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Phase C — findings triage.** Each finding is an ordinary anchored comment (D6) — severity chip + skill name + recommendation, clickable to its passage. The timeline's filter bar gains a **Findings** facet (by run, by severity). Addressing a finding is exactly J4 ("Address with AI") or a manual edit — nothing special to learn. When Stage 2 runs, its findings join the same stream tagged by stage.

**Triage has three exits, not two.** Fix it (J4 or by hand), or close it as **Resolved** or as **Dismissed** — *considered, and not accepted* (J11, D25). The noisy finding is the case this journey produces most, and before D25 its only exit said the opposite of what happened. The stage report card and the Findings facet therefore account for all three — `4 addressed · 2 dismissed · 1 open` — and a dismissed finding is **never** counted among the addressed or fixed (FR-REVIEW-3, FR-CMT-15). The `1 Critical open` chip on subsequent stage cards counts open findings only: a dismissed Critical is closed and drops off it, which is precisely what dismissing it was for, while an accurate *dismissed* account keeps that from reading as a fix.

**Edge states:** zero findings ("Sound" report card, one-click continue); finding anchored to text you already changed mid-run (normal D17 drift handling — re-anchor or orphan); you continue past the gate with Criticals open (allowed — D7 says you drive; the card notes "1 Critical open" as a chip on subsequent stage cards).

---

### J4 — Address a comment with AI (single, then batch)

**Single thread.** Every open comment card (any provenance — yours, finding, reviewer) carries **[Address with AI]** (also in the editor's comment popover). The thread then passes through **Working** (§2) before any proposal exists: the run-starting affordances are replaced by the binding `5a.5`/`5b.5` status treatment, the card claims nothing, and the run's clock, activity feed, and controls stay on the run card:

```
┌───────────────────────────────┐     while the run works (§2 Working):
│ ● HIGH · biblical-accuracy    │     ┌───────────────────────────────┐
│ "Eph 4:22 quote conflates     │     │ ● HIGH · biblical-accuracy    │
│  NKJV/ESV" → §2 ¶3            │     │ "Eph 4:22 quote conflates     │
│  Rec: quote ESV throughout    │     │  NKJV/ESV" → §2 ¶3            │
│ [Address with AI] [Reply] [✓] │     │ ◔ Addressing with AI —       │
└───────────────────────────────┘     │   edit/delete after the run  │
                                      │ [Reply] [✓]                   │
                                      └───────────────────────────────┘

after delivery:
┌───────────────────────────────┐
│ ◐ ADDRESSED — pending review  │
│  Agent: Replaced quote with   │
│  verbatim ESV; added citation.│
│  ↳ 2 linked changes  [Go to →]│
│ [Accept both] (or triage in   │
│  the text — J1 popover)       │
└───────────────────────────────┘
```

- Accepting the linked change(s) resolves the thread (FR-CMT-3/7); rejecting reopens it with the outcome recorded on the thread — one of the two causes of §2's *Reopened*, the other being a hand reopen (FR-CMT-20). **This card reads what D8 always gave it** — the per-change outcomes, and **no closure line**: nothing was closed on the way here, so FR-CMT-20's closure-history rule has nothing to bind (scoped at #1792). **While those changes are undecided the thread cannot be closed by hand at all** — neither `Mark as resolved` nor `Dismiss` (FR-CMT-21(a)) — because a hand-pressed resolve would assert what FR-CMT-7 denies and a dismissal would leave agent work undecided under a closed thread (D21).
- **Author side of a reviewer suggestion (FR-COLLAB-2b):** the thread's mini-diff carries `[Apply]` → creates a tracked change *attributed to the reviewer*, triaged with the standard J1 popover. Apply-with-conflict (underlying text changed/rejected meanwhile) → D17 re-anchor/orphan handling, thread notes it.
- **Orphaned threads (D17):** the orphan card carries `[Re-anchor]` — click, select the new home text in the doc, confirm; original quote stays on the thread either way. Author-only (reviewer-side orphans wait for the author, consistent with "author owns resolution").
- **While a run is in flight** the thread is in §2's *Working* state: it names no proposal, replaces its run-starting affordances with the active-run status treatment, and keeps `[Reply]` and both closure verbs — `Mark as resolved` and `Dismiss` — live (Principle 3, FR-CMT-21(b)) unless a linked change on the thread is still undecided, which makes both inert (FR-CMT-21(a)); `✎` and `Delete` are inert, and have been since the run first targeted this thread (FR-CMT-10, §2's ladder). Per J10/UXR-Q9, `[Stop]` remains on the timeline run card and is never duplicated here. A thread merely *targeted* by a live or queued run says so instead (§2's acknowledgment ladder, which replaces every thread-local run-starting affordance on the card and comment popover, and from that same moment renders `✎`/`Delete` inert while leaving both closure verbs live under the same undecided-change proviso) rather than offering re-delegation there; only the batch sheet's deliberate `[include them too]` opt-in can queue the thread again. Every way the run can end — delivery, defence, stop, failure, lost anchor — has a landing state in §2's exit table.
- **Re-delegate with guidance — on a thread reopened over a *rejection*.** That thread offers `[Try again — add guidance…]` (inline input; the agent sees the rejection + your note) **whenever no run already targets it** — it is a run-starting affordance and obeys the ladder like any other. The thread enters *Working* while that run is in flight; changes an earlier delivery left undecided stay listed **under that delivery's name** and stay decidable throughout — the new run's silence is about the new run only. **A thread reopened by hand is not always that thread** (FR-CMT-19/20): where the closure it inverts was a manual resolution or a dismissal with no delivery behind it, there is no rejection and no earlier work to re-delegate, so the card offers ordinary `[Address with AI]` rather than `[Try again…]` — v1 records no reason on the reopen act itself, so guidance has nothing to attach to. Where the reopened thread *does* carry a dismissal, that dismissal and its note go to the agent on the next run (FR-CMT-18).
- **Push-back path:** agent may reply-defend without editing (FR-CMT-4) — thread stays open, labeled *"defended — no change proposed"*; you resolve or overrule. On an external thread the defense syncs to the reviewer (D20).
- **Dismissing instead of addressing (J11):** an open thread may be closed as *not accepted* rather than handed to the agent — the ordinary exit for a finding you judge wrong, and usually what the defended thread above wants once you agree with the defence. It stays available **while a run is in flight** (D29, FR-CMT-21(b)): the thread states the closure, and a delivery clears it if one arrives (§2's exit table). It is refused only while a **linked tracked change is undecided** — and there `Mark as resolved` is refused with it: accept the changes and the thread resolves itself, or reject them, which reopens the thread, and dismiss it then (FR-CMT-21(a)).

**Batch.** From the editor toolbar when >1 open comment (from its `⋯` overflow at 900px): **[Address all… ▾]** with the filter set (all / severity ≥ / source / this review pass). The Timeline-header variant belongs only to a standalone Timeline component, not the main app. The default set is J4-Q1's — all open comments — minus any thread a live or queued run already targets, stated on the sheet rather than silently applied: `Address all · 6 open comments · 2 already in an agent run — [include them too]`. The count and the exclusion restate against whichever filter is chosen. The reason is correctness, not tidiness: UXR-Q1 executes a queued run only **after** the live one finishes, so a thread left in both would be addressed by the second run on a premise the first has already invalidated — its text moved, its thread changed. The opt-in keeps deliberate double-addressing available, and a thread that takes it shows the combined rung in §2's ladder.

With no held threads in the selected scope, the sheet omits the `0 already in an agent run` clause and the `include them too` checkbox. Opening the chooser focuses its first filter. A selected filter survives closing, submission, and reopening on the same document, but resets on a document switch; the held-thread opt-in resets after submission and when the held set changes. Escape and an outside click close the chooser in either host; Escape returns focus to its visible invoker (`Address all…` or `⋯` at 900px). An admitted Address all run focuses its owning run card after submission, whether Timeline was already expanded or expands under UXR-Q16.4.

During the run, a targeted thread enters *Working* only once the run has started work on it; threads not yet reached say `In an agent run` and are otherwise untouched. If the provider reports only run-level progress, no thread shows *Working* at all and the run card is the only progress surface (Principle 7). Cancellation remains run-scoped on that batch run card. Delivery is **one change-set card (J1 vocabulary), grouped by comment** rather than by section:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────┐
│ ⬒ ADDRESSED 6 COMMENTS · Agent  │
│ 9 changes · 5 replies · 1 defense│
│ ▸ ● H Eph 4:22 quote (2 chg)    │
│ ▸ ● M kenosis definition (1)    │
│ ▸ ● M §3 transition (3)  …      │
│ ▸ ⚑ defended: "daily" wording   │
│ [Review 1-by-1] [Accept all]    │
└─────────────────────────────────┘
```

Triage is J1's flow; deciding a comment's group resolves/reopens that thread individually. Defenses are listed distinctly (⚑) so they aren't lost in the accept sweep — and a defense you end up agreeing with is dismissal's clearest case (J11), so this is the one surface where they are met in bulk. Each ⚑ row therefore carries the ordinary thread action set and reaches `Dismiss` exactly where every other thread does. **No sweep ever dismisses.** `Accept all` and `Reject all` decide changes; nothing on this card closes a thread as *not accepted*, because the judgment is the entire content of the outcome and it is made thread by thread — FR-CMT-5 batches *addressing*, and no requirement offers a batch form of closure.

**Edge states:** two comments target overlapping text → agent produces one shared change linked to both threads (both resolve on accept); batch interrupted mid-run → completed comment-groups stand, threads never started are untouched and carry no note, a thread that **was** in flight returns to its prior state carrying `Address run stopped — nothing proposed for this comment.`, and the card says which (FR-AGENT-6, J7); where per-thread progress was never observable no thread carries a note and the run card reports delivered vs. not delivered; a comment the agent can't act on ("out of scope for the text") → defense-style reply, thread stays open.

---

### J5 — Start a review (author side)

**Trigger:** `Share → Start review` (toolbar) with the current doc pre-selected, or via chat. One sheet, no git vocabulary:

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Start review                         │
│  Documents:  ☑ kenosis-daily.md       │
│              [ + add documents… ]     │
│  Name:       [ Kenosis sermonette   ] │
│  Reviewers:  [ @pastor-jim  @mary_k ] │
│              (GitHub usernames)       │
│  Note to reviewers (optional):        │
│  [ Focus on §3 application…        ]  │
│              [Cancel] [Start review]  │
└───────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Preparing** (D18 machinery: branch, worktree, PR, invites — all invisible): progress card in timeline → resolves to a **review bar** — workspace-level chrome: full width, above the tab strip — and a live card in the timeline:

```
Review bar:       ⇄ In review "Kenosis sermonette" · 👤👤 · 3 open comments · synced ✓
Timeline card:    ⬒ REVIEW LIVE · started 2m ago · pastor-jim invited, mary_k viewing
                  [View comments] [Complete…] [Cancel review] [GitHub ↗]
```

The sync dot is the only machinery you ever see: `✓ synced` / `⇡ syncing` / `⚠ offline — will sync when connected` (D20 standing consent; FR-X-5 honesty). Clicking the dot forces a manual refresh (FR-COLLAB-9).

**The review bar holds a fixed height for the review's duration** (Amendment — #1804/#2111 document-notice slot, ratified by CJ 2026-08-28, DH-7). No state of it may grow it to a second line — not the offline sync-dot warning, not the multi-review switcher at more than one review, not a long review name. Content that does not fit is truncated, moved behind the switcher, or stated in the shared status region; the band's height is never what gives way. A workspace-level band that changes height reflows the entire document column beneath it, including the tab strip and the toolbar — which is the same reflow-under-the-caret harm the document-notice slot was ruled to prevent, one band higher and worse, because it moves *everything*.

**Pre-flight, handled inline in the sheet:** project has no git → "This folder isn't versioned yet — set that up?" one-click init + GitHub connect (D12); no remote/repo → create-private-repo offer; reviewer lacks repo access → "will send a GitHub invite" chip next to their name; unsaved changes → auto-saved on start.

**Completing:** `[Complete…]` runs the D18 gate — pending tracked changes **block**, listed per document ("kenosis-daily.md: 4 · ch2.md: 1 — [Review them now]" walks the whole set doc by doc in the J1 focused pass); unresolved comments **warn** ("2 comments still open"), don't block — a **dismissed** thread is closed and neither warns nor counts, here or in the review bar's open-comment count (FR-CMT-15, J11). Success = a timeline card ("Review completed · merged") and the review bar disappears — no other fanfare in the author window. Any open or later-resumed reviewer workspace transitions to J6's `Review ended` state with the completed outcome; it is never silently bounced to the waiting list.

**Cancelling:** `[Cancel review]` confirms in human terms: *"End this review without merging? Reviewers keep read access until you remove them. Keep the review's comment history for later?"* — Keep (default) / Discard. (That choice is D18's keep/discard branch, never named as such — Principle 4.) Cancellation sends the cancelled outcome and that history disposition into J6's `Review ended` transition: **Keep** leaves synced history readable; **Discard** replaces it with the explicit discarded-history account. Neither choice discards a reviewer event that never synced, and neither leaves a reviewer workspace claiming the review is live. Note: the review card's `[GitHub ↗]` link is a deliberate, documented Principle-4 waiver — it serves the D9 bonus lane, and it's the only git-adjacent control in author mode.

**Edge states:** reviewer never accepts invite (card shows "invited 3d ago — resend?"); starting a second review while one is live (fine — D18 worktrees; review bar shows a switcher when >1); starting a review with pending redline in the doc (allowed and default — reviewers see raw in-flight state, per J5-Q1).

---

### J6 — Reviewer's first session (make-or-break for pillar 3)

**Path to the door:** GitHub emails the invite (repo access + review request). The PR body — written by Emend — says plainly: *"This document is reviewed with Emend → download link. Prefer GitHub? You can comment on this PR directly."* (FR-COLLAB-5 stays the escape hatch.)

**First run, reviewer flavor (FR-COLLAB-2):**

```
1. Launch → one button: [Sign in with GitHub]  → device-code screen (code + open-browser)
2. Home:  REVIEWS WAITING FOR YOU
          ▸ "Kenosis sermonette" — from CJ Williams · 1 document · invited today
3. Click → "Opening review…" (auto-clone behind the scenes; no folder picker, no clone word)
```

**Reviewer workspace — deliberately less app:** no file tree, no agent chat, no accept/reject anywhere. Center doc + right timeline (this review only):

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┐
│ Kenosis sermonette ▾ (Documents: 1)  · from CJ │  REVIEW                       │
│                        · updated 2m ago        │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │
│ We rarely think of surrender as a daily act…   │ │ CJ (note): Focus on §3    │ │
│  {~~daily~>a daily act~~}  ← redline visible,  │ │ application.              │ │
│   attributed (CJ / Agent), but NOT decidable   │ └───────────────────────────┘ │
│   by the reviewer                              │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │
│                                                │ │ mary_k: Is "kenosis" too  │ │
│  (select text) → [💬 Comment] [✎ Suggest]     │ │ academic for this venue?  │ │
│                                                │ │  ↳ [Reply]                │ │
│                                                │ └───────────────────────────┘ │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘
```

Multi-doc review sets (FR-COLLAB-7): the title is a **doc-set dropdown** (`Kenosis sermonette ▾ · Documents: 3`) — the reviewer switches docs there; unread-comment badges per doc. **J2's project rail and project-home strip never appear in reviewer mode:** the dropdown exposes only the review set, never the surrounding project. No file tree ever.

**Mode boundary — absence is structural, not visual.** Entering a review chooses reviewer mode before the workspace mounts. The project rail, its collapsed restore control, the project-home strip, agent surfaces, accept/reject controls, author tab-close/file commands, and author document-status banners are absent from the rendered and accessibility trees; CSS collapse or disabled author controls does not satisfy this boundary. A normal `[Back to reviews]` unmounts the review workspace rather than revealing an author project underneath it. An external completion/cancellation first enters `Review ended` and keeps the last trustworthy workspace mounted until the reviewer chooses that return. This keeps Principle 4's hidden machinery and Principle 6's document-centered navigation true without giving the reviewer a second, project-shaped route to the same document.

- **Comment:** anchored thread, posts immediately (D20 — no submit-batch step; reviewers see each other's threads).
- **Suggest (D19):** composer pre-filled with the selected text; reviewer edits it; a mini-diff previews old→new; posts as a suggestion-comment. On the reviewer's screen it shows as *"suggested — awaiting author"*; if the author/AI applies it, the thread shows *applied* and the text updates with the reviewer's attribution. **Suggestions capture the *displayed* text** (what the reviewer saw, pending redline rendered); if the underlying text has changed by apply time, standard D17 handling (re-anchor or orphan) applies.
- **Correct your own (J10):** a reviewer revises or deletes their own comment or reply on the same terms as an author (FR-CMT-8/12) — the one verb this reduced workspace gains beyond Comment / Suggest / Reply. Nobody corrects anybody else's words: the author never touches a reviewer's comment and vice versa (D24).
- **Dismissal, read-only (J11):** reviewers resolve nothing and therefore dismiss nothing (FR-COLLAB-2) — the verb is absent from this workspace, as accept/reject are — and so is `Reopen`, closure's inverse and therefore the author's act too (FR-CMT-19, FR-COLLAB-2). What a reviewer *does* see is the author's outcome on their own comment: `⊘ CJ dismissed this comment · 4m ago`, with the note where one was given, in the review rail where a resolution would have appeared (FR-CMT-17, D20). It never renders as *resolved* and never quietly disappears. Being told your comment was declined is the honest form of an event that happens either way; the alternative — dismissal the author keeps to themselves — would show the reviewer nothing or a false record, which is the defect this feature exists to remove, one surface over. **If the author reopens it, the reversal is stated, not erased:** the thread returns to the reviewer's open set and the rail reads `↺ CJ reopened this comment` beneath the dismissal line it inverts — **this is the hand reopen, the only kind that inverts a recorded closure** (FR-CMT-20 as scoped at #1792); a thread the author's own rejection reopened has no closure line here to keep, and the rail shows what it always showed, because a rail that silently drops the dismissal would leave a reviewer who already read it unable to tell what happened. **FR-CMT-20 now requires exactly that**: a reopen publishes wherever the closure it inverts published (D20's standing consent, no per-item send) and is *stated* there rather than erasing the closure line. The same requirement binds the author's own card, from the other side — no Emend surface may show the author less of this thread's closure history than this rail shows the reviewer.
- **Live updates:** author edits arrive as a subtle "document updated" pulse on the top bar — never a mid-read jump; new/changed paragraphs get a temporary margin tick.
- **No "finish review" ceremony** (J6-Q1): watching comments arrive is the signal. (A wrap-up note is just an ordinary document-level comment if a reviewer wants one. Verdict-chip affordance: cut.)

**Managed-review failure is not Missing on disk.** The reviewer never owns the managed checkout as a project and never edits or autosaves a document file. If that checkout or a synchronized review document disappears, Emend enters the reviewer `Updating` or `Review unavailable` state above. It never enters J2's author-buffer lifecycle and never renders `Missing on disk`, `[Keep editing]`, `[Recreate]`, `[Save As…]`, `[Close…]`, or the destructive `Close without saving` guard. Those actions preserve an author's only writable in-memory copy; reviewer mode has no such copy to destroy. A queued reviewer comment or suggestion is separate append-only review data (FR-COLLAB-9) and remains queued through the review-level failure.

**The author can end the review while the reviewer is away.** Completion or cancellation observed from `Opening review`, `Ready`, `Updating`, `Offline — queued`, or `Review unavailable` enters `Review ended`; the workspace never continues to advertise live sync and never auto-navigates mid-read. From Opening or Review unavailable, the terminal outcome supersedes the pending/error message, stops any retry, and shows no document if no trustworthy snapshot ever existed. Completion and cancellation-with-Keep leave already-synced comments and suggestions in readable history; cancellation-with-Discard removes that synced review-comment history from the review rail and states who discarded it. A locally queued event is retained in every branch but terminally unsent: it is labelled `Not sent — review ended`, is never published after the review audience closes, and exposes `[Copy]` so the reviewer's words remain recoverable. `[Back to reviews]` leaves that local record intact. This is the reviewer-side account of J5's terminal action, not a second finish ceremony.

**Edge states:** reviewer opens a doc mid-agent-batch on author side (they just see changes appear with the pulse — same as any author edit); reviewer offline (comments queue locally, `⚠ will sync` dot — FR-COLLAB-9 append-only makes this safe); the author ends the review while an event is queued (the event takes the terminal unsent treatment above and is not lost or sent to a closed audience); the selected review document becomes unavailable (review-level failure above, with no author recovery surface); orphaned thread after author rewrite (D17 chip: "original text changed", quote preserved); reviewer tries the GitHub lane instead (their PR comments appear in the same timeline, provenance-tagged).

---

### J7 — Agent permission & interruption

**Permission request (FR-AGENT-3, D14).** The agent hits a gated action (shell command, script run). Its working card pauses and grows a permission block — inline, never a modal; the editor stays fully usable:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────┐
│ ◔ Agent working…      ⏸ waiting │
│  ✓ read workflows/sermon-…      │
│  ⚠ WANTS TO RUN:                │
│  `python scripts/analyze_       │
│   scripture_refs.py drafts/…`   │
│  why: "verify all citations     │
│   before stage 2"               │
│ [Allow once] [Always allow ▾]   │
│ [Deny — tell it why…]           │
└─────────────────────────────────┘
```

- `Always allow ▾` scopes: this exact command / this script / (never broader than the project).

**J7 script-scope ruling (CJ, 2026-09-07, #2399; D14 / FR-AGENT-3).** Asked “May same-run script approval cover changed arguments when the verified project path and script contents are unchanged?”, CJ answered **“Yes.”** A script grant therefore lasts only for the current run and covers changed arguments only when the canonical project-contained script path and its contents remain unchanged. The interpreter and execution/security controls remain bound. The callback supplies no trustworthy working directory: do not infer a relative script path from the project root. Compound, dynamic, unresolved, or outside-project invocations have no script scope; Allow once, exact-command scope where available, and denial remain the existing choices. Verify the identity again when deciding a waiting request and before reusing a grant. This approval is independent of D21 protected-document delivery approval.

- `Deny — tell it why…` opens an inline note; the agent continues with your reasoning (mirrors J4's guidance pattern).
- Ignored requests just wait — card shows `⏸ waiting 4m`; the agent never times out into acting.

**Interruption (FR-AGENT-6).** `[Stop]` on any working **run card** → the card resolves honestly into one of:

```
■ STOPPED — nothing applied            ■ STOPPED — partial delivery
  No files were changed.                 Completed: §1 changes (5), 2 replies
                                         Not started: §2, 1 comment
                                         [Review what landed]
```

Same rendering for crash/CLI-death ("Agent stopped unexpectedly — …"), with a `[Retry from here]` affordance where the run was resumable (e.g., unstarted batch threads).

When the run was addressing a comment, the thread leaves *Working* by §2's exit table. Its outcome is stated on the thread; the run-level accounting stays here. The same event therefore has one run account and one thread outcome, never two copies of either.

**Away-from-keyboard:** permission requests and terminal failures raise an **OS notification** (default on); routine completions ("revision delivered") do not (default off, toggleable) — the agent asks for attention only when blocked or broken.

**Permission attention when the run card is filtered out (#2429; CJ, 2026-09-07).** While the current project has at least one pending permission request, the existing Timeline host carries one nonnumeric Pause badge, whether the matching run card is exposed, facet-filtered, or hidden with the collapsed timeline. Expanded, the `TIMELINE` label becomes the attention action and stays separate from the honest collapse toggle; collapsed, the existing `Timeline ››` chip is the attention and reveal action. The badge clears when no request remains pending, whether by decision, Stop, terminal run state, or project replacement. It never becomes a request count: stacked decisions still happen in FIFO order on their single run card, UXR-Q2's `N new in Chat` continues to count submitted cards only, and J7's OS notification remains a separate requirement.

Arrival preserves the current facet, timeline rail mode, timeline scroll, editor selection and DOM focus. In Review 1-by-1 it also preserves the current change, review index and focused-pass mode. The cue does not animate. A visually hidden status node (`role=status`, `aria-live=polite`, `aria-atomic=true`) announces exactly `Agent is waiting for permission.` once for each unique `{projectId, runId, headRequestId}` when that request first becomes the FIFO head. Duplicate callbacks, revisions, timer ticks, a non-head addition, facet changes and cue activation are silent; advancement to a new head announces once, and project replacement resets the announcement identity.

The author's explicit activation is navigation they requested: if necessary it expands the timeline, selects Chat, scrolls the stream to the matching run, expands that card and focuses the oldest actionable FIFO head permission block's real heading. That heading is programmatically focusable and has the accessible name `Permission request: <action>`; the block is a section labelled by it. Focus never lands on an approval button. When a request clears while focus is anywhere inside its permission block — including controls, menus, a denial note, or a future focusable descendant — focus moves to the next FIFO request heading, or to a programmatically focusable status heading on the same run card when none remains. The expanded host always retains one real level-2 heading with accessible name `Timeline`; it is non-actionable, has `tabindex=-1`, and contains the visible label or attention action without changing its own identity. If the expanded Timeline attention action itself clears while it owns focus, focus moves with `preventScroll` to the adjacent collapse toggle when that owner-provided control is mounted, connected and focusable, otherwise to the persistent Timeline heading. Focusing either destination does not activate it. The heading's programmatic fallback displays the existing 2 px accent outline with 1 px offset regardless of input modality; a later Tab proceeds in ordinary DOM order. Clearing does not move focus when the action did not own it, and a new request while the heading owns focus does not move focus to or activate the remounted action. The collapsed Timeline button remains the same DOM node through badge arrival and clearance, at both fitting and compact widths; a focused button retains focus while the badge changes. On project replacement, restore the pre-activation element if it is still connected, otherwise focus the new project's Timeline host. None of those fallbacks scrolls the editor or exits focused review.

**Collapsed-host fit and interim appearance (#2540; CJ, 2026-10-02).** The one-row toolbar keeps the collapsed Timeline action inline at fitting widths regardless of permission state; other controls enter `⋯` when they no longer fit. At a 900px window with the 236px project rail and 400px comments column unchanged, its button uses a compact visible form in both idle and pending states. The idle form displays the return chevrons; the pending form displays the one nonnumeric Pause badge. Its accessible name is `Show Timeline` when idle and `Show permission request in Timeline` when pending. The badge is the 16px `#B5871F` disc and white Pause glyph from refresh (20); the existing chip border, hover wash and 2px accent focus outline apply. The current compact 44px button uses those tokens provisionally until CJ's design refresh draws the idle and pending compact states. At fitting 1000px the full chip stays inline before and after badge changes, preserving its button node and focus even when the old ordinary overflow rule would have returned the idle chip to `⋯`. At any full-chip fit threshold, the idle button reserves the pending badge's 16px space without drawing a badge; its measured width and neighboring controls' overflow placement therefore do not change when a permission arrives or clears. The compact outer button is already fixed at 44px, with the idle chevrons centered. A focused formatting control, whether inline or in keyboard-open `⋯`, keeps its own DOM node and focus through passive arrival. Neither platform gets a second toolbar row or a second permission cue. Generated `docs/design/` is not hand-edited here.

**Edge states:** multiple queued permission requests (stack within the card, decide in order); permission needed while you're in a review-1-by-1 pass (the Timeline-host badge above, no focus theft — Principle 2); Stop pressed during the *delivery* moment (delivery is atomic per FR-AGENT-6 — either the set lands complete or not at all); a run pauses for permission while addressing a comment (the thread's line reads `⏸ Waiting on permission`; the permission block is never duplicated onto the thread — one request, one place to decide); the run card remains the only place `[Stop]` appears (J10, UXR-Q9).

---

### J8 — First launch / onboarding (author flavor; reviewer flavor is J6)

**Screen 1 — a door, not a wizard:**

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│            Welcome to Emend                │
│  [ Open a folder… ]  [ Clone from GitHub… ]  │
│  Recent: (none yet)                          │
│                                              │
│  Writing partners           (FR-X-7)         │
│  ● Claude Code — signed in as cj@…      ✓    │
│  ○ Copilot CLI — installed, not signed in    │
│      [Guide me through sign-in]              │
│  ○ (none installed?) → editor works fully;   │
│      AI lights up when a partner is ready    │
│                                              │
│  GitHub: not connected — needed only when    │
│  you share or review.  [Connect now]         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- Provider detection distinguishes **not installed** (link to get it) from **installed, signed out** (walk through the CLI's own login — FR-X-9); status re-checks live so finishing a login in the terminal updates the card without restart.
- **GitHub sign-in is deferred by design** — first mention here, first *demand* only when J5 needs it (D12) — but `[Connect now]` on the welcome card lets you do it upfront (J8-Q1: also available anytime in Settings).
- Zero providers → no nagging: full editor-only mode with one quiet status chip.

**Screen 2 — project open.** Grounding is visible from minute one: timeline shows a one-time project card — *"Found 9 skills, 5 workflows, claude.md"* (tap to list them; FR-SKILL-1) — then workshop mode (J2) if no doc, or restored tabs (FR-ED-3).

**Edge states:** CLI vanishes later (uninstalled/PATH change) → provider chip degrades honestly mid-session (FR-X-5); both providers available → per-project default picker on this card, switchable per conversation (FR-X-7); voice setup is *not* onboarding — the mic button handles model download on first press (FR-ED-5).

**Screen 1's three doors do not all lead to Screen 2 (J9, #759).** `[Open a folder…]`, `[Clone from GitHub…]`, and a `Recent` entry each resolve against **instance ownership**, so choosing a project another window already holds brings *that* window forward and leaves this one sitting on Screen 1. That is a correct outcome, not a failed open, and Screen 1 has to be able to say so — see J9 and §4's status-region clause.

---

### J9 — Open another project (routing across instances) — **ratified by CJ 2026-08-09 (DH-7)**

**Trigger:** you choose a project — `Open a folder…`, a `Recent` entry, or `Clone from GitHub…` — from the welcome screen (J8 Screen 1) or, in an instance that already holds a project, from the native `File` menu. This journey adds no new affordance; it defines what the existing ones do. Opening a *document* inside the project you are in stays J2; this journey is one altitude up.

**The rule as the user experiences it:** *the project you choose is brought to you, and the project you are already in is never taken away.* Emend resolves the choice against which instance holds the chosen project, not against what the window you clicked in contains — and identically whether or not a run is in flight, so switch-in-place is never a hidden mode (FR-X-10).

**Phase A — the request.** The choosing instance states what it is doing (`Opening <name>…`) and stays fully usable while it does — Principle 3's *visible, never blocking* applies to the app's own work, not only the agent's. Nothing is torn down in anticipation: the live project session an instance holds (bound MCP server, watchdog, shared protected set, run queue, timeline, tabs, unsaved buffers) survives a request that may well turn out to concern a different window entirely. Asking twice for the same project joins the first request rather than opening two windows onto it. A request for a different project queues behind the active one in request order; the status line adds `· N more queued`, then states each outcome before advancing. Its FR-X-11 deadline still begins at the user's request, so queue time cannot make the outcome unbounded: expiration fails that request visibly and performs no launch, focus, or ownership change. Thus the scalar region accounts for each queued request; a user-dismissed in-flight line returns with its terminal outcome, so a later launch or focus is explained. Dismiss stays available while the request or a retry is in flight; dismissing hides the status line without cancelling the request, and the line returns with the terminal outcome.

**Phase B — one of four outcomes, stated where the user acted:**

```
The chosen project…              What happens                        The window you clicked in
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
…and this window holds none      Adopted here → J8 Screen 2          becomes that project
…is already open here            nothing reopens                     unchanged — says so
…is open in another window       that window comes to the front      unchanged — says so
…is open in no window            a new window opens on it            unchanged — says so
```

- **Adopted here** is the existing J8 Screen 2 path, unchanged: grounding card, then workshop mode (J2) or restored tabs (FR-ED-3). The status clears — the project on screen *is* the outcome.
- **Already open here** reopens nothing. No tab closes and reopens, no timeline reload, no scroll and no focus change (Principle 2): `<name> is already open in this window.`
- **Open in another window** raises that window and leaves this one exactly as it was: `<name> is open in another window — brought it to the front.`
- **Opened in a new window** launches an ordinary instance (Phase D) and leaves this one exactly as it was: `Opened <name> in a new window.`

**Why three of the four say something.** In three outcomes the window the user clicked in does not change, and in two of those the window that *did* change may be on another desktop, another monitor, or behind this one. Choosing a folder and seeing your own window sit still is indistinguishable from the silent no-op FR-X-5 forbids — the statement is what makes the outcome legible (FR-X-11, Principle 7). These are the ordinary non-modal status lines this design already uses; not a dialog, and not a toast (a transient surface this system has never used — §5 UXR-Q5).

**Phase C — failure.** If the project cannot be adopted, raised, or launched within a bounded time, the choosing instance says so and says why, and offers `[Try again]`. Every failed outcome carries `failureRoute: "undetermined" | "raise" | "launch"` `@anchor:project-failure-route-values`; that field alone selects the leading sentence: `Emend couldn’t open <name>.`, `Emend couldn’t bring <name> to the front.`, or `Emend couldn’t open <name> in a new window.` The detail comes from the seven exact reason strings in §2 and never from raw error text. The complete failure is one line; the reason tail clips first, then the project name to its visual floor, while the fixed sentence and actions are last to give way. Both instances and the project are left exactly as they were, with no partial ownership behind the failure — the user can retry, or carry on where they are, and never has to repair anything. **Emend never asks the user to resolve its own bookkeeping:** a lock left by an instance that no longer exists is not a state the user is shown, told about, or asked to force past (FR-X-10). A `Recent` entry whose folder is gone fails here, with its reason, rather than opening an empty window.

**Phase D — the arriving instance is ordinary (FR-X-12).** A launched instance runs J8 Screen 2 exactly as if the user had opened it there: its own grounding card, its own restored tabs for *that* project, its own timeline, run queue, agent surface, and protected-document boundary. Nothing marks it as having been launched by another window, because nothing about it is different. It is not a child: closing or quitting the launching instance never closes it, and no tab, conversation, queued run, or agent state ever transfers between the two.

**Raising a window is not focus theft.** Principle 2's *steals focus* verb binds focus taken **unasked** (UXR-Q2). Choosing a project *is* the ask, so the raised or launched window is focus granted, not stolen. The converse binds just as tightly: an instance never raises itself for anything the user did not ask for in that moment — not a finished run, not an arriving delivery, not another instance's activity. J7's notifications remain the only channel for "something happened somewhere else."
