**Closing a project while a run is active.** The refusal is stated, not swallowed: `Emend can’t close <name> while a run is in progress. [Show the run]`. The project stays open and the run is untouched. Activating `[Show the run]` leaves the refusal mounted while it remains true. It expands the existing timeline occupant when needed, preserves a facet that already contains the exact run card and otherwise selects `All`, and reveals that card in the timeline's own scroll container with 12px top clearance. The card is shown whole only when `cardHeight + 12px <= timelineViewportHeight`; otherwise its top is aligned at that clearance and full visibility is not promised. Focus lands on the programmatically focusable card container, never `[Stop]`; the document scroll, caret, and active document remain untouched. The action is idempotent and makes no announcement.

Completion of the blocking run clears the refusal automatically whether it occurs before or after activation; activation never clears it by itself, and an activation/completion race still reveals and focuses the same now-completed card. The refused close is never resumed automatically. If automatic clearing would remove `[Show the run]` or Dismiss while it owns focus, focus returns with `preventScroll` to the stable authoring target recorded when close was refused, or to the connected shell-level close-attempt target if that target is unavailable. Focus already on the run card or any other stable target outside the band is not moved; explicit Dismiss keeps its ordinary focus behavior. There is no stop-and-close shortcut in v1: stop the run with the existing controls (FR-X-3), then close again (FR-X-11).

**Edge states:** a run in the choosing instance keeps running throughout — nothing about another project ever appears in its timeline, and no run is stopped, paused, or migrated to serve an open (FR-X-10); unsaved scratch buffers are untouched in every outcome; `Clone from GitHub…` completes its clone and then routes through the same four outcomes, surfacing no git machinery (Principle 4); the `Recent` list does **not** pre-mark entries as open elsewhere — the outcome is stated after the act, not predicted before it (see UXR-Q8).

---

### J10 — Correct your own comment (revise or withdraw)

**Trigger:** a comment of yours should not stand as written — a typo, a quote pasted onto the wrong sentence, a note meant for another document, or a thought you retract. Resolve is the wrong record for all of these: it says *this was dealt with* (D24). Two corrections exist, and only over your own words: **revise** the body (FR-CMT-12) or **delete** the comment (FR-CMT-8).

**The user-facing verb is `Delete`**, exactly as the mocks draw it (`docs/design/specs/comments.md`, overflow menu). *Withdrawal* is what the record does — an append to your own log, never an erasure (D24) — and that word never appears in the UI. The one place the mechanism is spoken about is the confirmation, which states what survives rather than naming the machinery (FR-CMT-11, Principle 4's habit applied to sidecars).

**Where the actions live.** Correction is offered wherever a thread is rendered with its action cluster — the comments column (`4a`/`4b`), the editor's comment popover, and the thread's card in the timeline — because a thread is one object with one action set, whichever reading of the stream you are looking at (Principles 5 and 6; §4 "Right-rail occupancy"). They appear **only on comments and replies you authored**; on another actor's card they are **absent, not disabled** — there is no permission to explain, and the per-author log makes moderating someone else's words a different feature, not a variant of this one (D24). The mocks draw the `⋯ ✎ ✓` cluster on every card and every reply block: that is the cluster's *appearance*, binding as such, and not a statement about whose cards carry it.

**Phase A — revise, in place and unconfirmed.** `✎` turns the card body into a composer without moving the card (`specs/comments.md` "Editing state" owns the appearance: `Editing` replaces the timestamp, `⌘⏎`/`Ctrl+Enter` saves, `Esc` cancels and restores):

```
┌───────────────────────────────┐        ┌───────────────────────────────┐
│ ◍ You · 3m ago      ⋯  ✎  ✓   │        │ ◍ You · Editing               │
│ "Chekc this against the ESV"  │   ✎→   │ ┌───────────────────────────┐ │
│  ↳ [Reply…]                   │        │ │ Check this against the ESV│ │
└───────────────────────────────┘        │ └───────────────────────────┘ │
                                         │            [Save]  [Cancel]   │
                                         └───────────────────────────────┘
```

- **Only the body is editable** — not the anchor (re-anchoring is J4's `[Re-anchor]`), not provenance, not a finding's severity or source skill (FR-CMT-12).
- **Saving moves nothing.** The card stays where it is; the column does not re-sort, the document does not scroll, focus is not taken (Principle 2). In Recency mode the edit's activity time affects order on the next sort change or tab reload, never during this Save (CJ, 2026-10-01). Where the comment was published or another actor has replied, the card gains the **edited** marker with its revision time (FR-CMT-13); otherwise it simply reads as written.
- **The marker links nowhere.** There is no version browser in v1 (D24), so the marker states the fact and offers no navigation — the same refusal to promise a route that does not exist as UXR-Q5's reach note.
- **An empty body cannot be saved.** `Save` stays inert on an emptied composer, with `Cancel`/`Esc` the exit. Clearing the text is not a back door to deletion: withdrawal is a distinct record and a confirmed act, and a comment that renders as a blank card is a hole in the thread rather than an honest deletion of it.

**Phase B — delete, child-first and confirmed in place.** `⋯ → Delete comment` on a parent is available only after **every direct and descendant reply has already been deleted** (withdrawn), regardless of who wrote it (FR-CMT-9). This also applies to a reply that has children. Any unwithdrawn child, even in a resolved or dismissed thread, keeps `Delete comment` present but inert, with the reason `Delete replies first.` No confirmation opens in that state. Each author can delete only their own comment or reply in v1, so a reply by another actor can keep the parent unavailable indefinitely. Owner moderation is a later permissions-model decision, not an exception here (D24). Recheck child eligibility when Delete is committed, so a reply that arrives while confirmation is open causes a visible refusal rather than a parent withdrawal over a known live child.

Once eligible, `⋯ → Delete comment` replaces the card body with an inline confirmation. It is not a modal over the document; the rest of the app stays live. There are two provenance variants:

```
never published, no historical reply          published, or historically replied to
by another actor:                              by another actor:
┌───────────────────────────────┐             ┌───────────────────────────────┐
│ Delete this comment?          │             │ Delete this comment?          │
│ It hasn't been shared and no  │             │ Others may already have seen  │
│ one else has replied, so it   │             │ it. The thread keeps a note   │
│ will disappear from the       │             │ that you deleted it.          │
│ document.                     │             │                               │
│                               │             │                               │
│ Emend keeps the original    │             │ Emend keeps the original    │
│ text in document history.     │             │ text in document history.     │
│ Delete removes it from view,  │             │ Delete removes it from view,  │
│ not the record. No undo.      │             │ not the record. No undo.      │
│           [Delete]  [Cancel]  │             │           [Delete]  [Cancel]  │
└───────────────────────────────┘             └───────────────────────────────┘
```

- **No ordinary confirmation promises that replies remain or disappear with the parent.** Every child is already withdrawn when the parent reaches this stage. The former private cascade variant is superseded.
- **The provenance line names the residue of this act.** Publication to a live review (D20) or a historical reply by another actor chooses the already-seen line and a placeholder; otherwise the private line and clean disappearance apply. This is independent of whether any withdrawn child event remains in the log.
- **The second paragraph is fixed and required in both variants** (FR-CMT-11): *Emend keeps the original text in this document's history. Deleting removes it from view, not from the record. This can't be undone.*
- `Esc` and `Cancel` restore the card untouched; nothing has happened yet.

**Phase C — after.** A withdrawn comment leaves the working set (FR-CMT-9): its anchor stops highlighting and drops its gutter icon, it stops counting toward open-comment counts and J5's completion warning, and it is excluded from `Address all…` and from the comments the agent is offered for the document (TD-1). Withdrawn descendants remain stored in their authors' append-only logs and no longer count as live comments or blockers. When their containing thread is shown, the existing nested withdrawn-reply provenance line (`5a.3b`) still applies; child-first eligibility sets no new rule for that line after a later parent withdrawal. A parent withdrawal never performs a child's withdrawal. Where a placeholder remains, activating it still jumps to the thread's document anchor under Principle 6; withdrawal removes open-state emphasis, not document↔panel navigation.

```
never published, no historical other reply:    published, or historically replied to:
(the card and gutter icon are gone)            ┌───────────────────────────────┐
                                               │ ⊘ You deleted a comment · 4m  │
                                               └───────────────────────────────┘
```

**Late offline replies are the convergence exception, not an ordinary Delete path.** Eligibility is checked against the folded view available when the withdrawal is appended. A reply written concurrently on another machine can arrive afterward. The fold surfaces a bodyless parent placeholder with that reply beneath it, even if the parent was private when deleted and the late reply is by the same author; it neither drops the reply nor resurrects the parent (FR-COLLAB-9, P5, FR-CMT-13). The same applies to a reply whose parent was withdrawn under earlier app behavior. This exceptional view does not license deleting a parent over a *known* live child.

The placeholder carries author and time and no body, has no actions — `Reopen` among them, since withdrawal is terminal and a placeholder is not a closed thread (FR-CMT-19) — no reply field of its own and no anchor highlight. A thread reduced to a placeholder is not open, so it hides and reveals with resolved threads rather than sitting in the open column; it keeps its anchor-order position when shown. A late live reply stays intact and readable beneath it. Withdrawn placeholders contribute to the hidden-thread count together with resolved and dismissed threads (J11). The reveal control reads `N hidden threads` whenever any placeholder or dismissed thread is present and `N resolved threads hidden` only when every hidden item is resolved.

**Refusals — fail closed and visibly (FR-CMT-9/10).** An active or queued targeting run blocks both corrections; undecided linked changes and live descendants block Delete. The same menu also carries closure's linked-change refusal since D29 (J11), which is why the drawing below shows three inert items and one reason. The refused action stays present and inert with its reason stated on the spot, never silently missing and never a no-op — UXR-Q4's pattern one surface over:

```
⋯ menu on a comment you wrote, with undecided linked changes:
   Copy link to comment
   Mark as resolved                             (inert)
   Dismiss                                      (inert)
   ─────────────────────
   Delete comment                               (inert)
   2 linked changes still to decide. Accept them and this
   thread resolves; reject them and it reopens, and you
   can delete or dismiss it then.               [Go to changes]
```

- **A run that targets this thread refuses both corrections — from the moment it is queued, not from the moment it starts** (FR-CMT-10 as amended, ratified by CJ 2026-08-20; §2's ladder carries the copy for each rung, and the *Working* rung keeps this journey's ratified `Addressing with AI — you can edit or delete this comment once the run finishes.`). The widening is not caution: the run captures this comment's body and quoted passage when it is submitted (`src/comments/addressPrompt.ts` `@anchor:address-prompt-thread-summary`), so a queued run already holds your text, and rung 2 covers the ordinary batch case where the app may never observe work starting on this thread at all. An agent therefore never delivers against text that moved under it. The run's own `[Stop]` stays where J1/J7 put it, on the run's timeline card; the comment card does not grow a second way to kill an agent. **Closure is not refused at any of these rungs** (FR-CMT-21, J11) — correction rewrites the body, closure changes no text — so this card carries inert `✎`/`Delete` beside live `✓`/`Dismiss`, deliberately.
- **Undecided linked changes refuse deletion — and, since D29, both closure verbs with it.** Within *this* journey's two verbs the refusal is deletion only, with `[Go to changes]` routing to them — J4's existing `↳ … [Go to →]` vocabulary. Revision is *not* refused here, and that asymmetry is the point: revising a body orphans nothing. Once the linked changes are decided **and all descendants are withdrawn**, the thread may be deleted and their outcomes stand on their own (D24). The menu above draws `Mark as resolved` and `Dismiss` inert beside `Delete comment` because FR-CMT-21(a) refuses **closure** in this same state (D29, J11): one cause, three inert items, and therefore one reason — three blocked controls, not one, which is why J11 records that the folder's own one-control/two-control rule now points at card level for it.
- **A live descendant refuses Delete alone.** `Delete comment` stays in the authored parent's menu but inert, with `Delete replies first.` beside it; Edit and eligible closure controls remain available. This reason applies to roots and nested replies. Re-evaluate the gate at commit time; a known child arriving during confirmation visibly refuses the act. The parent author cannot remove another actor's reply to clear the gate in v1.
- No comment action ever decides, discards, or unlinks a tracked change on the user's behalf — that would mutate a protected document from a comment surface (D21, Principle 2).

**Why a confirmation here, when this design system prefers reversal.** The recorded house answer to the over-reach question rejected a pre-action warning by name, partly as blocking the user on their own deliberate act (§2 rule 1). This is not that case, and the difference is mechanical rather than tonal:

1. **Ctrl+Z cannot reach this.** The editor's undo stack is document text — `invertedEffects` restores markup and attribution together (tech-design §3.3). A withdrawal writes no document text, so there is no editor transaction to reverse, and pressing Ctrl+Z after deleting a comment would reverse an unrelated edit. The reversal the over-reach answer *relied on* simply does not exist on this surface.
2. **There is nowhere to put an after-the-fact reversal.** A toast is rejected by name in this document (*"a transient surface this design system has never used"*), and D24 rules out a trash and a restore. The one candidate — an undo on the withdrawn placeholder — fails exactly where it is needed most: in the private case FR-CMT-9 leaves **no residue at all**, so there is no object on screen to host it.
3. **Nothing has to be predicted.** The over-reach warning was refused partly because the timeline cannot model the group structure it would need to warn about. Here the consequence is exact, local, and already known before the act.

So the confirmation is argued, not inherited from the mock — and it is inline in the card rather than a dialog, which keeps the never-modal pattern intact even while it asks. **Revision stays unconfirmed** for the mirror-image reason: it destroys nothing in the working view, and `Esc` already restores.

**Reviewer mode (J6).** Reviewers correct their own words on identical terms — the same two verbs, the same refusals, the same placeholder. This is the strongest case for the feature, not a courtesy: J6 posts immediately with no submit-batch step, so a reviewer's slip is published the instant it is typed. Offline, a correction queues with the comments and converges on reconnect like any other event (FR-COLLAB-9, D24). A suggestion (D19) the author has already applied owns an undecided tracked change until it is triaged, so deleting it is refused until then; afterwards the applied change stands on its own.

**Edge states:** you delete the only comment on a document (the column empties — its empty state is undrawn, `specs/state.md` "Known gaps"); you delete a reply rather than a root (the same child-first gate applies if it has nested replies; once eligible, the existing withdrawn-reply presentation (`5a.3b` where applicable) governs inside the parent thread, which remains untouched); the comment is orphaned (D17) — correction is unaffected, the preserved original quote stays on the thread; a second machine of yours withdraws a comment you are editing (the withdrawal is terminal, so the composer closes onto the placeholder or disappears and the revision never resurrects it — FR-CMT-13, P5); an agent finding or defense is **not** deletable by you — it is not your comment; judging it wrong is **dismissal**, a different act on a different axis, answered by D25 / J11 (D24, epic #1162).

---

### J11 — Dismiss a comment or finding (close it without accepting it)

**Trigger:** you read a thread, judge it wrong or not worth acting on, and change nothing. `✓` says *this was dealt with*, which is the wrong record (D25). Dismissal is the second closure outcome: *considered, and not accepted; nothing was changed* (FR-CMT-14).

**It is triage, not moderation.** Nothing another actor wrote moves: the finding keeps its body, severity and source skill, the agent's defense stays readable, and the reviewer's comment stays as posted. What the record holds is **your judgment of it**. This journey therefore uses none of J10's deletion machinery: no placeholder, no honesty statement about surviving text, and — unlike J10 — no confinement to your own words.

**The user-facing verb is `Dismiss` (CJ, 2026-08-19, UXR-Q12.4).** The record's name is `dismissed` (D25), and the interface uses the same word: `Dismiss` and `⊘ Dismissed`. The word is now a control label, so this document's other uses of *dismiss* are judged by **proximity to a thread**: near a comment surface the word can read as the closure verb, so §4's `1 new in Chat →` chip is **cleared**, not dismissed. Away from threads — a file banner, a status region — the ordinary word stands.

**Where the action lives.** In the thread's `⋯` overflow, immediately below `Mark as resolved`. `✓` keeps its single meaning and gains no second glyph beside it. Dismissal appears wherever a thread renders its action cluster: the comments column, the editor comment popover, and the timeline card (Principles 5 and 6). It is offered **in author mode only**. In J6 the item is absent, not disabled, exactly as accept and reject are (FR-COLLAB-2).

**Phase A — dismissing costs one act, exactly like resolving.**

```
⋯ menu on an open agent finding:     the same thread afterwards:
  Copy link to comment               ┌───────────────────────────────┐
  Mark as resolved                   │ ⊘ Dismissed · just now        │
  Dismiss                            │ ● HIGH · biblical-accuracy    │
  (no Delete: not your words —       │ "Eph 4:22 quote conflates     │
   absent, never disabled, FR-CMT-8) │  NKJV/ESV" → §2 ¶3            │
                                     │ [Add a note]        [Reopen]  │
                                     └───────────────────────────────┘

⋯ menu on a comment you wrote yourself:
  Copy link to comment
  Mark as resolved
  Dismiss
  ─────────────────────
  Delete comment
```

`Dismiss` acts on activation and asks nothing first. There is no "are you sure": `[Reopen]` is the inverse, and it is on the card (Phase D).

**Phase B — the note is optional, and the card offers it after the act (CJ, 2026-08-19, UXR-Q12.1).** The dismissed card carries `[Add a note]` for as long as it stays dismissed, and the note is one short line that says why. **A note added afterwards is a second appended event**, not a field on the dismissal event. FR-CMT-17 makes the note publish wherever the dismissal published, so a reviewer who already saw `⊘ CJ dismissed this comment` sees the note join that line. **The event shape, and the fold that pairs the two, are the Architect's** (TD-1, P5, FR-CMT-18).

**The dismissed card does not vanish out from under the act.** A just-dismissed card **keeps its place in the column until the user's next action elsewhere**, the rule §2 already uses for a stopped run's note, and joins the hidden set after that. A reveal of the hidden threads brings `[Add a note]` and `Reopen` back with it. The same reachability rule covers the resolved card, which now carries `Reopen` too (Phase D).

**Phase C — what the record does, and who reads it.**

- **Closed exactly as resolved is** (FR-CMT-15): out of the open counts, hidden with the resolved threads under one chip, out of `Address all…` and out of the comments the agent gets for the document (TD-1), and no D18 completion warning. Nothing keyed to open-versus-closed changes, and **a reopen puts the thread back into each of those, item for item** (FR-CMT-20, Phase D).
- **Never renders as resolved anywhere** — §2's closure rule.
- **The external reviewer sees it** wherever a resolution would have appeared (FR-CMT-17, D20): `⊘ CJ dismissed this comment`, with the note. J6 carries the reviewer's side.
- **The agent sees it on re-delegation** (FR-CMT-18): a thread reopened after a dismissal carries the dismissal and its note, as a rejection already does. V1 builds no other consumer — no skill feedback loop, no suppression of a later run that raises the same finding, and no analytics.

**Phase D — `Reopen` is the inverse, and since 2026-08-20 it is a requirement rather than this journey's assumption (D28, FR-CMT-19/20).** CJ ruled the UX half on 2026-08-19 (UXR-Q12.3). A closed thread carries **`Reopen`**, whichever outcome closed it: dismissed, resolved by hand, or resolved automatically once its linked changes were decided.

- **Where it is offered, and where it is not.** On a **closed** thread and nowhere else, and **never on a withdrawn placeholder**, which is not a closed thread (FR-CMT-19, FR-CMT-13, J10).
- **How it is reached.** A just-closed card holds its place (Phase B), so the control is on the card the user already looks at. Once the card joins the hidden set, a reveal of that set is how the user reaches it. The control lives on the thread and nowhere else, with no separate reopen route. **Placement inside the card stays `docs/design/`'s:** beside `[Add a note]` on the dismissed card, an item in the single `⋯` on the resolved card. The rule is one-act reachability from the closed card.
- **It decides no agent work, and no refusal binds it.** A reopen never accepts, rejects, or unlinks a tracked change (FR-CMT-19, D21), so a thread that accepted changes resolved reopens as an ordinary open thread while those changes stand on their own. Neither limb of the closure-availability rule reaches it (FR-CMT-21).
- **Whose act it is, and whether it asks first.** The author's, on a thread of any provenance, **author mode only**. Reviewers close nothing and therefore reopen nothing (FR-COLLAB-2). It is **not confirmed** (FR-CMT-19).
- **What the record shows, on both sides — for a reopen that inverts a *recorded* closure, which is every hand reopen and no rejection-driven one** (FR-CMT-20). A reopen publishes wherever the closure it inverts published, and it is **stated** there rather than erasing the closure line (D20): J6's rail keeps `⊘ CJ dismissed this comment` and adds `↺ CJ reopened this comment` beneath it. The author's own card shows the same cycle, because **no Emend surface may show the author less of a thread's own closure history than the external reviewer's copy shows**.
- **What it lands in, and what it carries.** The *Reopened* condition of §2 — **one condition with two causes**, a rejection or a hand reopen — open again for the open counts, batch addressing, and the comments the agent is offered (TD-1). What it carries to the agent depends on what closed it and nothing else: per-change outcomes after a rejection (FR-CMT-3/7), the dismissal and its note after a dismissal (FR-CMT-18), and **nothing** after a hand-reopened resolution, which offers ordinary addressing rather than re-delegation of work that never happened (FR-CMT-20).
- **What the surfaces do not decide.** Whether a *manual* reopen is distinguishable from a machine-written one, and what the condition's two causes mean to the fold and to TD-1's re-delegation payload, are the **Architect's** (CJ, Q24(b)). The card must be able to say that the thread was closed and reopened, and must not claim a cause it cannot know.

**Refusals — one condition, and it binds both closure verbs (D29, FR-CMT-21).** The item stays present and inert, the reason is stated in place, and the copy gives a route to the work.

```
⋯ on an agent finding with undecided linked changes:
   Copy link to comment
   Mark as resolved                             (inert)
   Dismiss                                      (inert)
   2 linked changes still to decide. Accept them and this
   thread resolves; reject them and it reopens, and you
   can dismiss it then.                         [Go to changes]

(on a comment you wrote, `Delete comment` is inert here too —
 J10 draws that card; on this one it is absent, FR-CMT-8)
```

- **Undecided linked changes refuse both closure outcomes** (D21, FR-CMT-7, FR-CMT-10). The copy states the composition FR-CMT-21(a) requires — accept and the thread resolves itself, or reject, reopen, and dismiss — rather than only the obstacle, because the user's goal is reachable in one or two steps. **`Mark as resolved` inert is a state `docs/design/` has never drawn** (the `5a.4` family draws an inert `Delete comment` only). It is in the brief.
  - **A recorded appearance rule now points somewhere else, and this document flags it rather than diverging silently.** `docs/design/CHANGELOG.md` says "linked changes block one control, so the reason lives under that menu item; an AI run blocks two, so the reason lives on the card." Under D29 this cause blocks **two** controls on any thread and **three** on a comment you wrote (FR-CMT-10), so that rule now points at **card level** everywhere. The wireframe above keeps today's menu-level form. The placement is `docs/design/`'s and is in the brief.
- **An addressing run refuses neither closure verb, at any rung** (FR-CMT-21(b), which withdraws FR-CMT-16's in-flight limb). Both stay live **while no linked change is undecided** — an earlier delivery that left one refuses both by the bullet above, run or no run (FR-CMT-21(a)) — and the card's existing status line (§2 *Working*, `5a.5`) carries the acknowledgment: `⊘ Dismissed — the agent is still working; anything it delivers will bring this thread back.` A delivery clears the closure and the thread lands *addressed — pending* with the sequence stated (§2's exit table). **`✎` and `Delete` are inert throughout, and from one rung earlier:** correction's refusal starts when a run merely *targets* the thread, live or queued (FR-CMT-10 as amended, §2). The card carries two inert controls beside two live ones, for one reason — correction touches text and closure does not. A surface that mutes `Dismiss` because `Delete` is muted has copied the wrong rule.
- **That is the only refusal.** A thread that is orphaned (D17), defended (FR-CMT-4), reopened, published to a live review, or addressed right now is dismissible.
- **A closed thread is not re-closed, and no surface switches an outcome in place.** Resolution and dismissal never coexist (FR-CMT-15), so a change of closure outcome means **a reopen and then a new closure — two records, in that order** (D28, FR-CMT-20). Both closed cards carry that route (Phase D).

**Edge states:**

- **The defended thread** (FR-CMT-4) — the agent pushed back, you disagree, and nothing changed. The defense stays visible beneath the dismissed card, because dismissal hides nobody's words.
- **An orphaned finding** (D17) — dismissible, and the preserved original quote stays on the thread.
- **A document-level finding or comment** — identical behavior, with no anchor to release.
- **Two of your machines disagree** — one resolves and the other dismisses. The thread has one current outcome, decided by the log's existing deterministic fold (FR-CMT-15, P5). The card shows the fold's answer, invents no tie-break, and gives no account of the race. A user who disagrees with the result reopens the thread and closes it again.
- **A third-party reader that skips the unknown event type** shows the thread as still open (P5) — D25's knowingly accepted limit. Emend's own surfaces never do this.
- **Batch addressing never sees a dismissed thread** (FR-CMT-15). The `Address all…` sheet neither counts it nor mentions it.
- **Dismissing the last open thread on a document** — the column empties into the same undrawn empty state J10 already reaches (`specs/state.md` "Known gaps").

---

### J12 — Write in an untitled document (the pathless authoring surface)

**Ratified by CJ 2026-08-28 (DH-7), as drafted, #1804.**

**Trigger:** an untitled document is the active tab. J2's correlated-move displacement and D27's recovery of a retained buffer from a legacy unsupported restored tab are two routes here; D12 anticipates others. This journey describes the shared surface, not the route.

**Scope, and where this journey extends others.** J12 owns the pathless editing surface. It does not restate J2's lifecycle — buffer persistence, the two over-cap states, the loss notice's standing-fact rule, the five close-guard variants, quit, adoption and provenance all remain J2's. **It extends three things, at three named points:**

1. **Phase F extends J2's adoption clause** with a continuity guarantee.
2. **Phase B extends the single-flight save-as latch** recorded at § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*, by adding `Ctrl/Cmd+S` as a third entry point on it.
3. **Phase D extends the finalization freeze and its focus recording**, recorded in the same section, by saying what they require of a rich editing surface.

The contracts behind points 2 and 3 were previously recorded only in `docs/design/`; this delta is what gives them a behavioral home (CJ's ruling, 2026-08-28), and they bind on every pane, not only this one.

Appearance is `docs/design/` `9a`–`9f` and the document-notice slot, as refreshed for #1804. Generated `docs/design/` `9h` draws the legacy unsupported-source label and visible explanation without changing this surface's editing and adoption behavior; `docs/d27-legacy-unsupported-recovery-visual-refresh-brief.md` is the source brief.

**Where this pane's notices sit.** The amber loss notice and the Save As failure line take the **document-notice slot** — below the toolbar, above the standing-conditions strip — so the toolbar does not move when the notice mounts, is hidden, is re-expanded, or gains a failure line. **J12 states this for this pane only.** The slot binds on every pane, and that general rule, with its consequence for the FR-ED-7 banner and the tab-strip prohibition, is recorded separately, in the document-notice slot delta ("Delta B", #2123) — a separate proposal with its own sign-off.

**The one-sentence rule.** An untitled document is **an ordinary Emend document minus a path** — it gets every editing affordance that is a function of its own text, and none that is a function of a file, a record, or a project identity.

**Phase A — the surface.**

| Travels to the untitled pane | Stays with the file-backed pane |
|---|---|
| The editing surface, its keymap, undo/redo, spellcheck | Autosave and its scheduler (**off by construction** — no path) |
| Structural Markdown rendering; the `Rich text \| Markdown` axis | External-change detection and the FR-ED-7 banner (no path to diverge from) |
| CriticMarkup rendering; the four FR-ED-4 display modes | Missing-on-disk (FR-ED-2/7) and every local-path recovery control |
| Accept/reject, prev/next, type-filtered bulk ops | Dirty tracking, the dirty dot, the saved-state slot |
| Selection, comments and their anchors, and the affordances Phase E admits | Path-keyed change records and file-only controls |
| Dictation (UXR-Q14.1) | Agent reach that **addresses** a path — targeting, writing, delivering a tracked change (Phase E). **Amended by #2237 (ratified by CJ 2026-09-05):** the *read* channel travels. FR-CHAT-4 supplies an *active* untitled buffer's text to every submitted run as reference data — its whole text within D31's size bound, and a statement that it was withheld over it — so what stays behind is address, not read (J13 Phase C) |

**The tab is never dirty and never shows a dirty dot** (J2, unchanged). Its only dot is amber and means *earlier unsaved work was not preserved*. No editing affordance may introduce a second meaning for it — and a real editing surface is precisely when someone would wire dirty tracking in.

**Line endings.** Save As preserves the buffer's line endings verbatim (J2, ratified 2026-08-21) — a deliberate exception to FR-ED-2's LF normalization, not repealed by the surface gaining normalization machinery it shares with file-backed panes. **The CRLF-on-open banner never fires here**, because nothing was opened from disk. See Phase F for what that costs at adoption.

**Phase B — save on a document with no path.**

**`Ctrl/Cmd+S` on an untitled document is the same act as `[Save As…]`:** it opens the existing confined native save route. It is refused only where `[Save As…]` itself is refused.

- It **never reports that a save happened**, never clears or alters the loss notice, never touches the standing-conditions strip. While the document is untitled there is no save that could (J2).
- **Cancelling or failing changes nothing** (J2, unchanged).
- **It is a third entry point on the one save-as latch** (§ *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*), preserving *one panel, one file write, one adoption, one provenance event*.
- **With the close guard up, `Ctrl/Cmd+S` is the guard's `[Save As…]`, not the pane's.** One latch, and the guard owns the outcome surface — a failure renders inside the `alertdialog`, never in the pane line behind the focus trap. `Esc` still cancels the guard. **This holds throughout J2's per-document decision queue**, which is exactly when that guard is on screen: the freeze has not begun, because the payload has not been composed.
- **Once the freeze holds it is refused, not queued**, and Emend makes no additional statement. *Why that is not a silent no-op:* from the instant the payload is composed the frozen pane is visibly non-interactive — not gated on any delay — and focus has already been **moved out of the pane to a shell-level element that owns and labels the attempt**, so a screen-reader user has been told before the keystroke lands. The `Saving your session…` row appears only after 400 ms and is a *reinforcement*, not the account.
- **On success** the untitled lifecycle ends exactly as J2 rules, whichever entry point started it.

*Why routing rather than a silent no-op.* FR-ED-2 makes manual save a MUST, and this document's rule for a manual Save that Emend cannot honor is that it **is never a no-op — it goes to the surface that owns the decision.** The precedents differ from this case in shape, and the difference matters: Missing-on-disk and an unresolved FR-ED-7 divergence both interpose an **in-app guarded choice** before any native panel, because both involve a hazard to existing state — a file that might be overwritten, a disk side that might be lost. **An untitled buffer has no such hazard:** no path, nothing to overwrite, nothing to diverge from. The same principle therefore routes to a different destination — straight to the native panel, with no guard to interpose, because there is nothing to guard against. Opening the panel is not focus theft under Principle 2: the author asked.

**Phase C — what the author can read and decide.**

**Both display axes apply, unchanged.**

- **`Rich text | Markdown`** applies in full. The buffer is markdown source exactly as any document is (TD-2), so the WYSIWYG projection is well defined with no file involved.
- **The four FR-ED-4 display modes** (All / Simple / None / Original) apply in full, computed from CriticMarkup in the buffer — including *Original*, the buffer with its pending changes rejected. Where there are no markers all four render identically, as on any document with no pending changes.
- **Their controls live where UXR-Q7.2 put them:** the segmented control in the toolbar, `Display: … ▾` and the counts in the status bar.
- **Mode state is window-scoped and unpersisted**, so a pathless buffer raises no new keying question. #1803 has since minted a durable identity for untitled documents; making either display axis per-document and durable would still be a separate preference decision.

**Tracked changes exist, and are unrecorded** in the sense § *Tracked-change states* records: parsed with no document supplied, every marker in this buffer belongs to the unrecorded population — no durable identity, no author, no author-type, no agent note, no backend-asserted group.

The author **can**: read the redline, step through changes, accept and reject singly and in type-filtered bulk, and undo any of it as an ordinary editor action. Group closure (P4b) applies among unrecorded markers only.

Emend **must not** draw: any attribution on those changes; a **change-set card**, because a change set is one agent delivery and no delivery can reach this buffer; timeline triage of them; or any line from § *Change-set decision provenance*, which has no card to attach to.

**And silence is not permitted.** Principle 1 reads *"Provenance always visible… you can tell at a glance, **everywhere a change or comment appears**"* — a positive requirement. On this pane a change appears and you cannot tell. Worse, **the accept/reject control set is itself a provenance claim**: everywhere else in this app it means *someone proposed this to you*. Rendering the controls and withholding the attribution leaves that inference standing and unanswered, which is how an author concludes an agent touched a buffer no agent can reach. Nor does § *Change-set decision provenance*'s `Unverifiable` row license the silence: that row shows nothing, but is explicitly paired with another surface owning the exit — **here nothing owns the exit.** The precedent that fits is the app's move for a degraded capability: state the degraded fact (`Autosave paused — file missing`, `No autosave — there is no project path yet.`).

**So the statement is required, and CJ ruled its placement** (2026-08-28): the status bar's left cluster, qualifying the count — `1 tracked change · no author recorded` — on the count because the count is already there and already conditional.

> **The invariant, which binds whatever surfaces exist:** a redline may not appear on this pane without the statement that its changes are unattributed and unrecorded. **Where the statement has no surface to render on, the redline controls are not offered.**

**Two altitudes, and the gap between them is now closed by decision.** The status bar is **drawn** for this pane — `docs/design/` specifies its slots — and as of CJ's ruling of 2026-08-28 it is **being built**: epic **#2119** puts it in this card's tranche, rather than dropping the redline group or re-siting the statement. (Separately, **#2120** records that `Display: ▾` ships in the toolbar today, contradicting UXR-Q7.2 — a bug this delta does not own.) **The invariant above stands unchanged and is not conditional on that tranche.** It is the rule that makes the statement non-optional on *any* surface, and it will simply not bite, because the surface will exist. It is kept rather than retired precisely so that a future surface cannot quietly drop the statement by removing its home.

**The redline toolbar cluster is present only at ≥ 1 change and absent at zero** — never visible-but-disabled; the app's pattern for an inapplicable capability is absence. The refresh drew this state and it answers the file-backed pane too.

**When the group unmounts, focus goes somewhere named.** Deciding the last tracked change takes the count 1 → 0 and removes the group. Decided from the keyboard, focus is on a control that is about to cease to exist; left unhandled it falls to the document body and the author loses their place — an unasked focus move under Principle 2, and an accessibility defect. So: **when deciding the last tracked change unmounts the redline group, focus moves to a named destination — the document, at the position of the change just decided — never to nothing.** This is the same discipline recorded at § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*: a region that is about to stop being focusable hands focus somewhere deliberate first.

*The pointer case is real too — the group vanishes under the cursor that dismissed it — but it is not settled here, and the document-notice slot is **not** the precedent for it: that ruling is about **vertical** movement under a caret that is in the document, while this is **horizontal** movement inside a toolbar where the caret never is. **Principle 2 is the argument that carries.** The threshold, any animation, and whether an overflow decision is sticky within a session are appearance, and stay open at UXR-Q14.5.*

**Identity is not durable, and nothing user-facing depends on it being.** Snapshot-scoped ids regenerate per parse generation. The *markers* survive quit and relaunch — they are text in the buffer, and the buffer persists (J2). The *ids* do not, and must not be persisted to appear to.

**Adoption preserves the distinction.** Unrecorded change markers are written verbatim with the rest of the buffer; without a linked change record, the adoption card makes no claim that tracked-change records moved. Comments already accumulated on the untitled document do move with it on Save As (#1803, FR-ED-8), and the adoption card may truthfully count those comments under J2's “2 comments moved with it” pattern.

**Spellcheck applies** (FR-ED-3). **Undo/redo apply**, per tab; the history is session-lived and is not restored across relaunch — the buffer is persisted, its edit history is not, and nothing may imply otherwise. **Inline rename does not apply** — no path. `[Save As…]` is the only naming act.

**Phase D — the freeze, applied to this surface.**

The cross-journey contract is § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*. J12 records what it requires of a **rich editing surface**, because a plain text field satisfied it incidentally and a rich one does not:

1. **No input may change the buffer's text while the freeze holds.** Typing, paste, drag-and-drop, a format command, an accept/reject, an undo or redo, and a programmatic transaction are all **refused**.
2. **The toolbar is inside the frozen region.** Every control on it mutates the buffer — including the mode switch, which rewrites the projection the payload is composed from.
3. **In-flight uncommitted input is discarded, not committed** — an IME composition, and a dictation live partial (UXR-Q14.1).

   **This has a real cost and J12 does not pretend otherwise.** For a CJK typist an in-flight composition can be a whole visible clause; a dictation partial can be an entire utterance. Discarding it silently is exactly the felt loss item 5 below exists to prevent, in a journey lineage whose premise is that this buffer's work is never silently destroyed. The discard is nonetheless **forced** — uncommitted text is not in the composed payload and cannot be added without racing the terminal write. The alternative worth assessing is **commit-then-freeze**: flush any in-flight composition into the buffer *before* composing the payload, so the clause is kept rather than dropped. Whether that is achievable without racing the write is the **Architect's**; J12 records the cost and the candidate rather than declaring the loss costless.
4. **The refusal is silent** — no error, no announcement, no flash. Legibility comes from the visibly non-interactive pane and from the focus move.
5. **The restored position includes the selection.** The cross-journey section records the author's position before the freeze and restores it on cancellation; on this surface that record includes the caret and selection range inside the buffer. Returning an author to a document but not to their sentence is a felt loss a plain field never had to answer for.

**Phase E — what the untitled pane admits and withholds.**

- **Comments are admitted before Save As** (CJ, 2026-10-01; FR-ED-8). Creation, anchors, gutter icons and comments-column content behave as on a file-backed pane. The toolbar comment group and status-bar open-comment count appear at ≥1 comment and are absent at 0; an untitled document with no comments is a real empty state, not an unavailable capability (`docs/design/` `9g.7`/`9g.8`).
- **The collapsed rail uses the ordinary empty-comments rule** (UXR-Q14.3, amended by CJ 2026-10-01). The per-window collapsed state can follow the author to this tab without another request; the comments column remains at its established width and says `No comments in this document` only after this document's comments load succeeds with no threads (§4 *Right-rail occupancy*).
- **`Quote to chat` is admitted. `Ask agent` is not.** Quoting a selection into the composer is a pure text operation needing no path, and it gives this pane a deliberate way to send selected text, even though the pathless quote currently has no back-link (the separate Principle 6 completion follow-up below). **Amended by #2237 (ratified by CJ 2026-09-05):** this bullet as ratified read "since no agent can read this buffer it is the surface's **only** Principle 6 round trip." FR-CHAT-4/D30 makes that premise false — an *active* untitled document's text is supplied as reference data on every submitted run, whole within D31's size bound and named as withheld over it (J13 Phase C). No agent can *address* it, which is the separate clause below and is unchanged; the quote keeps its admission on its own merits, because ambient context is not a deliberate, dismissible act and is no substitute for one. `Comment` is present on the pathless selection (2026-10-01 ruling). **CJ ruled UXR-Q14.4 on 2026-09-30:** while this selected text belongs to a pathless document, `Ask agent` is **absent, not disabled**; it cannot start a selection-scoped run with no project path. After successful Save As adoption gives the tab that path, the ordinary file-backed `Ask agent` action is available on the preserved selection (Phase F). `Quote to chat` stays available before and after adoption. The pathless selection popover is drawn in `docs/design/` `9g`, using the ordinary `3a`/`3b` selection popover styling.
- **What the quote's back-link does across a relaunch.** J2 persists the untitled buffer and tab, and the timeline transcript rehydrates (#1813), so both the quoted text and its source tab can survive a relaunch. #1803 now supplies a durable untitled document identity, but the quoted-excerpt payload still carries no such identity and the current focus route only reaches file-backed editors. The current implementation renders a pathless quote as quoted text without a back-link, both before and after relaunch; it never offers a control that looks live but cannot navigate (Principle 7). Enabling a durable pathless back-link is a separate follow-up, not an effect of the identity migration alone.
- **The untitled document is inside D21's protected set, and no agent can reach it.** D21 scopes protection as the union of documents open in tabs, documents in an active review set, and documents targeted by the current agent operation — **membership by open tab, not by path** — so an untitled tab is a protected document exactly as any open document is. Separately, and for reasons that are contingent rather than structural, **no agent channel can currently address it**: the TD-1 MCP tracked-change contract and D3's direct-write channel both address a path, and this document has none. **Protection here is therefore vacuous rather than absent** — there is nothing to protect it from yet, not nothing protecting it. FR-AGENT-2's "all other files remain direct-writable" does not reach this buffer on either of two independent grounds: it is not "other" (it is in the protected set), and it is not a file. **Nothing in this journey narrows D21**, and if an agent channel later addresses this document, protection applies with no clause here to un-say. **Reach here means *address*, not *read* (#2237, ratified by CJ 2026-09-05):** FR-CHAT-4 supplies an active untitled document's text to every submitted run as reference data, so the buffer *is* read; what no channel can do is target it, write to it, or deliver a change into it. That distinction is J13 Phase C and §2 *Outbound turn-context disclosure states*, and it changes nothing about protection.
- **`Share` and every versioning/review verb are absent** — no project path to review (D18, J5). **On macOS this is window chrome, not a pane affordance:** the title bar reflects the active tab's capabilities, so `Share ▾` and the saved state are both absent while an untitled tab is active and return when a project tab is. That extends the already-ratified absence of this pane's macOS saved state rather than opening a new question.
- **Nothing here weakens J2.** The close guard's five variants, the quit sequence and its persistence proof, adoption, provenance and the `(n)` ordinal are unchanged.

**Phase F — adoption changes the surface's contents, not the surface.**

On a successful Save As the tab becomes an ordinary project document (J2). J12 adds the continuity guarantee that only matters once the surface is real:

**Adoption is a change of contents within the existing surface.** The toolbar row itself, the mode switch and the format group are untouched; `Share ▾` **arrives into the existing row**; the comment group is already present at ≥1 comment and stays put, and the loss notice, standing-conditions strip, pane footer, amber dot and `UNTITLED` qualifier leave. The saved-state slot, autosave and external-change detection engage. **The buffer is not re-created**, and the author's **caret, selection, scroll position and undo history survive** — an author who saves mid-sentence is still mid-sentence. Observably: **no control on the toolbar row may flash, reorder, or lose focus across adoption**, and nothing about the adoption moves the author's focus (Principle 2). *(Appearance: `docs/design/` `9c.7`, which draws the same guarantee as a same-row group swap.)*

**One join J12 must make.** Phase A rules that the CRLF-on-open banner never fires on this pane, and that Save As writes line endings verbatim. Both are right, and together they leave a hole: **a CRLF-bearing buffer adopted into the project is an ordinary document whose next ordinary save applies FR-ED-2's LF normalization — with the warning that exists to prevent exactly that surprise having never fired.** So: **at adoption, if the adopted buffer contains CRLF, the FR-ED-2 line-ending moment fires then** — the same one-time notice and the same opt-out, offered before the first normalizing save rather than after it. The author gets the choice FR-ED-2 promises them, one step later than usual because there was no open to attach it to.

*This hole is pre-existing, not created by #1804* — a CRLF displaced buffer adopted today has it. It is ruled here because J12 owns adoption continuity and because this card surfaced it; whether it stays folded into #1804 or gets its own card is CJ's to call.

---

### J13 — What leaves with a turn (the open-document context an agent run carries)

**Ratified by CJ 2026-09-05 at the DH-7 gate (#2237, PR #2242).**

**Trigger:** the author has been editing `a-commandment.md` and has not saved. They type a message to the agent — or press `[Address with AI]` on a comment, or `Address all…`. Under **FR-CHAT-4** (D30; CJ's contract ratified 2026-08-27 at #1793, reaffirmed 2026-09-02) that submitted run carries an `open_documents` envelope `@anchor:timeline-open-documents-block` whose `active` entry holds the **exact submit-time contents of the live buffer**. FR-CHAT-3's quoted selection has the composer's quote bar as its disclosure surface — the author can see what they are sending. Ambient open-document context has nothing. Text that exists nowhere but the author's screen leaves the machine on every turn while the document is dirty, and the app never says so. That is **Principle 7's** territory first — degrade honestly, nothing important happens silently — and **Principle 1's**, provenance visible wherever the work appears. Principle 6 stands behind them rather than carrying the argument: the document is the center of gravity, so what the panel does with it should not be invisible from the panel.

**The shape of the answer, before the detail.** Disclosure is keyed to **the author's own text being in play** — not to the envelope, and not to whether the text in fact left; it is a **standing condition**, not an object attached to one message; it is **visibility, not control**; and it names **which** document and **whether** its text went, never **how much** of it. §2's *Outbound turn-context disclosure states* is the inventory and holds the presence rule verbatim; this journey is the argument for it, and states the same rule in the same words rather than a second one.

**Phase A — the standing condition, before any turn.**

While the active document is one whose text FR-CHAT-4 would send — dirty, or pathless — the composer states it in the same muted register it already uses for the standing facts about Send:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ┃ "…put off the old man"              ✕     │ ← FR-CHAT-3 quote bar, only if quoted
│ ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
│ │ Message Claude…                         │ │
│ └─────────────────────────────────────────┘ │
│ Claude Sonnet ▾ · Ctrl+Enter to send ·      │
│ a-commandment.md goes as edited, not as     │ ← J13's standing line, only while this text is in play
│ saved                                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**The sketch draws the standing line wrapping to a second row, and that is a finding rather than a proposal.** The composer footer band does not hold this copy at the 1400px the mocks are drawn at (brief, item 1), and the line is drawn at its real length instead of being shortened to a string that would fit, so the constraint is visible to CJ rather than hidden by a convenient example.

- **Standing, not per-turn — and this is the whole ruling, not a preference.** The quote bar is a precedent, not the answer: it is *per-turn* because the author performed an act (selecting, quoting) whose product is pending, and it is dismissible because the act is theirs to undo. Ambient context is neither. A per-turn element the author did not create and cannot dismiss would imply an act they did not take and a control they do not have, and it would arrive on every single turn until it stopped being read at all. A standing line makes a claim about **the state of the app** rather than about a pending message, which is both the truth and the only phrasing that survives the next paragraph.
- **This is what makes it true for the runs that have no composer — and J13 states a rule rather than a census, because the census keeps growing.** FR-CHAT-4's scope is *every submitted agent run*, not chat, so the rule is over **every route that submits an agent run, of which the composer is one**. A pre-send disclosure attached to the composer's own pending message is silent for every other route. Recorded in this document today, and **non-exhaustive by construction**: J4's `[Address with AI]` on the thread card and the same action in the **editor's comment popover**; `[Try again]` on a thread whose address run failed (§2's own run-exit table) and `[Try again — add guidance…]` on a rejection-reopened thread; `Address all… ▾`; J3's `[Address findings]` **and `[Continue → 2/3]`, drawn in the same button row on the same card**; J7's `[Retry from here]` on a run card; the selection popover's `Ask agent`, which `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md` draws as starting a turn scoped to the selection and which UXR-Q14.4 now excludes *only* for the pathless pane until Save As; and FR-SKILL-3b's timeline-header overflow action when it lands. That is **at least ten routes, at least nine of them composer-less** — a floor rather than a count, and every route this document has gained has been composer-less. When Timeline is expanded, a **standing** line is on screen and true at the moment the author presses `[Address with AI]`, because it says *this document's edits go with agent turns*, not *this message will carry them*. UXR-Q13.3 supplies the collapsed comment card's narrower sent-text cue and run link.
- **Two things the enumeration is careful about, because an earlier draft of this delta got the count wrong twice.** The batch sheet's **`[include them too]` is not a route** — it widens the run `Address all…` starts rather than submitting one, and §2 already names it as that rule's sole exception; listing it as a peer is what made the first count look complete. And **"the composer" is not one surface**: this document records the rail composer, the workshop composer (`10c`, drawn with no footer band at all), and the collapsed rail's absence of one. The criterion above is stable under both corrections; a number is not, which is why the number is stated as a floor and the rule is stated instead.
- **One inherited defect, named and not fixed here.** §2's acknowledgment-ladder rule says *"J4 records three ways to start an address run for a thread,"* sixteen lines before §2's own run-exit table records a fourth (`[Try again]` on a failed run). That is **ratified text**, and J13 cited it as authority for part of an earlier count, so it inherits the error rather than creating it. The ladder's own class rule — *the rule is over the class of thread-local affordances, not a list of buttons* — protects the behaviour; only the enumeration is short. **Correcting ratified text is CJ's, not a delta's in passing**, so it is raised here and left alone.
- **Two of those routes deserve naming, because they test the ruling rather than merely counting against it.** The **editor comment popover** starts a run while the author's attention is in the document, not the rail: the standing line is on screen when Timeline is expanded but nowhere near the act, which is a real limit of a composer-hosted surface and part of why Phase B's card line is not optional. **`[Try again — add guidance…]`** is the harder case — it is an inline input the author composes into and submits, so by this journey's own test (*the author performed an act whose product is pending*) it has the strongest claim of any non-composer route to a per-turn treatment. J13 does not give it one and does not need to: Phase B's rule attaches the record to the card representing the author's act, which generalizes over it without a special case. Naming it is the point; inventing a second per-turn element for it is not.
- **"Standing" here is deliberate parallel vocabulary, not a collision.** J12 Phase F and the document-notice slot amendment name a `standing-conditions strip` in the **document pane**; this is a line in the **composer**, in the timeline rail. The word is reused because the two are the same *kind* of thing — a fact about the surface that is true between acts rather than attached to one — and §5's numbering preamble is about identifiers, not adjectives. If CJ would rather the word not travel between panes, the line is *ambient* and the ruling is unchanged.
- **Present exactly while the active document's text is in play — §2's rule, in §2's words.** That is: the active document is one whose text FR-CHAT-4 would send — **dirty, or pathless — whether or not the size bound then withheld it**. Not while merely dirty-and-backgrounded; not on a clean active document; not on the ordinary turn. **Deliberately *not* keyed to `content` actually shipping**, which would make the over-bound state silent — the one genuinely false claim available here, and the reason §2's fourth row exists. There is one rule and it lives in §2; this bullet restates it rather than paraphrasing it, because a journey that argues for a table must not carry a second table.
- **It takes nothing.** No focus, no scroll, no announcement on arrival — its appearance and disappearance track a state the author is already causing by typing and saving (Principle 2). It has no ✕ and no control of any kind (Phase E).
- **Placement is the candidate, not the ruling.** It is drawn above in the composer's existing footer row — `docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md`, "Composer" item 3, whose own heading is **Footer**; *band* is this delta's word for it and not the folder's, recorded here so the coinage does not harden into a false memory of what `docs/design/` says — because that band already carries the standing conditions of Send in exactly this muted register — reuse of a drawn element rather than a new one. Whether a third item fits there at the 1400px the mocks are drawn at, or whether the line needs its own row above the footer, is **measured fit**: appearance, and CJ's, in the brief below. It is the same question §4's *Toolbar row budget* had to answer for a different row.

**Phase B — the turn's own record, after it.**

The submitted turn's card states what rode with it:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ You · 2m                                    │
│ Tighten the hook in the opening section.    │
│ ───────────────────────────────────────     │
│ carried a-commandment.md as edited          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- **Once per submitted turn, on the card that represents the author's act** — the user chat card where there is one, the **run card** where there is not (J4's single and batch address runs, which produce a run card and no user message). One rule, two card types, never both for one turn, so the stream never records the same turn twice (Principle 5 — one timeline).
- **This is not garnish; for every run-starting route but the composer it is the full record of what rode with the run.** A composer line cannot cover a run started from a comment card while the timeline rail is collapsed (UXR-Q13.3 — the composer remains in expanded Timeline). The collapsed comment card now shows a run link and, when unsaved text actually rode the turn, a brief in-card disclosure; the linked run's transcript remains the full record, including over-bound withholding. **The question that made this load-bearing is now answered.** A successful run card **collapses by default** to a ruled content string (§2's *Completed-run summary*, §4's *Timeline at scale*) that this line was not part of, so whether it survived that collapse decided whether the transcript half of this proposal functioned at all. **CJ ruled on 2026-09-03 that it survives** (UXR-Q16.6): the amendment lives in §2's *Completed-run summary*, which owns the string, and binds the constraint below with it — inbound and outbound stay legible as opposite directions, never one list.
- **Why the transcript needs it at all.** Principle 1 says provenance is visible everywhere a change or comment appears; Principle 5 makes the timeline the single record. A turn that shipped a whole document and shows nothing leaves the author unable to tell, next week, which turns carried their text — and because the buffer moves between turns, the answer is unrecoverable from anywhere else.
- **Absent is absent, never zero.** A turn that carried no `content` renders **no line and no rule** — never `carried: 0`, never "no documents carried", never an empty row holding the space. This is deliberately the same rule as the agent card's grounding indicator (`docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md`), for the same reason: a turn is legible as carrying nothing by the **absence** of the line, so a zero must never render.
- **It is not the grounding indicator, and does not touch it.** The agent card's `refs: N files read` is **inbound** — what the reply read. This is **outbound** — what the app sent. They point in opposite directions and are never merged, never summed, and never one count: a document supplied in the envelope is never counted in `refs`. J13 settles nothing about which-files disclosure on the agent card, which remains **#1391**'s and `docs/design/specs/state.md`'s open question.
- **No deep-link on this line, deliberately.** Every other Principle 6 back-link in this document resolves to something that still exists — an anchor, a change, a thread. What this turn carried was a snapshot the app makes no claim to retain, and the document at that path has moved on since. A link would present the live text as the text that was sent. Principle 7 forbids an affordance that looks live and goes somewhere else, so the document is **named and not linked**. If a later decision retains the payload, this clause is superseded rather than amended; J13 requires no retention and implies none.

**Phase C — the untitled document, which is not the same fact.**

An **untitled or scratch** document (D12, FR-ED-8, J12) is `unsaved: true` by construction and carries its `content` **whenever it is active and within D31's size bound** — that is the only route to it, which is why FR-CHAT-4 made it a rule and why D31 declined to waive the bound for it. **FR-CHAT-4 as merged says `always`, and #2248 amends exactly that clause**, so this journey states the amended form rather than the one it was drafted against. It therefore reads differently to the author on three counts, and takes its own copy:

- **There is no saved version to contrast with.** "as edited, not as saved" is meaningless where nothing was ever saved; the *whole* text goes, not a delta from a file.
- **It is unconditional in the one way that matters, and bounded in another.** A path-bearing document stops sending `content` the moment it is saved; an untitled one never stops on that account while it is the active tab, so the line is a property of the surface rather than of a dirty state. Two bounds on that, both stated because "always" was too strong: a **background** untitled tab supplies nothing at all and is named by `label` alone, and an untitled buffer **over D31's size bound carries no `content` either** — the bound is not waived for a pathless document, and that case is §2's sixth row rather than this one's.
- **The pane has no dirty state to hang it on.** §2's *Untitled document surface states* records "**none — the tab is never dirty**": no dirty dot, either platform. Copy phrased around unsaved *changes* would name a state this pane does not have (Principle 7).

So, within the bound: `“Untitled 1” goes in full — it has no file to read`, standing while that tab is active, and `carried all of “Untitled 1”` on the turn. Over it, §2's sixth row says the harder thing — this entry itself supplies no route to the text, because there is no file to fall back to either. The name in every form is the tab's own disambiguated label — the same string FR-CHAT-4 sends as `label`, "so the same words identify it to the user and to the agent."

**J12 is amended at three sites, because #2237 makes one of its premises false.** J12 Phase E admits `Quote to chat` partly on the ground that "no agent can read this buffer," which makes the quote the surface's only Principle 6 round trip; its D21/agent-reach bullet says no agent can reach the pane; and **Phase A's *Travels / Stays* table lists "agent reach by any channel" as staying with the file-backed pane**. Under FR-CHAT-4 an agent reads the whole buffer on every submitted run, so all three are amended together — leaving one of them uncorrected would have J12 assert the old premise and the new split in the same journey. Two halves have to be separated where J12 had one:

- **Unaddressable — still true, unchanged.** TD-1's MCP tracked-change contract and D3's direct-write channel both address a path, and this document has none. No agent can target it, write to it, or deliver a change into it; J12's "protection here is vacuous rather than absent" stands exactly as ratified, and nothing here narrows D21.
- **Unreadable — now false.** Its text is supplied as **reference data** on every submitted run while it is active and within D31's bound — and over the bound the app says, in statement (c-pathless), that it could not be. Either way the read channel exists, which is the half J12 denied. §2's `Agent reach` row is amended to say so.
- **`Quote to chat` keeps its admission on its own merits** — a deliberate, dismissible way to send the selected range, unlike ambient context. The pathless quote currently has no back-link; restoring navigation to that range is a separate Principle 6 follow-up. **UXR-Q14.4 is now answered (CJ, 2026-09-30):** `Ask agent` is absent on the pathless selection until Save As gives it a project path; that rule is about giving a selection-scoped run a *target*, not about whether FR-CHAT-4 supplies reference text. `Quote to chat` and `Comment` remain available throughout.

**Phase D — composition with the quote bar.**

Both can be true of one turn, and FR-CHAT-4 already orders the payload: ambient first, quote last as the more specific referent.

- **Neither surface suppresses, summarizes, or reconciles the other.** The quote bar keeps its position, its text and its ✕; the standing line keeps its position and has no ✕. Dismissing the quote never changes the standing line, because dismissing a quote does not save a document.
- **They never compete for the same space, though the composer is not a fixed height.** The quote bar sits above the input and the standing line below it, so both are simultaneously visible and read as what they are: one thing the author attached, one thing that is true of the app. The quote bar is conditional (`docs/design/specs/timeline-rail.md`, Composer item 1), so the composer is shorter or taller depending on it — which is part of why the standing line's own row is a measured-fit question for CJ rather than an assumption made here.
- **They say different things, and the copy keeps them apart.** The quote bar shows *the text you chose*; the standing line names *the document you are in*. The ambient line never excerpts the buffer — the buffer is fully visible one pane away, and reproducing it in the rail would be both absurd and a second, worse copy of the document.
- **A quote may name a document that is not open** (FR-CHAT-3 captures its `document_path` `@anchor:quoted-excerpt-document-path` when the quote is attached). That is the quote bar's business and the standing line never mentions it; the line speaks only about the active document's content.

**Phase E — what this journey deliberately does not admit.**

- **No opt-out, and no control of any kind.** FR-CHAT-4 is a MUST with no opt-out. A dismiss, a toggle, a "don't send this," or a per-turn suppression would change what the app is required to send — a **requirements change, which is the PM's and CJ's, not a journey's**. J13 proposes none and would be exceeding its authority if it did. **What the author does get is state they already control — and this journey states honestly how much of it is protection and how much is only a change of channel.** Saving the document stops `content` shipping, but the agent then reads the file itself (D4; FR-CHAT-4 says so in terms), so saving is a way to stop sending your words *twice*, not a way to keep them back. Making another tab active is the same trade one document over. **The acts that genuinely withhold the text are **not saving**, and **closing the tab** — and for an untitled buffer, only closing it.** §2's over-bound rows are the one place the trade runs the other way: there, saving is what puts the real text somewhere the agent *can* read it — a capability, which is all the app is ever in a position to state (UXR-Q16.3's third constraint). The disclosure's practical value is that it makes those acts legible as choices at all. **Whether visibility without a control is sufficient was CJ's call, not this document's, and he ruled it: visibility only, no opt-out (2026-09-03, UXR-Q16.1) — accepting this asymmetry as the recorded cost rather than as an argument still open.**
- **No count, no measurement — and no promise about what the agent will do with any of it.** CJ ruled the 256 KiB bound on 2026-09-02 (**D31**, #2238), so the question this delta drafted against is closed, and the position is unchanged by it: an over-bound document's disclosable fact is *whether* its text went, not how much of it there was. Neither surface states a byte, word or character count. **Nor does either surface say what the agent will read.** FR-CHAT-4 rules that the app can only emit, and that what an agent does with a correctly stated payload "no requirement on the app can bind and no test of the app asserts" — so a line reading *the agent reads the saved file* would promise, at the exact moment the app is degrading, something the app cannot know. It is the same class of claim as the deep-link this journey already refuses two bullets above, and the same principle bites. **And the limit is Emend's, not the document's:** FR-CHAT-4 delivers the prompt on stdin precisely so oversized payloads *can* arrive whole, and D31's reasoning is turn cost — so the copy says *over Emend's size limit*, never *too large to send*. The first and third of those are constraints 1 and 3 on CJ's copy in UXR-Q16.3; *the limit is Emend's* is the reasoning inside constraint 3 rather than a fourth rule, and constraint 2 (no dirty state named on an untitled pane) is the one this bullet does not touch.
- **No payload inspector.** No "show what was sent" panel, popover or expansion on either surface. It would presume the app retains the payload, it is a second rendering of a document already on screen, and nothing in FR-CHAT-4 asks for it (YAGNI).
- **Nothing in the editor.** The disclosure lives where the agent channel lives; the document already says what it is, through the dirty dot and the `UNTITLED` qualifier. **The status bar does not host this standing line (UXR-Q13.3, CJ 2026-09-30).** The composer and disclosure remain together in expanded Timeline. The rail composer still needs a measured fit treatment for its long J13 strings (brief, item 1); the status bar's `⋯` overflow cannot carry a disclosure that must remain visible.
- **No claim about the other open documents.** The line speaks only about the active document's text. It never implies a background dirty tab's text is included (it is not) and never enumerates the open set. That is a proportionality judgment, not a claim that identity is harmless: identity **is** new information — D30 exists partly because "the agent is never told which document the user is looking at" — but it is metadata about attention, where `content` is the author's unsaved words, and a line on every turn would spend the surface's whole signal before the case that needs it arrives (§2, *The author's text being in play is the trigger*).
- **No new element where one will serve.** The standing line reuses the composer's footer band; the card line reuses the agent card's drawn footer treatment on a card that does not have one yet. Whether the second is a genuinely new drawn element is the brief's question, not this document's answer.

**Edge states.**

- **The rail is collapsed (UXR-Q13.3 and UXR-Q16.4; CJ 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01).** The composer and its standing line remain in expanded Timeline, accessible through the `Timeline ››` chip. If the author presses `[Address with AI]` on a persistent comment card, the comments column stays visible and that card gains a link to the run. When that run actually sent unsaved document text, the card says `Unsaved text sent with this run` beside the link. If D31 withheld text over the size bound, this brief line is absent rather than claiming it was sent; the linked run card provides J13's honest by-name-only record when the author opens Timeline. For a run started **outside a persistent comment card**, including `Address all…` or file-backed selection `Ask agent`, Timeline expands at submission to present the new run and the applicable disclosure on its owning submitted-turn card. No composer or standing line is introduced into the comments column. The owning submitted-turn card remains the full per-turn record; a run card that owns it keeps the disclosure after its own default collapse (§2 *Completed-run summary*). If the current facet filters out the new run, select `All` and reveal the run; otherwise keep that facet. This scoped UXR-Q2 exception keeps the J13 line immediately visible when it applies. The ordinary `1 new in Chat →` cue remains for submissions that do not take this auto-expanded path.
- **The run card collapses.** A successful run card collapses by default to `▸ ■ Completed 0:17 · 5 files read · 2 changes` (§2's *Completed-run summary*, #1725; §4's *Timeline at scale*), and that collapsed row's content is **ruled** — J13's outbound line is not in it. So for every composer-less route, the sole disclosure may leave view the moment the run succeeds, and combined with the collapsed rail above there is a path on which the author is never shown it at all. **Answered by CJ, 2026-09-03 (UXR-Q16.6): the line survives the collapse**, for the reason this journey argued — otherwise the only record of what a composer-less run carried disappears the moment the run succeeds. It is recorded as an amendment to §2's *Completed-run summary*, the section that owns the ruled string, and it carries this journey's constraint with it: the outbound line sits beside `5 files read`, an **inbound** count, so the two stay legible as opposite directions rather than as one list. The outbound and inbound direction treatment is now drawn in `docs/design/` `4g`; the full run card remains #1391's separate surface.
- **Workshop mode has no composer footer to host the line, and the state is unreachable rather than merely moot.** `10c` draws the workshop composer as input and `Send` only — no footer band, no provider switcher, no send hint, and not even the rail composer's `◉` attach button — and `specs/timeline-rail.md`'s Composer section says nothing about workshop mode at all, so that composer is **drawn but unspecified**.
