J13 does not need it specified: §4 defines workshop mode as the centre becoming the conversation **when no doc is open**, and an untitled tab is an open document (J12's pane; D21's open-tab clause), so an active untitled buffer and the workshop layout are mutually exclusive. With nothing open there is no text in play and no line, which is §2's first row. **The residue, stated rather than left:** if a future decision ever lets a buffer be active in that layout, `10c` has no band to host the line and it becomes a second placement question beside brief item 1 — not a UXR-Q13.3 case, which is the *collapsed rail*, a different surface.
- **The author saves while the composer is focused.** The line disappears, because the document's text is no longer in play — it is the file now, and the agent reads it there (D4). No announcement, no focus move: it tracks a state the author just caused (Principle 2). **Saving an over-bound document is the one case where this is a gain rather than a trade** — the marker is replaced by a file the agent *can* read, and §2's over-bound line is what told the author that was worth doing.
- **The author switches tabs between typing and sending.** The line re-derives from the active tab, and the envelope is captured at submit from the same state. What the line said at the instant of Send is what was sent — that is the property, and it is what a test bites on.
- **A queued run (FR-AGENT-7).** The envelope is captured at submit, not at dequeue, so the card's line states what was captured when the author pressed Send — not what is open, or typed, when the run finally starts.
- **A tab whose file changed underneath it and which the author has not typed into** is `unsaved: false` (FR-ED-7, FR-ED-3): no `content` leaves and **no line appears**, on either surface. Nothing of theirs is being sent, so there is nothing to disclose; FR-ED-7's integrity banner owns that moment and this journey does not crowd it.
- **Nothing open** (workshop mode, J2): the envelope is still sent, with `active: null`. No content, so no line, on either surface.
- **`[Deny — tell it why…]` is out of scope, and this journey says so rather than being silent about it.** J7's denial opens an inline note and the agent continues with the author's reasoning — author-composed text reaching a live agent through an affordance with no composer, no card of its own, and no disclosure. It is the closest structural twin of `[Try again — add guidance…]`, which Phase A names as the hardest case, so its absence would read as an oversight. It is out because FR-CHAT-4 binds *every agent run the app **submits***, and a denial reason **continues a run that was already submitted** rather than submitting one: no new `open_documents` envelope `@anchor:timeline-open-documents-block` rides it, so no new document context leaves with it. **What does leave is the author's own sentence**, which they typed knowing where it was going — the same standing this journey gives an ordinary composed message, and not the ambient, unasked-for disclosure J13 exists for. If a future decision ever attaches an envelope to a mid-run continuation, that is a J13 delta and this clause is superseded rather than amended.
- **The run fails, is stopped, or is refused.** The turn was still submitted and the envelope still left, so the card's line stands unchanged beside J7's failure copy. A line that vanished when the run failed would be a claim about *delivery*; it is a claim about *sending*.

**Historical J13 appearance brief (superseded where refresh (20) now draws it).**

At the time of this brief, `docs/design/` drew no such **disclosure**: `specs/timeline-rail.md` draws the composer's quote bar, input row and footer, and the agent message card with its grounding footer, and nothing anywhere states outbound context. It does, though, already draw an **outbound-content affordance** one row above the band J13 proposes to extend — the composer's `36×36 ◉` attach button, in the folder's icon inventory and with **no behaviour, state or disclosure specified anywhere** (`specs/state.md` records `composerQuote` as the composer's only state variable), against an FR that twice anticipates "a later explicit attachment mechanism." Naming it matters for item 1 below: the capacity question is not *does a third item fit* against a settled inventory, it is that a fourth element is already drawn and waiting for behaviour. Its `specs/state.md` "Known gaps" does not list one either — **26 entries**, none of which mentions the composer, outbound context, or what a turn sends; the nearest neighbours are the agent card (explicitly the *inbound* side, deferred to #1391) and feed empty states — so this is **unrecorded rather than deliberately open**, which CLAUDE.md's "silence is not permission" treats identically: a question to raise, never a free choice. The four original brief items are retained as decision history. Refresh (20) now draws the standing composer line in `4f` and the `↑ OUT` / `↓ IN` treatment in `4g`; the full run-card shell and any remaining J13 surface questions stay with their owning design work:

1. **The composer footer will not hold this copy, so the question is what gives.** The band is ≈344px of content box in a 372px rail; `Claude Sonnet ▾` and `Ctrl+Enter to send` take ≈200px, and a third item costs another separator, leaving **≈125px — roughly 22–24 characters on Windows**, which binds because macOS's `⌘⏎ to send` is shorter. The strings are now measured rather than estimated: the within-bound lines are **35** and **47** characters, and the two over-bound lines — carrying the route out CJ ruled on 2026-09-03 — are **86** and **90**, against ≈22–24 available. That is between three and four times the headroom, not a near miss, and Phase A's own wireframe shows it wrapping. **CJ's ruling also removes one of the three answers below for those two lines:** their content is ratified, so *the copy gets shorter* is no longer available for them, and the fit has to be found in the row or in the surface. The measurements after the rail width are **derived here, not quoted from the folder** — the 372px rail and `padding 12px 14px 14px` are the folder's, the 344px content box follows from them, and the ≈200px / ≈125px / 22–24-character figures are this delta's arithmetic over the drawn strings. The band is also the only unruled one-row surface **that this delta would add a third item to**: the project-rail footer, the facet bar and the timeline header are one-row and unruled too, but nothing is asking them to grow, while the band's two *comparable* peers are explicitly ruled *one row, `⋯` overflow, never a second row* (§4's *Toolbar row budget*; the status bar's right cluster). **A disclosure that can collapse into a `⋯` is not a disclosure**, so the real question is not "does a third item fit" — it does not — but **does the line get its own row, or another visible placement within the expanded Timeline composer?** UXR-Q13.3 now rules out moving the composer or standing line to the comments column or status bar; the rail composer's own fit remains for the design refresh.
2. **The run card is not drawn at all — and it is this journey's full disclosure for every route but the composer.** `specs/timeline-rail.md`'s Feed section has five subsections — three card types (user message, agent message, change-set) plus the collapsed summary row and the new-activity chip — and the run card is not among them; it exists only as lo-fi `1d`, which `specs/wireframes.md` lists as a *candidate* for hi-fi. **J7's `[Retry from here]` makes this sharpest:** it starts a run *from a run card*, so the disclosure surface for that route is the very card it was started from — undrawn, and **collapsed by default**, since CJ ruled on 2026-09-03 that the line survives that collapse (UXR-Q16.6, amending §2's *Completed-run summary*). That ruling makes this item more urgent rather than less: the collapsed row must now carry an **outbound** line beside `5 files read`, an **inbound** count, and keep the two legible as opposite directions rather than one `·`-separated list — on a card the folder has never drawn. This is the most consequential thing the brief asks for. **Sequencing matters:** #1391 item (c) — how an in-flight run reads — has a prior claim on that same undrawn card, so specifying a J13 footer on it before #1391 draws it would hand #1391 a constraint it did not choose.
3. **The user chat card has no drawn footer, and the slot is single-purposed on purpose — but the pattern is already reused elsewhere, which sharpens the question rather than settling it.** `specs/timeline-rail.md` draws the hairline footer row on the **agent** card only *within that file*; `9f.3`'s scratch-adoption timeline card uses the identical construction — `padding-top:6px; border-top:1px solid #F0EDE6; 11px #8C8779`, drawn in `mocks/Emend Authoring Shell.dc.html` at option `9f.3` and recorded in `CHANGELOG.md`, **not** in `specs/adoption-provenance.md`, which describes that card's content and carries no footer recipe — so it is an established reusable card-footer pattern rather than an agent-card singleton. The question is therefore not *may this slot be reused at all* — it demonstrably may — but whether it may be reused **on the sibling card in the same feed**, where `timeline-rail.md` gives a reason for its uniformity: an identical row position on every reply makes a run of replies read as one right-hand column rather than repeated interruptions. The agent card is drawn as *the same shell as the user message card*. So the question is not "is the drawn footer reused as-is?" but **does putting a second meaning in that slot defeat the stated reason the slot is uniform?** That is a rationale this document cannot overrule from the behavior side, which is exactly why it is CJ's.

**A ratified precedent the brief owes item 1, because it helps.** The house pattern is not uniformly anti-wrap: `specs/recovered-document.md` rules, for the untitled pane's footer, that responsive reduction is *defined, not improvised* — drop a type step, and **"the sentence wraps to its own line above the chip — it is never truncated, ellipsised, or removed"** — and gives the standing-conditions strip an explicit **drop order** rather than a `⋯`. The folder therefore already ratifies two alternatives to `⋯` for text that must not be lost, which is exactly J13's situation. That supports item 1's own preferred reading — **give the line its own row** — and it means the wrap in Phase A's sketch is closer to house precedent than the earlier framing of it as "the outcome the house pattern rejects" allowed. **The precedent's limit, named so CJ weighs it knowingly:** it is a **responsive-reduction** rule, and `specs/state.md` carves the 9-series out as the folder's only sub-1400px responsive authority — while J13's problem is **fixed-width**, since the rail is 372px at every window width, so the band overflows at the drawn width rather than at a narrow one. What the precedent establishes is the weaker and still useful thing: **the house is not uniformly anti-`⋯`, and it has twice chosen wrap or a drop order for text that must not be lost.**
4. **The weight of the standing line.** Quiet enough not to habituate into invisibility, present enough that the author reads it once. Taste at the pixel layer — the half of this design that is CJ's.

### J14 — Format text from the editor toolbar

**Ratified by CJ 2026-09-06 at the DH-7 gate (#2388); applicability and interim appearance supplement ratified 2026-09-07; exact-target and block-edge supplement UXR-Q22 ratified 2026-09-07.**

**Trigger:** With an editable document active, the author places the caret or selects text and invokes **Bold**, **Italic**, **Heading 1**, or **Bulleted list** from the toolbar; Bold and Italic may also be invoked by `Mod-B` / `Mod-I`.

The format group acts on the active editor's retained target, once, as one undoable source edit. If the whole target already has the chosen semantic format, the command removes it; otherwise it applies it to the whole target. The control updates immediately from the resulting document and selection state.

**The semantic answer is exact or pending, never stale.** Each control queries `active`, `inactive`, or `pending` for the exact document revision and selection it would act on (§2). A newly reached target that the authoritative background parse does not yet cover makes only the affected controls temporarily unavailable and unpressed. The controls retain their geometry; a focused one retains focus and its external ring. When CodeMirror publishes the exact parse, each control becomes active or inactive in place without moving the selection, focus, scroll, or editor mode and without announcing an unsolicited update. This is the FR-X-4/TD-13 trade: Emend waits for an exact background answer rather than synchronously parsing the whole document or displaying an answer for an older target.

Pointer, `Enter`, and `Space` do not activate a pending control. A pending `Mod-B` or `Mod-I` makes no edit, keeps editor focus and selection, and is neither queued nor replayed: the target might change before the parse arrives. The shared status region says `Formatting is still loading for this part of the document. Try again in a moment.` and clears that line when the exact target resolves if the line still owns the region. Pending is temporary uncertainty and never uses an applicability-refusal reason.

```text
caret enters an uncached region
→ Bold: pending, unavailable, unpressed
→ Mod-B: no edit; loading line appears
→ CodeMirror publishes the exact parse
→ Bold becomes active or inactive in place; caret and selection do not move
→ the author invokes Mod-B again if still wanted
```

**Applicability is fail-closed and atomic.** YAML frontmatter and fenced or indented code are not prose-format targets; Bold and Italic also do not enter inline code. Heading 1 and Bulleted list can contain inline-code spans because their edit is to the containing block, not inside the span; neither block command applies to a table or raw HTML block. A target touching any region excluded for that command produces no edit at all — Emend does not format only the convenient fragments — and focus and selection stay where the author left them. The shared status region uses one exact template, naming the invoked control and the first excluded region in document order: ``<Control> isn't available for this selection because it includes <reason>.`` The reason is `YAML frontmatter`, `code`, `a table`, or `HTML`; for example, `Heading 1 isn't available for this selection because it includes a table.` This sentence describes a known refusal and never a pending query.

**Block normalization preserves the authored unit.** A mixed selection of eligible paragraphs, headings, and list items normalizes every touched nonblank block to the chosen Heading 1 or Bulleted-list structure; when all touched blocks already have it, the same act removes it from all of them. A GFM task item is a Bulleted-list item, so its control is active. Toggling the list off removes the bullet and preserves `[ ]` or `[x]` as literal authored text; toggling the resulting paragraph on restores the task item:

```markdown
- [x] Ship the release
```

→ `[x] Ship the release` → `- [x] Ship the release`

A soft-line continuation is one semantic CommonMark paragraph, not one block per physical line. Heading 1 therefore preserves a multiline paragraph's authored line breaks by using one Setext H1, and toggling it off removes the underline while retaining both content lines:

```markdown
A title that starts here
and continues here
```

→

```markdown
A title that starts here
and continues here
===
```

A single-line paragraph continues to use ATX `# `. An existing Setext H1 is semantically active regardless of its source spelling and follows the same toggle. The command layer owns a valid underline length, marker choice and indentation bytes; the UX contract owns preservation of task-marker text, soft line breaks and one-block meaning.

The current Rich text/Markdown projection does not change. The command has the same source result in either view, and switching views before or after it neither loses the selection nor creates an edit.

Pointer, toolbar-keyboard, and direct-shortcut activation use §2's focus rule. In every case the editor target survives and maps through inserted or removed syntax, so a second act operates on the text the author just formatted rather than on delimiter characters.

The group remains the same component through J12 Save As adoption; no remount, flash, reorder, state reset, or focus loss is permitted. During J12's finalization freeze, the existing whole-toolbar refusal applies.

Appearance is already bound by `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md`. The `B`/`I`/`H1`/`≔` drawings are temporary glyphs, not permission to select a production icon set.

**Design-refresh brief — pressed, pending and focused.** Keep those literal placeholders and every existing measurement. Add only the state treatment the generated folder currently omits. An active format control with `aria-pressed="true"` uses the app's established toggle treatment: accent `#2F5D8C` fill with a white glyph. Keyboard focus on an inactive control uses the ordinary platform focus ring — Windows `2px #2F5D8C` with a `1px` offset; macOS `0 0 0 3px rgba(47,93,140,.45)`. Focus and active compose rather than replace one another: retain the accent fill and white glyph, then draw that same external focus ring. A pending control removes `aria-pressed`, sets `aria-disabled="true"` and `aria-busy="true"`, keeps the inactive geometry with no accent fill, and renders its glyph in inert `#A39E92`; only the controls whose exact query is pending receive that treatment. Focus and pending also compose: retain the inert glyph and no-fill surface, then draw the same external focus ring so focus does not disappear when a query becomes pending. Resolution to active or inactive changes only this state treatment in place. No combination changes geometry, moves a control, or resizes the one-row toolbar. This brief chooses no production icon and does not replace `B`/`I`/`H1`/`≔`. CJ authorized implementation from this exact brief on 2026-09-07 until the next generated `docs/design/` refresh carries it; the refresh remains required to reconcile the generated visual layer, but it no longer blocks this tranche.

### J15 — Recover from a render failure (the shell cannot render its own error surface)

**CJ resolved the behavioural choice on 2026-09-04 (12:24Z, three numbered items), and ruled four more things on 2026-09-07** — native close, the close refusal as an assembly, its list punctuation, and that a queued reviewer event is not a close guard. Those rulings are transcribed here and in §2, each under its own signpost, the four later ones sharing §2’s provenance note. **What remains proposed is three items:** the close refusal’s three drawing decisions (**UXR-Q24.1**); the mount/focus/announcement contract, whose only open question is whether Principle 2 needs a carve-out, and none is proposed (**UXR-Q24.2**); and three of the eight §6 absences (**UXR-Q24.3**). None of them re-opens the choice he resolved. Copy and state detail live in §2 *Render-failure fallback states*; appearance is bound by `docs/design/specs/render-failure-fallback.md` and turn 12 (`12a`–`12f`), the artifacts item 3 required.

**Trigger:** an error escapes synchronously while React is rendering the Emend product tree. The shell disappears. Unlike #2102's unhandled rejection there is no surviving shared status region to report it in, and a blank window makes a display failure indistinguishable from a crash, a hang, or lost work — the one place FR-X-5's *degrade visibly, never silently* cannot be met by the ordinary error surface.

**Phase A — contain what the reader can trust. Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 1):** *proceed with the proposed whole-window React render-failure fallback.* Emend replaces the failed tree with §2's whole-window fallback, keeping only the platform's outer window frame. No project rail, tab strip, toolbar, document-notice slot, status bar, review bar, timeline, comments column or reviewer body stays mounted around it, and there is no pane-level `Something went wrong` card. The visible containment is deliberately wider than any claim about where the exception arose: a render stack crosses pane and shared-state boundaries, and this surface has no evidence that the last-rendered neighbours still describe current state. The Architect may use one technical boundary or several, provided every arrangement preserves that visible containment; this journey assigns no component homes.

The replacement is immediate and still: nothing of the old shell fades, slides or lingers, nothing reloads automatically, and no selection or scroll position is claimed from a tree that no longer exists. Focus moves once, to the fallback's single `main` root, and `[Reload window]` is the next keyboard stop — a proposed contract, set out in §2 (*Mount, focus, and the one announcement*), which also records why no Principle 2 carve-out is asked for.

**Phase B — say what is still true, by mode. Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 2):** *the four mode-safe copy variants … are approved.* §2 carries the approved strings; the shape of the claim is what J15 owns:

- **Author with a project** — name where durable document text lives, then name the limit. A file-backed document and a J12 pathless buffer never collapse into `Your work is safe`; durable file text and restorable session text are two different claims, and this screen can make only the first.
- **Author with a project, and a run that was working or waiting** — append the conditional sentence, and only then. It says the run may still be active and that reloading neither stops it nor decides its permission request. The last activity item is never re-rendered as current.
- **Author before a project is established** — say no project is open, and make no file or session claim at all.
- **Reviewer** — name the review and the work that may not have been sent, and nothing about a project, path, checkout, branch or autosave (J6, Principles 4 and 7). The row carries no durable half deliberately: CJ ruled on 2026-09-07 that a queued reviewer event is not a close guard, because J6 and FR-COLLAB-9 keep it recoverable, so the only reviewer work this surface speaks about is a composer draft that really may be lost (§2).
- **Mode not established** — the neutral row. A mode-safe omission beats a guessed reassurance.

**Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 1):** for as long as this fallback is mounted, Principle 3's live activity feed and J7's inline permission controls and `[Stop]` are **unavailable**, scoped to the interval between the failed tree and a successful reload/remount. The conditional sentence is an acknowledgement, not a control. J7's ratified *"the agent never times out into acting"* is what makes the interval safe: a request with no visible controls waits rather than proceeding.

**Phase C — recover by restarting the display, and by authorizing nothing else. Ruled by CJ, 2026-09-04 (item 2):** *`Reload window` as the sole action … including the specified rejected/non-completing reload retry state.* The reader chooses `[Reload window]`. One remount is requested; the act is not authorization to save, submit, decide a change, answer a permission request, stop a run or issue any other command, and the render failure attributes no outcome to any of them. Pending renders as the inert-but-focused `Reloading window…`, and a duplicate activation joins it. Success returns through the ordinary welcome / author / reviewer entry path — whose own first frame is the entry path's, not this surface's. Rejection or bounded noncompletion stays in this instance: the button returns, keeps focus, and one polite atomic status line is inserted for that request. A remount that begins and fails again mounts a **new** instance and repeats Phase A once.

**What the remount does not preserve is not stated to the reader, and that is unresolved (§2; carried by #2437).** A remount keeps only what the session write holds, and J12's pathless pane has no autosave by construction — so the sole action can discard the very untitled work Phase D refuses to abandon on a close. The asymmetry is named here rather than closed by gating a control CJ approved as the sole action.

**Phase D — honour native close without bypassing the guards that were lost. Ruled by CJ, confirmed 2026-09-07, accepted outside this PR thread** (§2 records the provenance, because a reader searching the thread will not find it there). The platform close affordance is not a second in-surface action, and it is not disabled either. Where no ordinary guard applies it closes the window exactly as from the shell, drawing nothing. Where any guard would normally hold — dirty or untitled author work, an active or awaiting run, an unsent reviewer composer draft — the close is **refused**: the fallback and the protected work stay mounted, a mode-safe reason appears, and `[Reload window]` remains the only action. There is no `Close anyway` and no discard, because the guards that would have made such an offer honest died with the tree, and because a discard offer would contradict the body sentence immediately above it. Only a successful remount returns the ordinary save, discard, stop, permission and send controls. This is what makes the Phase B exception survivable rather than a silent abandonment: a run in flight refuses the close instead of vanishing with the window.

**That ruling is the two dispositions and nothing more.** Which slot the reason occupies, that the slot is mutually exclusive with the reload-failure line, and that a refusal moves no focus are the drawing's three decisions, recorded in §2 as proposed (UXR-Q24.1). They are not asserted here as CJ's.

**Relationship to #2102, and to TD-25.** The two are complements, not alternatives:

| Mechanism | What is still mounted | What the user sees | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unhandled rejection (#2102 / TD-25) | The app shell and its shared status region | One dismissible line, §2's *Unattributed internal failure*; `<detail>` in author mode only | No invented retry; every local surface keeps working |
| Render failure (#2130 / J15) | Only the platform window frame | The whole-window fallback; no raw detail in either mode; no `Dismiss` | One deliberate remount request; a guarded native close refuses; nothing else is authorized |

For a single causal episode J15 supersedes the status line while the fallback is mounted — the line is not preserved inside this surface and not queued for after the reload — and neither mechanism consumes the runtime error signal `docs/test-plan.md` §9b depends on. **TD-25 is cited only as the sibling contrast, never as authority.** The render-boundary signal, structured logging that leaves the runtime signal intact, mode and close-guard establishment outside the failed tree, and the remount success / rejection / bounded-noncompletion contract are the **Architect's**, and J15 does not inherit TD-25 by analogy.

**Two things about that region J15 does not yet answer, and does not guess (carried by #2437).** §2's status region has two ranks, and the paragraph above addresses only the lower one. (a) **The higher rank.** *"A message about something the user asked for outranks this line"* — a project open, an out-of-project refusal, a reviewer `Opening review…`. A render failure landing while such a request is in flight destroys the surface that was going to report its outcome, and the reader is left not knowing whether the thing they asked for failed or merely vanished. The remount returns through the ordinary entry path, which reports current state; whether that is a sufficient answer, or whether J15 owes the reader something at the moment of failure, is the open part. (b) **Dismissal memory across a remount.** That memory is per **window**, and a remount rebuilds the tree without replacing the window — so a dismissal taken before the failure may silently suppress a genuine recurrence after it, under a rule written for a window that never went away.

**Visual authority.** Turn 12 is the refreshed Windows and macOS artifact set item 3 required, and it draws every state this journey names: `12a` / `12b` the author-with-project window on Windows 11 and macOS with focus on mount, `12c` / `12d` the remaining copy rows plus the two reload outcomes across both frames, `12e` the mount transition and focus order, and `12f` native close — the safe close and the mode-safe refusal. `docs/design/specs/render-failure-fallback.md` owns the composition, the one-button weight, the target-specific focus offsets, the pending treatment and the single status slot. That folder is appearance authority only: §2 and this journey own the behavior and the exact copy, and the mechanism is the Architect's.

**Edge states:** the failure lands on J8 before a project is open (neutral copy); in the workshop or ordinary authoring (author-project copy); with an untitled buffer active (the saved-file sentence covers saved documents only; the uncertainty sentence covers the buffer, and #2437 records what the reload does not preserve); in any of J6's six reviewer states — `Opening review`, `Ready`, `Updating`, `Offline — queued`, `Review unavailable`, `Review ended` — where the reviewer row is state-independent and names no managed-path detail; inside Settings or another overlay (the whole product tree is replaced, so no focus trap is stranded); after a rejected or noncompleting remount (same instance, focus on the restored button, one announcement); during a remount that fails again (one new instance, no retry count); with a run possibly live or awaiting permission (conditional sentence only — no stale status, and no claim that reload is a run control); on a safe native close (the window closes); and on a guarded native close (refused, mode-safe reason, focus unmoved).

**What this journey does not settle.** The pending → success transition is undrawn by design — the ordinary entry path owns its own first frame. **What remains proposed is three items:** the close refusal’s three drawing decisions (**UXR-Q24.1**); the mount/focus/announcement contract, whose only open question is whether Principle 2 needs a carve-out, and none is proposed (**UXR-Q24.2**); and three of the eight §6 absences (**UXR-Q24.3**). Nothing there re-opens the behavioural choice CJ resolved on 2026-09-04.

---

### Document integrity & lifecycle moments (cross-journey)

All use one vocabulary: a **non-modal banner** in the **document-notice slot** — never a dialog over your text. That slot is **immediately below the toolbar and above the standing-conditions strip** — or, on a file-backed pane, above the document-info row — and it binds on **every** pane; the FR-ED-7 integrity banner, an untitled pane's amber loss notice, a Save As failure line and the adoption-provenance pending line all take it, in that order when they compose. The phrase "below the tab strip, above the buffer" — `docs/design/`'s own earlier wording, not FR-ED-7's; the requirement states no placement at all — describes **two** slots once a pane has a toolbar, and this resolves it to the lower one. **The reason is movement, not hierarchy:** a notice can mount, unmount, collapse, expand or gain a failure line, and above the toolbar every one of those shifts the format controls under a caret mid-edit. Consequently **nothing — no notice, banner, failure line or status row — is ever inserted between the tab strip and the toolbar.** (Amendment — #1804/#2111 document-notice slot, ratified by CJ 2026-08-28 (DH-7); appearance in `docs/design/specs/authoring-shell.md`, "The document-notice slot.")

- **File contents changed on disk** (FR-ED-7 — git op, external editor, or an agent write that slipped protection): `"This file changed outside the editor — [Reload] [Keep mine] [Compare]"`. v1 Compare = side-by-side view of both versions (no three-way merge editor — YAGNI until proven needed). Unsaved local edits make [Keep mine] the safe highlighted default. Once Emend observes a divergent revision, autosave, unmount flush, Cmd/Ctrl+S, imperative save, and dirty-close Save issue no further write to that path while the choice is unresolved; queued/not-yet-issued work is refused, edits remain dirty in memory, and explicit manual/close Save returns attention here rather than acting as implicit Keep mine. An atomic save issued before observation may still complete — v1 does not claim cross-process compare-and-replace or transaction-long confinement against a non-cooperative writer — but its completion cannot dismiss or replace the captured side shown by the banner and Compare. A poll recognizes that known Emend publication specifically; a later genuinely external revision becomes the current side shown here. [Reload] replaces the buffer with the current captured bytes and atomically restores them to disk, remaining honestly dirty until that recovery write succeeds — that recovery write applies the same FR-ED-2 line-ending normalization as every other save (the CRLF opt-out still applies), so v1 does not claim byte-for-byte preservation of a foreign editor's line endings on Reload; [Keep mine] alone authorizes the next local-content write against the current observed disk side and immediately saves edits accumulated during the pause; Save As remains a separate safe path to a user-chosen document identity. Captured bytes are recovery data for this unresolved process episode and are not guaranteed across process loss. (CJ architecture amendment #1599, 2026-08-16.) **If [Reload]'s own recovery write fails**, the buffer keeps the captured bytes exactly as Reload placed them, the document stays honestly dirty, and the write pause stays active — the banner reappears amber with the honest heading ("The version from disk is here, but it isn’t saved") and the real reason, and offers only `[Try again]` (highlighted default) · `[Save As…]` · `[Compare]`. **[Keep mine] is omitted from this state**: after a Reload the buffer already holds the disk version, so the label would be ambiguous at best and a no-op at worst. `[Try again]` resubmits whatever the buffer currently holds — not necessarily the bytes originally captured, since typing is never blocked while this banner is up — through the same atomic save path Reload always used, bounded to one in-flight attempt at a time; a repeated failure shows the new failure's own reason. `[Save As…]` remains the same safe escape it is everywhere else in this family. (Failed-Reload action set ruled by CJ, 2026-08-28, #1641; implemented #2074.)
- **Missing on disk** (FR-ED-2/7; #698; confinement amendment ratified by CJ 2026-07-30): when a completed rail update proves that an open file's path is absent without a correlated rename/move, its tab/buffer stays open and the banner says `"This file is missing from its project path. Your open copy is safe. [Keep editing] [Save As…] [Close…]"`. There is no `[Reload]` or `[Compare]` while no disk version exists. `[Recreate]` joins those actions only when Emend reports transaction-long confinement support for this file; it is absent on macOS and any unsupported filesystem. Reviewer mode remains unchanged and never exposes this author recovery.
  - `[Keep editing]` dismisses the banner but retains a persistent `Missing on disk` tab status and the full buffer; activating that status reopens these actions.
  - Editing remains safe in memory, but autosave pauses for this document with `Autosave paused — file missing`; it never silently recreates the absent path. Manual Save performs no write and reopens the guarded choice: `"This file is missing from disk. [Cancel] [Save As…]"`, adding `[Recreate]` only when the same confinement capability is available.
  - `[Recreate]` is conditional and capability-gated: it writes the current buffer to the original path only while transaction-long confinement remains supported and that path remains absent. Success restores its rail row, marks it active if its tab is active, clears the missing state, and resumes autosave.
  - If capability disappears after `[Recreate]` was shown or invoked, typed `ConfinementUnavailable` `@anchor:recreate-confinement-unavailable` returns before any filesystem read or write. Emend leaves the view, buffer, rail, and **Missing on disk** state unchanged and shows the non-modal failure `"Emend can’t safely recreate this file at its original path. [Save As…] [Keep editing]"`; `[Save As…]` remains available.
  - If the path exists again (`EEXIST`/status), Recreate performs no overwrite and does not read the reappeared file. It exits to the established FR-ED-7 changed-contents/`[Compare]` flow, which owns loading and preserving the disk side. Any other failure likewise preserves the buffer/state and shows the error detail.
  - `[Save As…]` saves the current document and its history to a user-chosen path inside the project. Success adds the new row, marks it active if its tab is active, clears the missing state, and resumes autosave at the new path; cancellation or failure changes nothing.
  - `[Close…]` asks `"This file no longer exists on disk. Close and discard Emend’s open copy?"` with `[Cancel] [Save As…] [Close without saving]`. Only the last, explicitly destructive choice discards the buffer.
  - If the original path reappears automatically, identical content restores its row (active only when its tab is active) and clears the missing state; differing content transitions to the ordinary changed-contents banner above, preserving both sides.
  - These are UX guarantees; the Architect owns rename correlation, confinement capability, and conditional-write mechanics.
- **CRLF on open** (FR-ED-2): one-time banner, `"Line endings will be normalized to LF on save — [OK] [Don't convert this file]"`.
- **Delete a doc with history** (FR-ED-8): prompt names what exists — `"kenosis-daily.md has 3 comments and 2 pending changes. Delete the document and its history?"`
- **Scratch-doc adoption** (FR-ED-8, D12): Save-As into the project → sidecars materialize silently; a quiet timeline card confirms ("Untitled-2 saved as …/kenosis-daily.md — 2 comments moved with it").

### Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus (cross-journey)

**Amendment — #1804, ratified by CJ 2026-08-28 (DH-7):** these three guarantees were recorded only in `docs/design/`, which is regenerated wholesale and is binding on appearance, not on behavior. Two of them are not appearance at all — one is a durability guarantee, one protects an already-composed session payload — so they are recorded here. **They bind on every document pane, file-backed and untitled alike**; J2's quit sequence and J12's Phases B and D are applications of them, not exceptions to them. Appearance remains `docs/design/`.

- **One Save As act, however it is entered.** Every route to Save As on one document — the pane control, a close guard, and `Ctrl/Cmd+S` — shares a **single-flight latch**: *one save panel, one file write, one adoption, one provenance event*. A second request raised while one is in flight **joins** the existing operation rather than opening a second panel; the outcome renders in whichever surface is frontmost, and a guard's variant wins when both are up. This is a durability guarantee, not a convenience — two panels would mean two writes and two provenance events for one act.

- **A composed session payload is never written against a still-mutating document.** **The freeze begins when the payload is composed — not when the close or quit attempt begins** — and holds until that attempt resolves. Everything before composition is deliberately outside it: J2 resolves a window's dirty documents one at a time first, and each of those guards offers `[Save As…]`, which must stay live. Freezing at the start of an attempt would forbid the very action the queue exists to offer. Once the freeze holds, editing, tab mutation, notice toggling and Save As are **refused, not queued**; nothing replays when it clears, because the payload was composed against the text as it stood and must still describe it. The frozen region is announced as busy and is genuinely non-interactive, not merely dimmed — **a surface that only looks frozen is not frozen.** A Save As raised during the freeze is refused; a close raised while a Save As is in flight waits for it to settle and takes its outcome.

- **Focus leaves the frozen region before it freezes, and is given back.** Focus is never left inside a frozen region. Before freezing, Emend moves focus to a shell-level element that **owns the attempt and labels it**, and records where the author was — including the caret and selection inside a buffer. Cancelling restores that recorded position. **A refused close restores it too (CJ, 2026-09-05, UXR-Q20.4)** — it is a failure with no dialog, so the attempt resolves with the window still here and nothing carried out, which is the state cancelling produces and therefore takes cancelling's destination. Leaving focus on the shell element that owned the attempt would park it on something that has stopped labelling one. A failure that *does* raise a dialog moves focus to that dialog's default action; an omission or an unusable persistence proof moves it to the guard that owns the decision. Nothing about a freeze announces itself by moving the author somewhere they did not ask to go (Principle 2).

---

## 4. Global chrome (derived from J1–J8)

The journeys and the FR-X-5/FR-X-11 shared-status amendment force exactly four regions and two modes — nothing else earned a region:

**Author mode**
- **Left rail — project** (collapsible): J2's Documents hierarchy plus discovery-shaped Skills disclosures and workflow leaves, in workshop and document-authoring layouts. Git/internal/tooling paths are excluded; no git controls beyond what D18/J5 surfaced — versioning lives behind `Share`.
- **Center — document:** tabs, editor with a `Rich text | Markdown` segmented control, toolbar (format · track-changes cluster: nav / accept-reject / type-filtered bulk · dictation mic · Share), status bar. The **review bar** appears only while a review is live (J5). It is **workspace-level chrome: full width, above the tab strip**, in the same band as the shared status region below — not in the document-notice slot, and therefore not subject to that slot's tab-strip prohibition, which governs document-scoped notices only. **Workshop mode** (J2): center becomes the conversation when no doc is open. Display mode sits in the status bar, not the toolbar — see "Toolbar row budget" below.
- **Right rail — timeline** (collapsible): the single stream — chat, change-set cards, comment threads, findings, workflow/review cards — with facet filters (All / Chat / Comments / Changes / Findings) and the message input (mic per FR-ED-5). Collapsing it reveals the comments column — see "Right-rail occupancy" below.

**Review bar band (Amendment — #1804/#2111 document-notice slot, ratified by CJ 2026-08-28, DH-7):** the sentence above resolves an ambiguity in this section's own enumeration ("tabs, editor with a `Rich text | Markdown` segmented control, toolbar, status bar"), which is not in drawn order and so could not disambiguate "above the editor." **Review scope is not document scope.** The bar names a *review*, spans a document *set*, and gains a switcher at more than one — so a per-document slot would make it mount and unmount on every tab switch, and would put a review-scoped control inside a document-scoped band. Above the tab strip it sits in a position this document already establishes twice: this section's shared status region, and J6's reviewer status region. It is a third position, not either horn of an "above or below the toolbar" question — and because the document-notice slot's prohibition governs document-scoped notices, the review bar never engages it.

**Reviewer mode (J6):** center + right only; reduced verbs (Comment / Suggest / Reply, plus J10 correction of the reviewer's *own* comments); no file tree, no project-home strip, no agent, no accept/reject, and no author local-file lifecycle. The `1k` lo-fi mock binds the layout and hierarchy; §2's reviewer-workspace inventory and J6's mode boundary bind what is structurally absent.

**Reviewer status region (J6):** the reviewer reuses the same non-modal status-region surface and hierarchy as the other app modes: full workspace width, between the native/app title bar and the reviewer body drawn by `1k` (the doc-set bar, document, and review rail). `Opening review…`, `Review unavailable`, and `Review ended` live here; retry/return controls are real buttons in the line, never controls hidden in the document or timeline. The line is one polite live region, so every transition is announced without moving keyboard focus or scrolling either pane; unavailable/ended remain persistent and non-dismissible until retry succeeds or the reviewer chooses `[Back to reviews]`. Focus remains where the reviewer left it, and the region and its actions are reachable in normal tab order. The ready/offline `1k` layout beneath it is unchanged, so this reuses established status chrome rather than introducing a new panel or visual component.

**Status region (FR-X-5, FR-X-11; ratified by CJ 2026-07-27 and widened 2026-08-09; reviewer-mode widening ratified by CJ 2026-08-14 through #1515; project-open refinement ratified 2026-09-14 through #1752):** a non-modal line between the app header and body on the welcome screen and in authoring, workshop, and reviewer modes. On the established welcome/author/workshop surfaces it remains dismissible, including while a J9 project-open request or retry is in flight; dismissing an in-flight request hides the line without cancelling it, and the line returns with the terminal outcome. In reviewer mode, J6 owns persistence: `Opening review…` clears only on a state transition, while `Review unavailable` and `Review ended` are non-dismissible until retry succeeds or the reviewer chooses `[Back to reviews]`. An out-of-project `File > Open…` refusal says `"That file is outside the open project. Open its folder as a project to edit it in Emend."`; J9 supplies the project-open progress and outcome copy; J6 supplies reviewer-specific placement, copy, actions, and announcement behavior; other failures say `"Emend couldn’t complete the requested action. <detail>"`, or `"Emend couldn’t complete the requested action. Try again."` when no detail is available. Starting a new request clears the prior status so a repeated failure is announced again; a successful document open leaves the status clear, while J9 states each outcome where the user acted.

**A failure nobody asked for gets its own sentence (FR-X-5, #2102).** The sentence above is written for a request the author made; used for a failure they did not make it would assert a request that never happened and advise a retry of work Emend cannot re-run. A failure that reaches no part of the app therefore uses §2's *Unattributed internal failure* wording, its one-line bound and clipping order, and its precedence, repetition and dismissal rules instead, in this same region on every surface that has one. **In author mode it is the first occupant of this band that arrives with no user act before it**; reviewer mode already has one, in §2's `Review ended`, which states an outcome the reviewer did not ask for. That is why this state's height and its quietness are constrained here rather than left to the region's general behavior. It is reported **without being taken over**: reporting it never stands in for handling it at the site that should have, and never suppresses the escape that `docs/test-plan.md` §9b depends on.

**Project-open outcomes use this region on all three author surfaces (J9, #759, #1752).** A request begun on the welcome screen can end with that window still there (`already open in another window`), precisely the case that reads as a no-op if nothing says otherwise (FR-X-11). The **same region, in the same place, under the same rules** therefore hosts document and project actions rather than creating a welcome-only vocabulary. Welcome can host Requesting, Open in another window, and Failed; Adopted here clears the line, and its other outcomes are unreachable there. Authoring and workshop host the remaining project-open states. Reviewer mode has no project-open entry point, so J6's persistence rules never intersect this set. Appearance now binds in `docs/design/specs/project-open-status.md` and refresh (16) options `13a`–`13h`.

**Status honesty points** (FR-X-5): provider chip (bottom bar), review sync dot (review bar), voice state (mic button), shared status region (between app header and body). Each degrades visibly, never silently.

**Provider chip & switching (FR-X-7):** the chip sits next to the message input (`Claude ▾`). Switching applies **from the next message forward** in the same repo-level thread (D4); timeline provenance already records which partner produced each card, so mixed history stays legible. (Context handoff between providers is a tech-design concern, flagged there.)

**Outbound turn context (J13, #2237 — ratified by CJ 2026-09-05; collapsed-rail supplements ruled 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01):** the composer carries one more standing line, in the same muted footer register as the send hint, exactly while the active document's text is in play — dirty, or pathless, whether or not FR-CHAT-4's size bound then withholds it (§2's presence rule, which is the only one). It is a **standing condition of the composer** rather than an attachment to a pending message, so it remains true as a state rule at `[Address with AI]` and `Address all…` time even when collapse hides the line — **the rule is over every route that submits an agent run, of which the composer is one, and this document records at least ten of them** (J13 Phase A) — and the submitted turn's own card states what rode with it, once per turn. §2's *Outbound turn-context disclosure states* holds the copy and the presence rule; J13 holds the argument; placement and weight are drawn in generated `docs/design/` refresh (20), `4f`/`4g`. Collapsing the rail leaves the composer, provider switcher, mic, and standing line in expanded Timeline, reached through its chip. The collapsed comment card's run link and, only when unsaved text actually rode that run, its brief disclosure are ruled below under *Right-rail occupancy* (UXR-Q13.3).

**Toolbar row budget (UXR-Q7.2, ratified by CJ 2026-08-05):** the document toolbar **stays one row**, and that constraint decides placement. Display mode and save state therefore live in the **status bar** — the bottom bar this section already assigns the provider chip — not in the toolbar's track-changes cluster as this section previously had it. The status bar reads, left: `Markdown` · word count · tracked-change count · open-comment count; right: `Display: … ▾` · save state (Windows only — macOS puts it in the title bar) · agent connection. Anything new that wants toolbar space goes into an overflow, never a second row.

**Editor dictation placement (UXR-Q14.1, ruled by CJ 2026-09-30):** the mic is an editor-toolbar control on both file-backed and pathless authoring panes, in Rich text and Markdown views. Dictation inserts into the active editor buffer; a project path is not a prerequisite. It uses §2's shared voice states and FR-ED-5's local model and device flow. Adding it does not create a second toolbar row or waive the measured `⋯` overflow rule above. Generated `docs/design/` refresh (20) draws its Windows and macOS placement, state treatments and narrow-row behavior in `3c`/`3d`; `docs/uxr-q14-editor-mic-visual-refresh-brief.md` is the source brief. Reviewer-mode dictation placement is not decided by this author-toolbar ruling.

This supersedes the placement half of the 2026-07-31 FR-ED-1 amendment: `Rich text | Markdown` and `Display:` remain **separate axes**, which was that amendment's substance, but they are no longer adjacent. The reason is a real budget rather than a preference — at the 1400px the mocks are drawn at, the toolbar in the redline state must already carry the segmented control, the format group, `✓ Accept` · `✕ Reject` · the `‹ 3 of 14 ›` stepper · `Bulk ▾`, and `Share ▾`; `Display: all markup ▾` does not also fit. Display mode is a reading preference set rarely, not a per-edit action, so it is the right thing to move. Appearance is `specs/authoring-shell.md` "Toolbar" and "Status bar."

**Format-group behavior (J14, #2388; ratified by CJ 2026-09-06; UXR-Q22 supplement ratified 2026-09-07):** the format group is an editor command surface in both projections, not a Rich-text-only palette. Each control's exact query follows §2's `active` / `inactive` / `pending` rule; pending is its only temporary unavailable state and never borrows the known-inapplicability refusal. Its controls and temporary glyphs consume the already-ratified row budget; the real icon set remains outside this tranche and returns through CJ's generated `docs/design/` refresh.

**The four display modes are unchanged.** FR-ED-4 is a MUST for **All / Simple / None / Original**; `specs/authoring-shell.md` describes the menu as "all markup / final / original," which is three modes under different names. The mock draws only the closed state, so this is spec prose and not a drawn decision — and the *set* of modes is behavior, outside the pixel layer's authority. The four stand; the naming mismatch goes back to the design project for the next refresh rather than being reconciled here.

**Right-rail occupancy (UXR-Q7.1, ratified by CJ 2026-08-05):** the rail has **one** control — the `Timeline ‹‹ / ››` collapse toggle — and two occupants that are never simultaneous. Expanded, it holds the timeline described above. Collapsed, the timeline reduces to a `Timeline ››` toolbar chip and the **comments column** takes the rail: the open document's threads rendered *spatially*, sorted by anchor position (recency the alternative), reciprocally linked to the gutter icons in the document margin — click either and both select. There is no separate affordance that picks between the two: comments-in-the-rail is what collapsing the stream reveals, never a mode the user selects directly.

**Comments-column ordering (UXR-Q13; CJ 2026-10-01):** `Position` orders trustworthy live anchors by their current passage offsets. Document-level threads and orphaned anchors have no trustworthy live position; they follow the anchored threads in a stable tail. `Recency` uses the latest accepted event on each thread, newest first, including replies, edits, and resolution changes. Saving an in-place edit leaves that card in its column slot even in Recency mode; other cards continue to follow their own activity. The edited card's new activity time takes effect on the next sort change or tab reload (J10). An event's UUID or the backend's deterministic UUID list order is not a recency signal.

**Collapsed-rail supplement (UXR-Q13.1–3 and UXR-Q14.3; ruled by CJ 2026-09-30):** the comments column retains the rail's established width when it has no threads; collapsing Timeline never widens the document. On an open document, including an untitled one, it presents the quiet message `No comments in this document` **only after that document's comments load succeeds and establishes that it has no threads**. While that load is pending or has failed, the column makes no zero-comments claim; this condition does not specify a new loading or error treatment. `timelineCollapsed` remains a **per-window** preference across tab switches. The column updates to the active tab's threads or its applicable empty message without changing the preference. Workshop mode has no open document and no right rail (J2); this supplement does not add one there.

The composer, provider switcher (FR-X-7), mic (FR-ED-5), and J13 standing outbound-context line remain in **expanded Timeline**, accessible by activating the `Timeline ››` chip; none moves into the comments column or status bar. `[Address with AI]` on a persistent comment card while Timeline is collapsed leaves the comments column in place and adds a link to that run on the comment card. The card keeps one link to its latest accepted run after completion and across tab switches in the window; only another accepted run for that same comment replaces it. A rejected or run-less admission leaves the existing link and its submit-time cue untouched. A different document or project with an equal comment ID has its own link state. Activating the link expands Timeline, reveals the corresponding run, and focuses its card container; it never activates `[Stop]` or moves the document. If the run actually carried the active document's unsaved text within D31's bound, the same card also states `Unsaved text sent with this run` beside the link. No such line appears for a clean document or a text-withheld over-bound turn; the linked run's own J13 line still records what rode with it. This brief line is a visibility cue, not a payload inspector or a second copy of the document. **The in-card rule applies to comment Address with AI only.** Other run-starting actions while collapsed, such as `Address all…` and file-backed selection `Ask agent`, expand Timeline to show the new run and the applicable J13 line on its owning submitted-turn card; the current facet is preserved if it shows the run, otherwise `All` is selected so the run is visible immediately (UXR-Q16.4's scoped exception to UXR-Q2). A tab switch alone never changes the per-window collapse preference or focuses an empty message. The in-card treatment, empty states, and auto-expanded run landing are drawn for both platforms in `docs/design/` refresh (20), options `4c`–`4g`.

**Filtered permission attention (#2429, J7; ratified by CJ 2026-09-07):** the Timeline host is also the one attention and navigation surface whenever the current project has a pending permission request, including when its run card is excluded by the active facet or the timeline is collapsed. In the expanded rail the `TIMELINE` label is the action and the collapse toggle remains semantically separate; in the collapsed rail the `Timeline ››` chip is the action. J7 owns the waiting and cleared states, activation, focus recovery and live announcement; its #2540 fit supplement keeps the same visible collapsed button inline at both compact and fitting widths through badge changes. No permission decision or Stop leaves the run card, and UXR-Q2's submitted-card affordance is unchanged.

**Interim expanded-host focus seam (#2429 clear-focus supplement; ratified by CJ 2026-09-07):** production does not yet mount the generated adjacent collapse toggle. Until its eventual owner does, the stable non-actionable level-2 `Timeline` heading is the expanded action's clear-time focus fallback. The reusable host prefers a supplied toggle only while it is connected and focusable; this seam does not add collapse behavior or settle UXR-Q13. Programmatic heading fallback uses J7's visible accent outline regardless of input modality, and subsequent Tab order remains the header's ordinary DOM order.

This does not amend Principle 5. The two occupants cannot compete for the screen because they can never both be on it, and the timeline remains the single chronological stream whenever it is showing — its **Comments facet** filters that stream to comment *activity in time* (who commented when, agent addressing runs), while the column presents the same threads in *document order*. One event log, two readings of it, one at a time. Appearance is `docs/design/` `4a`/`4b`, `specs/authoring-shell.md` "Rail modes", and `specs/comments.md`.

**The rail-occupancy gaps and collapsed run-start rule are answered above.** `docs/design/` `4a`/`4b` draw the has-comments collapsed case; refresh (20) adds the no-comments and untitled states in `4c`/`4d`, the in-card run link and disclosure in `4e`, and the auto-expanded run landing in `4g`. UXR-Q16.4 is closed: only the auto-expanded path selects `All` when the current facet excludes the run; ordinary submissions keep UXR-Q2's existing behavior.

**Timeline at scale (D4 = one ever-growing stream):** closed threads — resolved **or dismissed** (D25, J11) — fully-decided change-sets, and successfully completed run cards collapse to single lines by default (expand on click); Failed, Stopped, running, queued, and cancelled run cards remain expanded. A completed run's line carries §2's persisted grounding/outcome summary, including the honest known-zero/unknown distinction. The list is virtualized; clicking a facet (Changes, Findings…) jumps to the *most recent* card of that kind; doc↔panel deep links remain the primary navigation. No search in v1 (D13) — collapse + facets + recency carry the load.

**Closed is closed, in every count this section names (D25, J11).** The status bar's open-comment count, the review bar's, the comments column header's `Open (N) ▾`, and the `Address all…` set exclude **dismissed** threads exactly as they exclude resolved ones, and dismissed threads collapse and hide with them under one chip. A thread **reopened** by hand or by a rejection re-enters every one of those counts and sets (FR-CMT-19/20), which is the same rule read backwards, not a second one. What the two outcomes never share is a label: no count of *addressed* or *fixed* may include a dismissal, and no collapsed line may read *resolved* for one (§2).

**Scroll motion (UXR-Q2, Principle 2's *auto-scrolls* verb):** motion the user did not ask for never moves them — neither an appended agent card nor a remote collaborator's edit scrolls a reader who has scrolled up. A reader still at the tail **is** followed there; sitting at the tail is itself a standing choice to watch what arrives. The user's **own submit** scrolls to its card — revealing the card **whole** when it fits within the viewport, or just its **top** (the natural reading start) when the card is itself taller than the viewport (#1324, CJ 2026-08-20).

Submit does **not** change the active facet: a filter the user deliberately set survives their own Send. When the submitted card falls outside that filter, the timeline surfaces a jump affordance instead — `1 new in Chat →`, counting up (`2 new in Chat →`) while more land, and cleared by clicking it, by scrolling to the card manually, or by any facet change. Clicking it switches to that facet and scrolls to the card — §4's ordinary facet-jump with a count on it, not a second navigation pattern. So Send is never a silent no-op, and the filter is never silently discarded.

This paragraph scopes **submit** only. Whether the document→timeline deep-link (Principle 6) should likewise restore a facet that hides its target thread is the same silent-no-op shape one interaction over, and is deliberately **not** decided here.

**Settings (inventory only — the MUSTs need an address, not a wireframe):** theme (FR-X-6) · autosave on/off + interval (FR-ED-2) · **Reveal markup at cursor (off by default; §2, #2389)** · provider default for this project (FR-X-7) · GitHub account connect/disconnect + status (FR-X-9) · voice: enable, model download/manage, input device (FR-ED-5) · notifications: completion toggle (J7) · feedback entry (FR-X-6). `Reveal markup at cursor` belongs in **General** and uses `docs/design/` `1m`'s existing switch-row pattern; it earns no authoring-chrome slot under the toolbar budget above. Placement note for FR-SKILL-3b when it lands: overflow action on the timeline header ("Distill lessons from this session…").
