## 5. Interview queue

**Numbering — how a UXR-Q number is claimed.** A delta that raises a new question into this queue proposes its number as **next free at the time of writing, confirmed at ratification**: the number is fixed by CJ at the DH-7 sign-off that ratifies the delta, not chosen at drafting. Until that moment the number the delta carries is a proposal — it makes the draft readable and binds nothing. The set it is confirmed against is every number this queue already carries on `main`, ratified, still open, or retired, together with any number confirmed at an earlier sign-off whose delta has not yet merged. Sub-numbers travel with their parent, so a delta renumbered at its gate keeps its `.1`, `.2`, … in order beneath the confirmed number; and a citation of the number made before ratification — in a card, a PR, a `docs/design/` brief, or a sibling constitution document — is a citation of a *proposal*, re-pointed at the confirmed number once there is one. **Why:** drafting a delta takes hours, during which `main` moves, while sign-off is a single instant at which the next free number is knowable. **UXR-Q12**'s own entry records that #1162's questions were raised as UXR-Q11 and renumbered when #1125's cascade-hide question merged to `main` with that number first, so CJ's answers of 2026-08-19 were given against Q11.1–Q11.4 and are the four sub-questions now numbered under Q12. On 2026-08-28 the #2102 delta drafted against UXR-Q14 while #1804's J12 delta was concurrently filling UXR-Q14.1–.7, so `UXR-Q14.2` briefly named a *new open* question in one draft and a *closed* one in the other before #2102 was renumbered to **UXR-Q15** — one identifier meaning two things in one document, which is worse than a bare clash. **This is the complement of UXR-Q14's numbering note, not a competitor to it:** that note governs a number *after* it is claimed — frozen, never reused, a closed item struck in place keeping its number, as UXR-Q7's items and UXR-Q14.2/.6 are — while this rule governs *how* it is claimed. The two never overlap, and each needs the other: the freeze is what makes a confirmed number safe for CJ's answers and for sibling documents to cite, and confirmation at sign-off is what stops the freeze from locking in a number two drafts both believed was theirs. **What this does not fix:** it does not stop two deltas *proposing* the same number, and is not meant to. It stops a proposed number being read as a claimed one, and moves the collision from merge — where it is expensive, because answers, cards and other documents already cite the number — to sign-off, where it costs one edit.

- [x] J1-Q1: Confirmed; added type-filtered bulk ops (accept all insertions/deletions); "changes as a list" view = P2.
- [x] J2-Q1: Workshop mode confirmed (chat center-stage pre-doc, slides to panel on birth).
- [x] J2-Q2: Agent picks location by convention, no dialog; design keeps room for an optional "where should this live?" confirm later.
- [x] **J2 project rail (#698, ratified by CJ 2026-07-30, DH-7):** the author-mode rail is the user-visible project navigator in workshop and document-authoring layouts. It excludes Git/internal/tooling paths; opens or focuses Markdown documents through one path; preserves the single repo-level timeline; and never appears in reviewer mode. Its inventory covers loading, refresh, empty/unavailable/unreadable projects, deep paths, non-Markdown context, correlated rename/move, and missing-on-disk recovery. Automatic observation plus explicit refresh keep the tree current without focus theft; autosave pauses for a missing file, recreation is conditional and non-overwriting, and every failure preserves the open buffer. Exact enumeration, ignore, observation, rename-correlation, and conditional-write mechanisms belong to the Architect.
- [x] **Timeline transcript unavailable (#1813, ratified by CJ 2026-08-26, DH-7):** a transcript rehydrate rejection preserves in-memory activity and renders `Activity unavailable` / `Earlier activity could not be loaded.` / `[Try again]`; retry scopes to transcript rehydration, and a failed load never renders a facet's completed-empty claim. The state reuses the established unavailable/error treatment and tokens.
- [x] J3-Q1: Dual ordering confirmed (report card by severity; margin/timeline by position).
- [x] J4-Q1: Batch default = all open comments (filters available but not pre-applied). **Amended by UXR-Q10 (#1122):** the default now excludes threads a live or queued address run already targets — one disclosed, one-click-reversible narrowing — and no other filter is pre-applied.
- [x] **J4 Address all host and chooser (#2676, ruled by CJ 2026-10-04):** In the main app the trigger is in the editor toolbar or its 900px `⋯` overflow; the Timeline-header trigger is a standalone-component variant. Omit the zero-held clause and checkbox; keep the selected filter through close/reopen on the same document and reset it on document switch; focus the first filter on open; close in both hosts on Escape or outside click; after admission focus the run card in either expanded or collapsed Timeline. This reconciles the old J4 host sentence with the shipped app and resolves refresh (26) M11. Appearance remains in the generated design handoff.
- [x] J5-Q1: Reviews share raw in-flight state (pending redline visible to reviewers from the start; no clean-up nudge).
- [x] J6-Q1: No "finish review" ceremony — watching comments arrive is enough; wrap-up = ordinary doc-level comment.
- [x] **J6 reviewer-mode completeness (#1515, ratified by CJ 2026-08-14, DH-7; PR #1547):** reviewer mode is selected as a whole-window state before review content mounts. Its state inventory, externally ended-review transition, and persistent reviewer status region keep the review set as the only document namespace; J2's project rail, project-home strip, author document lifecycle, and every Missing-on-disk recovery or destructive-close control remain structurally unmounted and unreachable.
- [x] J7-Q1: Notification defaults confirmed (blocked/broken on; completions off).
- [x] J8-Q1: GitHub deferred by default, with an upfront [Connect now] option on the welcome card and in Settings.
- [x] **J10 (epic #1125, ratified by CJ 2026-08-09 at reviewed head `145c655c`, DH-7):** correcting your own comment gets its own journey rather than a clause in J4, because it is neither an AI action nor author-only — it is the same two verbs in author and reviewer mode, and J4 is about handing a comment to an agent. The delta records: `Withdrawn` and `Editing` in §2's thread-state inventory plus an `edited` provenance dimension; the user-facing verb `Delete` against the record's *withdrawal*; correction offered on every surface that renders a thread's actions and absent (not disabled) on another actor's card; an **in-card, non-modal confirmation** whose first line names who could already have seen the comment and whose second states what survives; the two asymmetric refusals as a closed list; and the withdrawn placeholder versus clean disappearance. The confirmation is **argued rather than inherited** — Ctrl+Z cannot reach a sidecar withdrawal, a toast is rejected by name, and the private case leaves no residue to host an undo — which is the answer to the note D24 owed the Designer.
- [x] **J10 cascade-hide — historical ruling, superseded by CJ 2026-09-30.** CJ ruled on 2026-08-18/20 that a private root withdrawal hid its own-author replies without removing their stored records, with a third confirmation variant naming them. This answered the original private-thread gap but no longer governs J10. The later child-first ruling requires every direct and descendant reply to be withdrawn by its own author before a parent can be deleted; J10 Phase B/C and FR-CMT-9 carry the current rule. The historical decision remains recorded here so the change of direction is explicit.
- [x] UX review pass (2026-07-05): 12 of 15 findings folded in (integrity banners, doc-set navigation, re-anchor, suggestion semantics, Settings inventory, cancel flow, severity accessibility, voice states, author-apply, micro-affordances, testability nits). Flagged-as-my-call: v1 Compare = side-by-side (no 3-way merge); provider switch = from-next-message.
- [x] UXR-Q1: One run at a time per project; queued requests as visible cancellable cards. (§2 Agent concurrency)
- [x] UXR-Q2: Principle 2's three verbs share one test — *did the user ask for this?* *Applies* binds protected documents whatever the actor; *auto-scrolls* binds motion the user did not ask for, so a user's own submit may scroll to its card; *steals focus* binds focus taken unasked, so entering a focused pass grants focus rather than stealing it. Submit ordinarily does not change the active facet `@anchor:timeline-panel-handle-submit`; an out-of-filter card surfaces a `1 new in Chat →` affordance instead. **Scoped exception (UXR-Q16.4, CJ 2026-10-01):** a run started outside a persistent comment card while Timeline is collapsed expands the rail and selects `All` if needed to reveal its new run and applicable J13 disclosure. It preserves a facet that already contains the run. The comment-card path remains collapsed and the ordinary expanded-rail submit rule remains unchanged. (CJ, 2026-07-20; exception 2026-10-01 — amends §1 Principle 2, §4 "Timeline at scale".)
  - Raised by P2b-1a #347: `TimelinePanel.test.tsx`'s "does not force-scroll a scrolled-up reader" cited Principle 2 but drove the append through the *user submit* path, conflating agent-appended with user-submitted. The clarifying precedent is older, though — J1's "Review 1-by-1 … editor scrolls" and §4's facet-jump were both already ratified user-initiated scrolls, so the narrow reading of *auto-scrolls* was always the operative one. This records it rather than loosening it.
  - Per-verb scoping (rather than a blanket agent/user split) keeps J6's "never a mid-read jump" for a remote human's edits inside the principle, and keeps the *steals focus* guarantee that `ChangeSetCard`, `CommentThreadCard`, and `commentHighlights` rely on — those are focus taken *unasked*, while J1:126's focused pass is focus the user asked for by entering it.
  - Left open, not decided: whether the document→timeline deep-link (Principle 6, live at `commentHighlights.ts` `@anchor:comment-highlight-deep-link`) should restore a facet hiding its target thread. Same shape as the submit case; a #347 follow-up.
  - Two follow-ons in #347: retarget the mislabelled test to an agent-appended card, and **change** `TimelinePanel.tsx`'s submit handler — it currently restores the All facet (PR #392), which this ratification supersedes.
  - **Amended by CJ (2026-08-20, #1324) — which edge of the just-sent card the submit-scroll reveals, left open by the 2026-07-20 ratification above:** reveal the entire submitted card when it fits within the viewport; when the card is taller than the viewport, reveal its top so reading begins at the natural starting point. One measured, once-per-request scroll on the existing `scrollRequest` seam (`VirtualList`'s `reveal: "full"` `@anchor:virtual-list-reveal-kind`, `virtualization.ts`'s `scrollTargetForReveal` `@anchor:scroll-target-for-reveal`), preserving the PR #392/#504 anti-scroll-stealing (apply-once) contract — the card the issue filed against had never been mounted at submit time, so the seam's first pass can only judge "does the card fit" off its estimate; a second, narrowly-scoped correction fires once the card's real height is known, still within the same request identity.
- [x] UXR-Q4: A change-set card never triages a document it cannot prove it matches. Triage actions on a card fail closed — visibly disabled with an affordance naming the card's document, never a silent no-op and never a silent wrong decide — whenever the card's document is not the active tab, or the live document's tracked-change parse no longer corresponds to the parse the card's ids came from (delivery to a background tab, delivery to an unopened doc, or a UXR-Q3-suppressed delivery are the three known paths). `ChangeSetCard` therefore carries its document identity (as `CommentCard` already does). Cross-document and stale-generation triage become *routable* rather than refused only when change identity is stable (TD-4 content-addressed ids — follow-up #493); route-and-focus was considered and deferred to that follow-up rather than built on ordinal ids. (CJ, 2026-07-20 — raised by the PR #479 (#346) round-4 Critical: per-doc ordinal ids collide across documents, so mismatched triage fails open against a protected document, violating invariants 2 and 4; amends J1 Phase B/C.)
- [x] **UXR-Q5 (ratified by CJ 2026-07-22, DH-7): a decision that reaches past its card says so, and a card that cannot verify a decision report says nothing.** Two connected J1 Phase C holes, referred by CJ from #416 after the 2026-07-21 sweep-in ruling (group closure crosses change-set boundaries; P4b applied, not amended).
  - **(a) Over-reach was invisible.** The plumbing to know it happened already exists — `onDecide` `@anchor:change-set-card-on-decide` returns the full decided set (`timeline/ChangeSetCard.tsx`) and the editor computes closure before dispatching (`editor/trackedChanges.ts`'s `decisionGroupClosure` `@anchor:decision-group-closure`) — so the acting card's own counts reconcile correctly. Nothing told the user their action reached beyond the card they were looking at. Answer: a **post-action reach note** on the acting card, **conditional on the card gaining a reversal path** (§2 rule 1 — undo reverses the document whole but not the timeline's account of it, so the note would otherwise outlive its own fact). Rejected: a pre-action warning (it needs group structure the timeline deliberately does not model, §3.3, and would block the user on their own deliberate act against J7's inline-never-modal pattern) and a toast (a transient surface this design system has never used, and provenance is not transient).
  - **(b) A stale card could be decided *for* but could not decide itself.** `TimelinePanel` forwards each decided-id batch to **every** mounted card ("no per-card document linkage … not a promise every change-set card mounted at once belongs to the same open document"), while UXR-Q4 gates a stale card's own controls off — and the card's reconcile effect carries no `mismatch` guard at all. Answer: **fail closed in the reconcile direction too.** Editor change ids are positional ordinals re-derived per parse, so an unscoped id set handed to a card from a different parse retires ids on collision, not on fact — the identical ordinal-collision *failure mode* UXR-Q4 fixed for the *decide* direction, still open in the *report* direction. A card absorbs only reports it can prove address its own parse; the rest it ignores and stays visibly gated. The asymmetry is removed by deleting the state change, not by narrating it.
    - **Severity, calibrated:** identical mechanism, **not** identical severity. UXR-Q4 was wrong text decided in a *protected* document (invariants 2 + 4). Here the decide direction stays gated, so no document is mutated and no invariant is breached — a correctness defect in triage state, not a Critical.
    - **But it bites harder than a wrong count.** A collided id drops out of `pending`, hence out of `sectionsOf`/`sweepIds`, hence out of Accept all, per-section **and** Review 1-by-1; `isChangeSetCollapsedByDefault` `@anchor:change-set-collapsed-by-default` can then collapse the card to "N changes decided". Real pending changes are orphaned from their own triage surface with no recovery short of a panel remount. That is what makes this a defect rather than the legibility gap the brief described.
  - **One fact, two affordances — deliberately not merged.** (a) and (b) share one datum (the decided set, with its provenance) but answer different questions at different tempos: (a) is the actor asking *"did I do more than I meant to?"* — a bounded outcome of one act; (b) is a bystander card asking *"why did this change state on its own?"* — a persistent status on a card the user may not even have been looking at (it can be off-screen in the virtualized window or hidden by the active facet). Fusing them would make (a) permanent noise or make (b) a moment the user misses.
  - **Scope note (design-relevant, not a design decision).** Over-reach is rarer than the code suggests: on disk `group ⊆ change set` is structural (one `grp-<uuid>` `@anchor:change-group-id-mint` minted per `propose_changes`), but the editor discards the backend's authoritative grouping and re-derives adjacency groups over the whole document (#416's own analysis). Straddling groups are therefore mostly an artifact of that wiring gap. This argues *for* the light accounting line and *against* any heavyweight gate — but the affordance is right regardless, since P4b permits real straddles once grouping is authoritative.
  - **Handed back to intake, not decided here** (Designer stays in lane). Two constraints on any fix, stated as requirements the UI rule imposes — the mechanism belongs to **#493** (TD-4 stable change identity), where CJ folded the report-direction defect on 2026-07-21:
    1. **Generation-stamp the report at emission, not at absorption.** "Scope `externallyDecidedIds` by document + generation" is insufficient as a filter: `changeSetDecidedIds` is a running accumulator that survives reseeds, so ids accumulated under one generation keep being forwarded after the live field reseeds — and a card that *matches* the current target absorbs them, passing rule 2's test while its premise is false. The provenance has to ride with the batch from where it is emitted.
    2. **Reversal has to reach the timeline** (§2 rule 1). #680 now carries the restored ids through that path and narrows the add-only decision contract with an explicit reversal event, satisfying the prerequisite for the reach note.

    Separately, the unused authoritative `DeliveredChange.group` `@anchor:delivered-change-group` is a wiring finding (#566). No FR-*/D#/TD# is proposed by this delta; no P4b change is required.

The clean-tab path is unchanged: an agent-attributed write to a clean tab is a tracked change, not an outside edit, and both baselines move (M11). Chosen over apply-and-preserve (a three-way merge on the delivery path — new machinery with its own failure modes) and apply-and-warn (still transiently destroys work and leans on unverified undo recovery). (CJ, 2026-07-20 — raised by the PR #479 (#346) review Critical; amends J1 Phase B.)

- [x] **UXR-Q8 — project-open routing across instances (raised by the #759 Designer delta; ratified by CJ 2026-08-09).** J9, §2 "Project-open routing states", and the §4 shared status-region clause are ratified with all four Designer recommendations accepted:
  1. **Outcome feedback — accepted uniformly.** `Already open here`, `brought it to the front`, and `opened in a new window` state their outcomes; adopted-here clears silently because the project on screen is its own outcome. A raised window that lands on another desktop or monitor cannot be the only feedback.
  2. **One shared status region — accepted.** The region appears on all three surfaces, its name and fallback copy are generalized, and no welcome-only vocabulary is introduced. The welcome-screen placement remains a visual brief for CJ's design project because `1a` predates the decision.
  3. **What the choosing window does during the bounded wait.** `Opening <name>…` appears in the status region, the window stays fully usable, a repeat request for the same project joins the one in flight, and different projects queue in request order with a visible count. Rejected: disabling the open affordances (blocks the user on the app's own latency, against Principle 3) and any modal or spinner over the surface. A cold second instance can take seconds, and the line remains the honest evidence during it.
  4. **No `Recent` pre-mark in v1 — accepted.** Outcomes are stated after the act, never predicted before it, matching the project rail's existing rule (#698: "the rail never predicts success"). A pre-mark would also require live cross-instance presence before any request exists.
  - **Deliberately not decided here:** where the `Open a folder…` entry point lives for an instance that already holds a project (§4 inventories no `File` menu; the delta assumes the native menu and adds no new affordance), and the copy for `Clone from GitHub…` while the clone itself is running (a distinct, longer wait from Phase A's).
  - **Dependency resolved:** PR #1150 merged the requirements as **D26 + FR-X-10/11/12** before this design gate closed. This document cites both the ratified decision and requirements.
- [x] **UXR-Q9 — comment-correction appearance (`5a`/`5b`/`5c`; resolved by CJ 2026-08-09).** The regenerated visual layer now draws both in-card confirmation variants, the withdrawn root and reply placeholders, both refusal states, the edited marker, their Windows/macOS control states, and representative editor-popover/timeline reuse. `4a`/`4b` remain the canonical normal column. Settled choices: `Delete → Cancel` matches the existing `Save → Cancel` order; destructiveness uses a filled `#A33A2E` button + explicit verb + visible no-undo statement, never color alone; the placeholder is a quiet solid-hairline card rather than the dashed Resolved treatment; linked-change refusal explains itself under the inert Delete item with `[Go to changes]`; an active AI run leaves `⋯`, Copy link, Mark as resolved, and `✓` available while `✎` and Delete are inert, with one card-level reason and no duplicate `[Stop]` — **those drawings were later amended by the 2026-08-20 bounds and the 2026-09-30 child-first ruling (UXR-Q11):** correction goes inert one rung earlier, from the moment a live *or queued* run targets the thread (FR-CMT-10), and the undecided-linked-changes state renders `Mark as resolved` inert as well as `Delete` (FR-CMT-21(a)), which `5a.4` does not draw; the same component set appears on the comments column, editor popover, and timeline card. Appearance is binding in `docs/design/specs/comments.md`; behavior is binding in ratified J10.
- [x] **UXR-Q10 (#1122, ratified by CJ 2026-08-03 and amended 2026-08-09 during #1123's rebase, DH-7): a thread with an address run in flight has its own state, and no thread-local affordance offers a second run for a comment a run already has.** `deriveThreadState` `@anchor:derive-thread-state` presented an in-flight run as **Addressed — pending**, so a thread mid-run claimed a proposal existed and offered no route to it — a defined state used for its opposite (Windows build 2026-08-03). Answer: a distinct comment/thread state, **Working** — §2's Agent-states `Working` row projected onto one thread — distinguished from *Addressed — pending* by progressive words, the binding `5a.5`/`5b.5` active-run status treatment, and the absence of proposal content rather than by animation. The run remains cancellable from its timeline card (FR-X-3); J10/UXR-Q9 governs control placement, so the comment card duplicates neither `[Stop]` nor a route/control row. Every way a run can end has an exit (delivery, defence, stop, failure, lost anchor, and an in-flight marker that outlives its run — **the table gains a delivery-into-a-dismissed-thread row, and the defence row becomes prior-state-aware, under D29 / #1162**), resting on **FR-X-5 / Principle 7 / J7's "resolves honestly"** rather than FR-AGENT-6, which is document-scoped and says nothing about thread state. Around it: an **acknowledgment ladder** in UXR-Q1's and §4's existing vocabulary (queued · targeted-but-not-started · started) that mints no further states, uses the same card-level status treatment, and binds **every thread-local** run-starting affordance while controls and accounting stay on run cards (**and, since FR-CMT-10's amendment of 2026-08-20, renders correction inert at every rung — while closure is bound at none, #1162**); J4's batch-sheet `[include them too]` is the sole deliberate exception that may queue an already-targeted thread again; **one clock and one control set per run** on the run card (**and, since UXR-Q17's reword of 2026-09-02, rungs 1–2 and the also-queued modifier name no run, because none exists to name — #2228**); and a general accessibility rule — **run state is never conveyed by animation alone** — beside §2's severity-encoding rule. Rejected: flipping every targeted thread of a batch to *Working* (it flattens J7's Completed / **Not started** and claims activity Emend may not be able to observe), per-thread timers (§4 records one stream), and duplicate thread-level run controls. **Amends §2 (comment/thread inventory and the rules beneath it), §3 J4 (single-thread flow and wireframe, batch, re-delegate bullet, batch-interruption edge state), §3 J7 (Interruption scoped to the run card; two edge states), and J4-Q1 in both halves — see below.** (CJ, 2026-08-03 — raised by #1122; precedence amendment by CJ in session 2026-08-09: later J10/UXR-Q9 and `5a.5`/`5b.5` govern the active-run thread surface.)
  - **J4-Q1 amended in both halves.** *Scope:* the batch default is all open comments **minus any thread a live or queued address run already targets**. *Pre-application:* that one narrowing **is** pre-applied, deliberately — UXR-Q1 executes a queued run only after the live one finishes, so a thread left in both sets would be addressed by the second run on a premise the first has already invalidated. It is not silent: the chooser states the exclusion and its count and reverses it in one click (`Address all · 6 open comments · 2 already in an agent run — [include them too]`), it restates against whichever filter is chosen, and **no other filter is pre-applied**. This is recorded as an amendment rather than defended as eligibility: the ladder deliberately leaves these threads' state unchanged, so they *are* open comments, and narrowing the default over them changes J4-Q1 rather than merely applying it.
- [x] **UXR-Q11 — child-first withdrawal visual refresh (originally cascade-hide appearance, raised 2026-08-18; revised by CJ 2026-09-30).** The prior three questions about depicting a cascade confirmation are retired: there is no parent-with-live-replies Delete confirmation in the current J10 rule. Generated refresh (20) updates `5a`/`5b`/`5c`, adds `5d`, and updates `specs/comments.md`/`specs/state.md` to show an authored parent's inert Delete with a reason while any direct or descendant reply is live; it removes the cascade confirmation and ordinary parent-with-live-replies placeholder, while preserving the published or historically replied-to placeholder once all replies are withdrawn. The late offline reply beneath a placeholder remains an exceptional convergence state to depict or specify. Generated `docs/design/` refresh (20) draws the child-present refusal and eligible confirmation in `5d`; the folder is not hand-edited in the repo.
- [ ] **UXR-Q12 — dismissal: CJ's four answers, the requirements that followed, and an undrawn surface (raised 2026-08-19 by epic #1162 **as UXR-Q11**, renumbered when #1125’s cascade-hide question merged to `main` with that number first — CJ’s answers of 2026-08-19 were given against **Q11.1 / Q11.2 / Q11.3 / Q11.4** and are the same four sub-questions numbered here; the delta remains parked for CJ at the DH-7 gate).** J11 and §2's closure rule give D25 / FR-CMT-14..18 their behavioral home. **CJ answered Q12.1, Q12.3 and Q12.4 on 2026-08-19; Q12.2 was left open and is now answered by ratified requirement.** On **2026-08-20 CJ ratified D28, D29 and FR-CMT-19/20/21** (PR #1792) — reopen as a user act, and one closure-availability rule for both outcomes — and this document is conformed to them. The appearance is still drawn nowhere.
  1. **Is the note offered after the act, or does `Dismiss…` open a composer that commits it? — ANSWERED: after the act (CJ, 2026-08-19).** `Dismiss` acts on activation and the dismissed card carries `[Add a note]`, because D25 kept the reason optional exactly so the honest exit is never more expensive than the false one. The consequence carried into J11 Phase B: the note is a **second appended event**, not a field on the dismissal event, and FR-CMT-17 requires it to publish wherever the dismissal published. The event shape is the Architect's.
  2. **Should manual resolve be refused wherever dismissal is? — ANSWERED by ratified requirement, not by this document (D29 / FR-CMT-21, CJ 2026-08-20).** Neither symmetric answer whole: while any **linked tracked change is undecided**, *both* are refused — the block is durable and user-paced, and a hand-pressed resolve there asserts what FR-CMT-7 denies; while a **run is in flight**, *both* stay available — the block is machine-paced and self-clearing, and refusing resolve there would have deleted §2's ratified "acknowledged, not swallowed" behaviour and left no exit for the run's duration. FR-CMT-16's in-flight limb is withdrawn. **The UX consequence to draw:** `Mark as resolved` is now inert in a state where it is live today.
  3. **Does a resolved thread gain `Reopen`? — ANSWERED: yes (CJ, 2026-08-19), and the requirement followed (D28 / FR-CMT-19/20, CJ 2026-08-20).** J11 Phase D now cites rather than argues: offered on a closed thread and nowhere else, **never on a withdrawn placeholder** (withdrawal is terminal), author only and author mode only, on the closed card itself — to hand while a just-closed card still holds its place, and found by revealing the hidden set once it has joined it — unconfirmed, deciding no agent work, carrying no reason or note of its own in v1, and landing in *Reopened* — **one condition, two causes**, with FR-CMT-20's closure-history rule bound to the hand reopen alone (scoped at #1792): a rejection-driven reopen inverts no recorded closure and keeps D8's per-change outcomes with no closure line. The dependency this delta flagged as missing is discharged. What stays elsewhere: whether a manual reopen is distinguishable from a machine-written one in the record, and what the two causes mean to the fold and re-delegation, are the **Architect's** (Q24(b)).
  4. **`Dismiss` or `Decline` as the user-facing verb? — ANSWERED: `Dismiss` (CJ, 2026-08-19).** One vocabulary from sidecar to requirement to button. `Decline` is not chosen. Because the word is now a control label, §4's `1 new in Chat →` chip says *cleared* instead.
  - **The appearance gap — a brief, not a mock to inherit.** `docs/design/` draws **nothing** for either half of this feature: no `⊘ Dismissed` card or pill, no `Dismiss` overflow item beside `Mark as resolved`, no `[Add a note]`, **no reopen control on any surface**, no inert/refused rendering for either closure verb, no reviewer-side rendering of a dismissal or its reversal, and `specs/comments.md`'s hidden-threads-chip table predates the outcome (a dismissal in the hidden set forces its neutral `N hidden threads` row, exactly as a withdrawn placeholder does). **The folder is not silent about that, and an earlier draft of this entry was wrong to say so:** `docs/design/CHANGELOG.md` (2026-08-09) records "Also unrepresented: **D25 / FR-CMT-14..18** (thread dismissal, epic #1162, proposed 2026-08-09) is undrawn. Dismissal shares this thread card and will want the same treatment once ratified." The design layer noticed it and deferred it pending ratification; what `specs/state.md` "Known gaps" omits is a separate, smaller omission. Seven items belong in the brief beyond the dismissed card itself: the **inert `✎`/`Delete` at the queued and targeted rungs**, which FR-CMT-10's amendment creates and `5a.5` draws only for a started run — the same muted controls and one card-level reason, on a card whose closure verbs stay live; an **inert `Mark as resolved`** with the accept-or-reject route, which D29 creates and the `5a.4` family has never drawn (it draws an inert `Delete comment` only); **`Reopen` on both closed cards**, plus the reopened card that must show the closure it inverts (FR-CMT-20) without reading as a new state; the **gutter icon and anchor highlight** (§2's closure rule states the constraint — never resolved's `✓` or fill — but not the mark); `specs/state.md`'s per-document `threads[].resolved` **boolean**, which a two-outcome closure axis invalidates, together with `showResolved` and `correctionBlock` (`null | 'linked-changes' | 'ai-run'`, whose `'ai-run'` value no longer blocks closure at all under FR-CMT-21(b)); `specs/timeline-rail.md`'s collapsed-row string `Thread resolved · headline scope`, which §2's "a closed thread states its outcome" rule directly amends; and the **level** the linked-changes refusal reason sits at, which the CHANGELOG's own one-control/two-control rule now points at card level (J11 Refusals). Successor to the `5a`/`5b`/`5c` correction family; the option ids are the design project's to assign.
- [x] **UXR-Q13 — what the collapsed rail costs (raised 2026-08-05; ruled by CJ 2026-09-30).** Falls out of UXR-Q7.1: collapsing the timeline reveals the comments column. §4 *Right-rail occupancy* records the three answers below and the added Address-with-AI link/disclosure ruling. Generated `docs/design/` refresh (20) draws these states in `4c`–`4g`.
  - **Ordering supplement (CJ, 2026-10-01).** §4 records Position's live-anchor order and stable unpositioned tail, Recency's latest-activity order, and J10's in-place-edit stability exception. The two sort labels already appear in `4a`/`4b`; this decision supplies their previously unspecified edge behavior.
  1. **No-comments empty state.** Keep the comments column at the established rail width on an open document. Show `No comments in this document` quietly only after that document's comments load succeeds with no threads; pending or failed load cannot assert zero. The same loaded-empty rule applies to an untitled pane (amended 2026-10-01).
  2. **Collapse scope.** `timelineCollapsed` stays per-window across tab switches. The column shows the active tab's threads or the appropriate empty message; it does not force Timeline open when the next tab has none.
  3. **Composer and comment-address runs.** The composer, provider switcher, mic, and J13 standing disclosure stay in expanded Timeline and are reached through its chip. Address with AI from a persistent comment card keeps the comments column visible and gives that card a link to the run. If unsaved document text actually rode the run, the card also says `Unsaved text sent with this run` beside the link; it does not say so for withheld text. UXR-Q16.4 separately rules that other collapsed run-start routes expand Timeline, selecting `All` only when the current facet excludes the new run.
- [ ] **UXR-Q14 — what #1804 forces open (raised 2026-08-28; five of its nine items closed the same day).** Following **UXR-Q13's** precedent, no taxonomy is offered for which of these are "known gaps" and which are unrecorded: that document already ruled the distinction changes nothing, because CLAUDE.md's "silence is not permission" makes all of them questions to answer and none an implementer's free choice. Ownership is likewise settled — this document's own preamble rules that a mock implying behavior it does not record is *a J# delta to capture and sign off*. **Numbering note:** these sub-numbers were renumbered once during the design pass that produced this delta; they are frozen here and no number is reused hereafter — a closed item keeps its number and is struck in place, following UXR-Q13's precedent.

  1. ~~**Whether the FR-ED-5 mic belongs on the editor toolbar — the only MUST-level item.**~~ **Resolved — yes (CJ, 2026-09-30).** The mic belongs on the editor toolbar on file-backed and pathless authoring panes, in both editor views. §4 now enumerates it and retains UXR-Q7.2's one-row measured overflow rule. §2 already supplies the common voice states. Generated `docs/design/` refresh (20) includes Windows/macOS mic placement, voice states and narrow-row behavior in `3c`/`3d`; `9c.9` remains a superseded comparison variant. **This identifier is retired and is not reused.**
  2. ~~**Whether the save-as latch, the finalization freeze and the focus recording need a behavioral home here.**~~ **Resolved — yes (CJ, 2026-08-28).** Recorded at § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus*, at cross-pane scope. **This identifier is retired and is not reused.**
  3. ~~**What the collapsed rail shows on a document where comments are impossible rather than absent.**~~ **Resolved — CJ, 2026-09-30.** Superseded by CJ on 2026-10-01: an untitled document takes comments, so after a successful empty load the same column shows Q13.1's `No comments in this document`. It can appear after a tab switch because Q13.2 keeps collapse per-window. **This identifier is retired and is not reused.**
  4. ~~**Whether `Ask agent` appears in the selection popover on a pathless buffer.**~~ **Resolved — CJ, 2026-09-30:** keep `Ask agent` absent until Save As gives the document a project path; keep `Comment` and `Quote to chat` available on the untitled selection (the `Comment` ruling is CJ, 2026-10-01). An absent action never suggests that a pathless selection can target an agent run (D21, FR-AGENT-2, TD-1), while FR-CHAT-4 can still supply the active buffer as reference data. Successful Save As restores the ordinary file-backed selection action on the preserved selection (J12 Phase F). The pathless selection popover is drawn in `docs/design/` `9g`. **This behavioral question is answered; the visual refresh is delivered. This identifier is retired and is not reused.**
  5. **The redline group's overflow threshold and its appear/disappear treatment.** The group is present at ≥ 1 change and absent at zero, so accepting the last change removes it mid-edit and, at the threshold, can reflow the row twice on one accept. J12 settles the **focus** consequence (Phase C). Generated `docs/design/` `3d`/`9g.5`/`9g.6` settles the measured Plex-font breakpoint, context-control leave order, format-group movement as one unit, `⋯` contents and row order, and click/keyboard menu opening. Production breakpoints still need remeasurement with platform fonts; any transition animation and whether an overflow decision stays sticky within a session remain appearance questions.
  6. ~~**Whether J5's review bar can still sit where it sits.**~~ **Resolved — CJ, 2026-08-28.** The review bar is **workspace-level chrome, full width above the tab strip**, in the band §4's shared status region and J6's reviewer status region already establish. It took **neither** branch of the either/or this question posed: the tab-strip prohibition governs *document-scoped* notices, and a review bar is review-scoped — it names a review, spans a document set, and gains a switcher at more than one — so the prohibition never reaches it and **no exception is needed**. The wording amendment and the fixed-height constraint on the bar are recorded separately, in the document-notice slot delta (#2123). **This identifier is retired and is not reused.**

  **UXR-Q14.7 — a frozen file-backed pane is drawn nowhere.** Lifting the finalization freeze to every pane (CJ's ruling of 2026-08-28) creates an appearance state nothing has drawn. `9d.1` draws a frozen *untitled* pane; a frozen **file-backed** pane has a fuller toolbar, a status bar with its full slot set, and — while a review is live — J5's review bar. Which of those are inside the frozen region, and how the shell-level close-attempt target reads against them, is undrawn, and `specs/state.md`'s regenerated Known gaps do not list it. Recorded as a brief for the next `docs/design/` refresh rather than left for that project to discover on its own.

  **Closed by CJ's 2026-08-28 design refresh (PR #2111), recorded for the trail:** ~~where the amber document-notice sits on a toolbar-bearing pane~~ — **ruled for every pane**, one slot below the toolbar, with a binding prohibition on anything between the tab strip and the toolbar; recorded separately, in the document-notice slot delta ("Delta B", #2123). ~~What the context group shows at zero changes on a file-backed pane~~ — **drawn**, and it answers `3a`/`3b`. ~~How an untitled redline states that it is unattributed~~ — **ruled**: the status bar's left cluster, qualifying the count.
- [x] **UXR-Q15 — may the shared status region's existing one-line reflow fire in author mode with no user act? (#2102; ratified by CJ 2026-08-28, DH-7).** Yes, bounded exactly as §2's *Unattributed internal failure* state bounds it: one line at all times, `<detail>` clipped rather than wrapped ahead of the sentences that explain the line, no focus move, no scroll. The residue is one line-height, once — the same magnitude that already ships on every project-open, refusal and failure since the region shipped — and reviewer mode already has a ratified unasked occupant in `Review ended` (#1515), so this extends a shipped precedent rather than introducing a new kind of thing.
- [x] **UXR-Q16 — what the author is told about the text that leaves with a turn (raised 2026-09-02 by #2237; J13 ratified by CJ 2026-09-05, Q16.4 answered on 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01).** FR-CHAT-4 puts the active buffer's exact submit-time contents into every submitted agent run while that document is dirty, and whenever an untitled one is active — in both cases within D31's 256 KiB bound, with an honest marker over it. J13 and §2's *Outbound turn-context disclosure states* propose the surfaces; these six were the calls J13 deliberately did not make for CJ. **The number is claimed** — §5's numbering preamble fixes it at ratification, and 16 was next free on `main` at drafting and **uncontested** at CJ's ratification of 2026-09-05 — `main` has since gained UXR-Q17–Q20 and none of them claims 16, which is the thing the preamble's confirmation set actually tests — so it is confirmed unchanged. **Of the six: four are answered by CJ on 2026-09-03** (Q16.1, Q16.3, Q16.5, Q16.6), **one was withdrawn** after the independent design review because the design system had already answered it (Q16.2, struck in place with its number frozen, UXR-Q14's convention), **and inherited Q16.4 has two subsequent rulings**: the collapsed comment-card route (2026-09-30) and auto-expansion with a scoped facet exception for run starts outside a persistent comment card (2026-10-01). All six items are disposed, so this parent item is closed. **Two ruling dates run through this delta and are not interchangeable:** the size bound and its untitled limb are CJ's of **2026-09-02** (D31, #2238, PR #2248); these four answers are his of **2026-09-03**.
  1. **Is visibility without a control enough, given that two of the three acts the author has change the *channel* rather than withhold the text? — ANSWERED: visibility only (CJ, 2026-09-03).** No dismiss, no toggle, no opt-out; the delta's proposal stands as ratified, and the reasoning below is the recorded reasoning rather than an argument still to be had. CJ accepted both halves of it: that a suppression control changes what the app is *required* to send and is therefore the PM's and his rather than a journey's, **and** the cost as stated — that saving stops the words being sent twice without withholding them, so the only acts that genuinely withhold are not saving and closing the tab. That cost is recorded, not resolved: if CJ later wants an act short of closing, it arrives as an FR delta and adds a state to §2's table rather than invalidating it. J13 proposed disclosure only, because FR-CHAT-4 is a MUST with no opt-out and a suppression control would change what the app is *required* to send — a requirements question that goes back to the PM and to CJ, never into a journey. The cost of that position, stated accurately rather than flatteringly: **saving the document does not keep the author's words from the agent** — the agent has the repo as cwd (D4) and reads the file itself, which FR-CHAT-4 says in terms — so saving only stops sending them twice. Switching the active tab is the same trade one document over. The acts that genuinely withhold the text are **not saving, and closing the tab**; for an untitled buffer, only closing it, and that destroys the work rather than protecting it. So the honest form of this question is: **is there any act short of closing a document that lets an author ask a question about it without its text leaving?** Today there is not. If CJ wants one, it is an FR delta.
  2. ~~**Two surfaces or one?**~~ **Withdrawn — the design system forecloses the alternative (independent design review, 2026-09-03).** A standing-line-only cut is not a cheaper coherent option: the composer is absent from `4a`/`4b` entirely, collapse is a **persistent per-window preference**, and every run-starting route but the composer bypasses it — at least nine of the at least ten this document records. That cut therefore leaves a collapsed-rail author with **no disclosure on any turn, ever** — not a thinner record but none — which Principle 7 does not permit. Offering CJ a choice a ratified ruling has already eliminated would cost a sign-off cycle. **Both surfaces — and the question that remained about the second one, whether its line survives the run card's collapse, is Q16.6, ruled by CJ on 2026-09-03: it survives. This identifier is retired and is not reused.**
  3. **The copy — eight strings under three constraints — ANSWERED in part: the over-bound composer lines carry the route out (CJ, 2026-09-03), against this delta's draft.** Composer: `<name> goes as edited, not as saved` · `<name> goes by name only — over Emend's size limit; save it so the agent can read it` · `“<label>” goes in full — it has no file to read` · `“<label>” goes by name only — over Emend's size limit; Save As… so the agent can read it`. Card: `carried <name> as edited` · `carried <name> by name only — over Emend's size limit` · `carried all of “<label>”` · `carried “<label>” by name only — over Emend's size limit`. Copy at this document's gate is CJ's, as the timeline facet empty-state copy was (ratified 2026-08-20), so these are proposals. **Three constraints J13 does bind, and the third is the one an author would be hurt by:**
     1. **No string states a size, a count or a measurement.** *Over Emend's size limit* is a fact the author can act on; a number is not, and D31's bound is stated in bytes for the payload, not for a person.
     2. **No string on an untitled pane may name a dirty state that pane does not have** (§2's *Untitled document surface states*: "none — the tab is never dirty").
     3. **No string claims what the agent will do with the payload.** FR-CHAT-4 rules that the app can only emit and that an agent's behaviour with a correctly stated payload is something "no requirement on the app can bind and no test of the app asserts." A line reading *the agent reads the saved file* — which an earlier revision of this delta proposed — is a reassurance at exactly the moment the app is degrading, and it is the same class of claim as the deep-link Phase B refuses. **Related, and part of why that string was withdrawn:** the limit is Emend's, not the document's. FR-CHAT-4 delivers the prompt on stdin *precisely so* oversized payloads can arrive whole, so *too large to send* attributes a policy bound to the author's manuscript. The register precedents run the same way — §2's *Unattributed internal failure* carries no reassurance clause, and *Completed-run summary* refuses to let a known zero stand in for an unknown.
     **A fourth thing CJ may want to weigh, offered rather than bound:** every card string is affirmative and in one verb family (`carried <name> as edited` / `carried all of “<label>”` / `carried … by name only`), deliberately, so that no turn's record leads with a failure verb against an author who did nothing wrong. An earlier revision read `did not carry <name> — too large`; it was the only negation in the set and it located the cause in the author's document.
     **The route out — raised here, and ruled against this delta's draft (CJ, 2026-09-03).** The delta declined to write a remedy into the copy and put the question to CJ. He ruled that the over-bound composer lines **must** carry it, and the strings above are amended accordingly — `; save it so the agent can read it`, and `; Save As… so the agent can read it` for a document with no file. His reasoning is Principle 7's own distinction, and it is the better reading: **degrading visibly is not the same as degrading usefully**, and this is the one state where the author has an act that actually helps, so a fact with no action is the wrong half of that principle. #2248 rules the route out twice — in D31 clause (5) and in FR-CHAT-4 — *“the route out is the user saving the document.”*
     **Scoped to the composer, not the card.** A card line records a turn that has already happened, where a remedy would be an instruction about a moment that has passed; the card strings are unchanged, and this delta agrees with that split rather than merely accepting it.
     **Checked against the three constraints above, rather than assumed:** the clause states no size or count (1); it names no dirty state on an untitled pane (2); and it makes **no claim about the agent** (3) — it says what *Emend* does with the text, which is the app's own behaviour to state.
     **Two precision points on the ruled clause, raised by this delta against CJ's first phrasing and ruled by him 2026-09-03.** (a) **`send` was the wrong verb against #2248's own clause.** Saving does not make the text ride the turn — a saved document is clean, so the envelope carries identity alone; what saving does, in #2248's words, is give the document *“a path and so … the agent a file to read without the buffer having to ride the turn.”* Inside this copy set, where `goes` and `carried` mean *rode the turn*, `send` therefore said something the app will not do. CJ took the repair, which keeps the ruling and the rhythm: **`save it so the agent can read it`** — a capability, which constraint 3 permits and which #2248 states in terms. (b) **For an untitled document the act is `Save As`**, which is what gives it a path at all (J12, FR-ED-8), so CJ ruled that line names it. He also ruled the displaced `and there is no file` clause **stays displaced**: naming `Save As` implicitly carries it — that is *why* the act is Save As rather than Save — so restoring it would state one fact twice on a line already measured as tight (N2, brief item 1).
     **The untitled-over-bound payload is no longer undetermined.** CJ ruled on 2026-09-02 that the bound is **not waived for a pathless document** — waiving it would make the exceptional path the unbounded one — so such a buffer carries no `content`, no `path`, and D31's statement **(c-pathless)**: this entry itself supplies no route to its current text. §2's sixth row is written against that, and against PR #2248 rather than against merged text, which is what the ratification bar at the head of this delta existed for — discharged when D31 merged.
  4. **Where the disclosure goes when the rail is collapsed — ANSWERED (UXR-Q13.3 and UXR-Q16.4; 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-01).** The composer and its standing line stay in expanded Timeline, reached through the chip. When `[Address with AI]` starts a run from a persistent comment card in the collapsed column, its card keeps the column visible and links to the run; if unsaved document text actually rode that run, `Unsaved text sent with this run` appears beside the link. The latest accepted link and its matching cue survive completion and tab switches until another accepted run for the same comment replaces them; an admission with no run changes neither. An over-bound run gets the link but no false claim that text was sent; its run card carries J13's by-name-only disclosure. A run started **outside a persistent comment card**, including `Address all…` and file-backed selection `Ask agent`, expands Timeline at submission to reveal the new run and its applicable J13 disclosure on the owning submitted-turn card. If the current facet already contains that run, preserve it. Otherwise select `All` and reveal the exact run. This is a scoped exception to UXR-Q2's usual preserve-facet rule, and this immediately visible run does not need the `1 new in Chat →` cue. J13's collapsed-rail edge state and §4 carry the same boundary; neither route introduces the composer into the comments column.
  5. **Does the first occurrence need more than a standing line? — ANSWERED: no. The standing line alone, with no onboarding moment (CJ, 2026-09-03).** Ratified on the delta's own stated ground, which is recorded here so the question can be **reopened on evidence rather than reargued from taste**: the gap #2237 names is that the author has *never been told this happens*, a quiet 11px line is the right steady state and might be the wrong first contact — but inventing a first-contact moment for a requirement CJ had just ratified would be the Designer guessing rather than asking. **J8 (first launch / onboarding) is where a one-time explanation would live** if authors are later observed to be surprised by the line; that observation, not a change of mind about the copy, is what would reopen this.
  6. **Does the card line survive the run card's default collapse? — ANSWERED: it survives (CJ, 2026-09-03).** Raised by the independent design review on 2026-09-03 and ruled the same day. CJ's reason is the one this delta argued: for a run started from any composer-less route the run card is the *full* record of what context rode with it, so a line that vanished when the run succeeded would leave those runs undisclosed and the transcript unable to answer what a past turn carried. **It lands as an amendment to §2's *Completed-run summary*** — the section that owns #1725's ruled content string — rather than as a clause inside J13, because a delta must amend a ruled string at the site that rules it; J13 cites it. **The constraint the delta proposed is ratified with it:** the outbound line sits beside `5 files read`, an inbound count, and the two must stay legible as opposite directions rather than one list. **The treatment is a brief item, not a ruling here** — the run card has no hi-fi appearance at all and #1391 holds a prior claim on it, so how one collapsed row carries two directions is CJ's design project's, not this document's. The argument that made it load-bearing is kept below.

     *Recorded as raised:* this is the one that decides whether Phase B functions at all. A successful run card collapses by default to `▸ ■ Completed 0:17 · 5 files read · 2 changes` (§2's *Completed-run summary*, #1725; §4's *Timeline at scale*), and that row's content is **ruled** without J13's line in it — so for every composer-less route the sole disclosure may leave view the moment the run succeeds, which also defeats J13's claim that the transcript is where a past turn can be asked what it carried. Three answers were put, and this delta noted that they were **not equally live** rather than letting CJ discover it. J13 ruled one constraint over all three — it restates, rather than adds to, Phase B's *never merged, never summed, never one count*, which comes from Principles 1 and 5: whichever answer was taken had to keep inbound and outbound legible as **opposite directions, not one list**. That constraint was neutral between the first two and bore hard on the third, because joining `▸ ■ Completed 0:17 · 5 files read · 2 changes` means joining a `·`-separated one list. **CJ took the first: the line survives the collapse**, so the directional marking is the work, and it is the brief's rather than this document's. The other two — that it does not survive (narrowing Phase B's transcript claim to expanded cards), or that it joins the summary's own content — are not taken. Not a taste question and not a copy question.
- [x] **UXR-Q17 — §2's acknowledgment ladder named a run this app has no name for; name it or word the rungs without one? (#2228; ratified by CJ 2026-09-02, DH-7).** **Word them without one.** The ladder's copy was written against a run *display name* that was never built and is not merely unplumbed: `RunRequest` `@anchor:run-request-shape` is `{project_root, prompt, operation_targets}`, and `RunStatus` `@anchor:run-status-shape`, `QueuedRun` `@anchor:queued-run-shape`, `StartRunResult` `@anchor:start-run-result-shape` and `ActivityLine` `@anchor:activity-line-shape` carry no label — `start_run` `@anchor:start-run-command` takes none, so there is nothing upstream to plumb. (The only `displayName` in the app is `providerMeta.ts`'s "Claude Code" `@anchor:provider-display-name`, a **provider**; `skill`/`workflow_run` `@anchor:thread-birth-provenance` are written at thread birth and name the review pass that *raised* the comment, so either would name the wrong run.) Four sites named a run, and all four are reworded: rung 1 becomes `⏳ Queued behind an agent run — …`, rung 2 becomes `In an agent run — …`, the run card's control becomes `[Stop]` (what it already renders), and the also-queued modifier becomes `⏳ also queued behind another agent run`. Rejected: **naming the run where one exists** (two renderings, two code paths and two test axes per site, for a name that would be absent in the ordinary case) and **minting run names** (new mechanism, and an ad-hoc J4 batch has no workflow to name). This is the reading closer to ratified intent rather than a retreat from it — §2 already records rung 2's constraint as *"it … stays true where per-thread progress cannot be observed"*, and the clause that carries the acknowledgment's substance (*"it already has this comment as written; you can edit or delete it once the run finishes"*) is J10's verbatim and untouched. Appearance needs nothing new: `docs/design/specs/comments.md` `5a.5` already draws this status slot's container, and only its words change (§2: "only its words change"). **Amends §2's ladder table (rungs 1–2 and the also-queued row), §2's rung-copy table, and J4's batch prose, which restates rung 2's line.** **Three consequences ratified by CJ 2026-09-03**, raised by the implementation and answered rather than left to an implementer: (a) **§2's collapse sentence is general** — "a thread with a live run is in none of those sets" binds at rungs 1–2 as written, so a resolve taken while a run holds the comment leaves the card expanded rather than collapsing over its own live acknowledgment; (b) **a cancelled run's threads keep the exit note** `Address run stopped — nothing proposed for this comment.` rather than clearing silently — the reading that already ships, since the conclusion path filters on `pending_run` `@anchor:thread-pending-run` alone with no `pending_run_started` check, so silence would have changed #1381 beyond this card and required a new store event that clears without recording an outcome; and (c) **`⏳ also queued behind another agent run`** is the accepted wording for the also-queued modifier — the fourth site named above, which CJ named but did not spell. Two sites outside the ladder still named a run and were **deliberately left** for CJ's own call, tracked in **#2257**: §2's *Agent concurrency* queued **run card** (`⏳ queued behind sermon-review · [Cancel]`) and J4's batch chooser (`2 already in sermon-review — [include them too]`, restated in J4-Q1's amendment). The rung-1/rung-2 distinction is also not yet supplied on the comment popover, so a held thread reads rung 2 there — the fail-safe direction, tracked in **#2258** with TD-15's revisit trigger. (CJ, 2026-09-02 for the reword and 2026-09-03 for the three consequences above — raised by #2228; surfaced by #1778 / PR #2184, which shipped the ladder's *withdrawing* half and left rungs 1–2 unrendered. Sibling of the same gap recorded on FR-AGENT-7/#713's matrix row.) **Both sites resolved 2026-09-03 by UXR-Q18 (#2257), below.**
- [x] **UXR-Q18 — UXR-Q17's reasoning extends to the two run-level sites it deliberately left: the queued run card and J4's batch chooser (#2257; ratified by CJ 2026-09-03, DH-7).** **Extend it — reword both name-free.** Same ground as UXR-Q17: no run display name exists anywhere in the app, so ratified copy should not specify one. §2's *Agent concurrency* queued run card becomes `⏳ queued behind an agent run · [Cancel]`, and J4's batch chooser becomes `Address all · 6 open comments · 2 already in an agent run — [include them too]`, reworded everywhere it is stated — §3 J4's batch prose and its restatement in §5's J4-Q1 amendment. **The cost is taken with open eyes:** two queued run cards now read identically, so a user with two pending runs cannot tell which `[Cancel]` cancels which. The counter-argument — that the queued run card is the one surface where a user must distinguish *which* pending request they are cancelling, and so arguably the one place a name would earn its mechanism — was put to CJ and ruled against: if that ambiguity ever bites in practice it becomes a J# question with evidence behind it, rather than mechanism built on anticipation (the same shape as UXR-Q16.5's reopen-on-evidence framing). Rejected: **minting run names for run-level surfaces only** (new mechanism — nothing carries a name today, and an ad-hoc J4 batch has no workflow to name), and **leaving both as they are** (no work, and the deviation was at least written down — but it leaves the constitution specifying something the app cannot do, which is what UXR-Q17 existed to stop). **Retires the FR-AGENT-7/#713 matrix deviation** this ruling closes — `docs/test-matrix.md`'s FR-AGENT-7/#713 row carried it verbatim (*"The card deliberately omits ux-design §2's illustrative run name because no run name exists in the data model"*), a shipped surface knowingly diverging from ratified copy; the sibling FR-AGENT-7/#2228 row's note that the deviation stood is dropped in the same pass, since it no longer does. **Amends §2's *Agent concurrency* queued-card copy, §3 J4's batch prose, and §5's J4-Q1 amendment (the same restatement UXR-Q17 amended for rung 2).** (CJ, 2026-09-03 — raised by #2228, filed as #2257; sibling of the same gap recorded on FR-AGENT-7/#713's matrix row.)
- [x] **UXR-Q19 — does rung 2 cover a queued run when queue membership is unavailable, and does the ladder bind per surface or per thread? (#2258; ruled by CJ 2026-10-04, DH-7).** It binds per thread across Timeline and editor popover. Positive membership of the exact holding run ID in the shared queued set gives rung 1 on both; absent or unknown membership leaves a held thread on rung 2, never a guessed queued claim. The “Run live” row covers this fallback. The earlier §2 proposal permitting the popover to stay queue-blind and differ from Timeline is withdrawn. TD-15's revisit records App props as the technical route, using Timeline's existing queued-card projection; no persisted thread field, second queue reader or context store is added. The single-thread queued run card and Cancel are ordinary FR-AGENT-7 work, not a new UX decision.
- [x] **UXR-Q20 — the refused-close branch: four questions it raised, all four ratified by CJ 2026-09-05 (DH-7; question 1 was CJ's own of 2026-09-04 at #2327/PR #2328, questions 2-4 were raised 2026-09-05 at the independent review of PR #2331; raised by #2330, answered by #2334).** **The answers, in order: keep everything; amend the sentence; move the bound to the class; restore the recorded position.** J2 now records that a landed write followed by a refused close is reconciled by a second write (the amendment above, ratified 2026-09-04). Four things it did not settle, each answered here. (1) **Should a refused close instead carry the closes out in the UI**, destroying a discarded buffer in a window that stayed open? **Ruled: no.** The window keeps everything it had. The authorisation to discard was given as part of quitting and does not survive the quit not happening, and it is the one irreversible act in the sequence. (2) **Was `[Quit anyway]` described correctly?** It was not: J2 said quitting anyway *closes*, while `main` reports a refused close there too, telling the author the window did not close and the session changes are still unsaved. **Ruled: amend the sentence** — its tail is conditioned above and its substance, including the older session's possible reopen and remaining-open-as-default, is untouched. (3) **May these disclosures interpolate the failure's own text?** They do, and `main` carried a second interpolation in the same single-slot status region while §2's #2102 amendment still read *"bounded to this one interpolation"* / *"precedent for nothing else"* — **the pre-amendment wording, quoted here as the question's own text and no longer live anywhere else.** **Ruled: §2's bound moves**, from that one site to the class it was the first instance of: a failure Emend **can attribute** may carry the failure's own text, under the constraints §2 already recorded. Emend authoring developer-shaped words of its own stays forbidden. (4) **Where does focus land after a refused close?** § *Save-as latching, session-write finalization, and focus* names where focus goes when an attempt resolves — cancelling restores the recorded position, a failure moves it to the failure dialog's default action, an omission to the guard. A refused close is a failure with **no dialog**: the freeze clears and the window stays. **Ruled: focus returns to the author's recorded position** — the same destination cancelling uses, because a refused close resolves the attempt with nothing carried out, which is the state cancelling produces. Recorded in that cross-journey section and implemented at both refused-close sites in the same card, so the record never describes behaviour the build lacks.
- [x] **UXR-Q21 — format-group behavior (#2388; ratified by CJ 2026-09-06, DH-7).** Six rulings make the already-drawn group executable. **(1) Semantic active state:** each button is pressed when its whole target has the format; a mixed target is unpressed and activation applies it to the whole target; a caret reflects inherited inline meaning or its containing block. **(2) Projection parity:** all four controls stay present and live in Rich text and Markdown and produce the same source edit. **(3) Input-origin focus:** pointer activation leaves or returns focus to the editor, toolbar `Enter`/`Space` activation keeps toolbar focus, and shortcuts keep editor focus; every route preserves the mapped editor target. **(4) Target and history:** inline selections retain the authored text without delimiters, an empty inline target inserts a pair with the caret between it, block commands retain the logical text/block target, and one activation is one undo step; repeating a toggle removes that semantic format when the whole target already has it. **(5) Shortcuts:** `Mod-B` and `Mod-I` join the tranche; Heading 1 and list receive none. **(6) List meaning:** `≔` is one **Bulleted list** toggle in v1; an ordered-list control or chooser is later work. The literal `B`/`I`/`H1`/`≔` glyphs remain placeholders, and the real icon set remains a later CJ-driven `docs/design/` refresh. Recorded in §2, J14, and §4. Traces FR-ED-1, FR-X-1, TD-2, J12, Principle 2.
- [x] **UXR-Q22 — J14 exact-target and block-edge behavior (#2388; ratified by CJ 2026-09-07, DH-7).** UXR-Q21's six rulings stand; four additions close the cases they did not decide. **(1) Exact active-state query:** each control answers `active`, `inactive`, or `pending` for the exact document revision and selection. Pending removes `aria-pressed`, sets `aria-disabled="true"` and `aria-busy="true"`, exposes `Formatting state is still loading.`, stays focusable, and renders unavailable and unpressed. It resolves in place without selection motion, focus motion, scroll, announcement, or mode change. A pending `Mod-B`/`Mod-I` is refused, never queued or replayed, and says `Formatting is still loading for this part of the document. Try again in a moment.` **(2) Task items:** a GFM task item is a Bulleted-list item; removing its bullet preserves `[ ]`/`[x]` as literal authored text. **(3) Tables and HTML:** Heading 1 and Bulleted list refuse a whole target that touches a table or raw HTML. Every known inapplicability uses ``<Control> isn't available for this selection because it includes <reason>.``, with the first excluded region in document order named as `YAML frontmatter`, `code`, `a table`, or `HTML`; pending never uses this copy. **(4) Soft-line H1:** a multiline soft-line paragraph becomes one Setext H1, preserving its line breaks, while a single-line paragraph uses ATX `# `; toggling either form removes only the heading structure. This records the measured FR-X-4/TD-13 constraint without introducing a synchronous whole-document parser. The literal `B`/`I`/`H1`/`≔` placeholders and deferred production icon set are unchanged. Recorded in §2, J14 and §4. J14 carries the exact appearance/state refresh brief for the next generated `docs/design/` refresh; the generated folder remains unchanged here, and CJ authorized implementation from the brief in the meantime. Traces FR-ED-1, FR-X-4, TD-2, TD-13, Principles 2/7.
- [x] **UXR-Q23 — where a filtered-out permission request asks for in-app attention (#2429; ratified by CJ 2026-09-07, DH-7; clear-focus supplement ratified 2026-09-07).** Use one nonnumeric Pause badge on the existing Timeline host whenever the current project has any pending permission request, with no arrival-time facet, rail, scroll, editor, review-position or focus change. Expanded, the `TIMELINE` label is an attention action distinct from collapse; collapsed, `Timeline ››` both reveals the timeline and performs the requested navigation. Explicit activation selects Chat, reveals the matching run and focuses its oldest actionable FIFO request heading; decision controls and Stop remain there only. J7 records stacked-request announcements and focus recovery, the waiting/cleared states, accessible names and the interim contrast-safe appearance. The clear-focus supplement makes a stable, non-actionable level-2 `Timeline` heading the expanded fallback while production has no collapse owner: if the focused action clears, a connected and focusable supplied collapse toggle wins, otherwise the heading receives `preventScroll` focus and the visible 2 px accent outline; ordinary Tab order then resumes, and neither a clear when the expanded action did not own focus nor a new request while the heading is focused moves focus. This does not itself implement collapse; UXR-Q13 is subsequently ruled and drawn in refresh (20). UXR-Q2's `N new in Chat` remains submitted-card-only, and OS notifications remain separate. The 2026-10-02 #2540 supplement keeps the collapsed Timeline button inline at compact and fitting widths in both idle and pending states, preserving its DOM identity and focus across badge changes; it supersedes the pending-only overflow exception. CJ authorized implementation from J7's exact brief; refresh (20) now draws the collapsed rail and its Pause badge. Traces D14, FR-AGENT-3, Principles 2/3/5/7, J1, J7, §4, UXR-Q2, #2399, #2429.
- [ ] **UXR-Q24 — J15's render-failure fallback: the prose alignment CJ's own ruling left open (#2130; raised 2026-09-07 by #2432, open for CJ, parked with the §2 and J15 delta above at the DH-7 gate).** **The number is proposed, not claimed** (§5's numbering preamble): #2429’s earlier CJ sign-off confirmed UXR-Q23 first, so this J15 parent is mechanically re-proposed as UXR-Q24 and remains unclaimed until its future DH-7 sign-off; turn 12’s generated `docs/design/` citations to J15/UXR-Q23 are stale citations of the former unclaimed proposal and correspond to this UXR-Q24, remaining unchanged until CJ’s next wholesale regeneration. **The three items below were renumbered on 2026-09-07** when CJ closed two and split two out; sub-numbers travel with a parent that is itself unratified, so the preamble's rule that a claimed number is frozen has not yet attached to any of them. **What is settled, and is not re-opened by anything below.** CJ ruled on 2026-09-04 at 12:24Z in three numbered items — (1) proceed with the whole-window fallback, accepting the narrow Principle 3 / J7 exception; (2) the four mode-safe copy variants and `Reload window` as the sole action are approved, including the rejected / non-completing retry state; (3) implementation waits for refreshed Windows and macOS artifacts, which turn 12 now supplies — closing *"This resolves the behavioral choice. PR #2301 / issue #2130 remain at the design gate only for the two platform artifacts and any resulting prose alignment."* Native close is ruled too, confirmed 2026-09-07 and accepted **outside this PR thread**; §2 records that provenance. **This entry is the *prose alignment* half of that closing line, and nothing more — three items.** **UXR-Q24.1 — the close refusal's three drawing decisions.** `docs/design/` files them under its own *"Three decisions the drawing had to make"*, separately from the acceptance, and the `12f` mock's *THE RULING DRAWN HERE* block carries only the two dispositions: that the reason reuses the one status slot, that the slot is mutually exclusive with the reload-failure line, and that a refusal moves no focus. §2 records all three as proposed. Only `state.md:56` and two CHANGELOG parentheticals fold them into the ruling; the folder's mock and its ruling paragraph do not, and this document follows the latter. **UXR-Q24.2 — Principle 2: this delta asks CJ to confirm that *no* carve-out is needed.** `docs/design/` §4 calls the once-only focus move *"J15's narrow, named exception to Principle 2"*, but Principle 2's focus limb is scoped as written to *"an agent delivery or an arriving permission request"*, and a render failure is neither, while the node focus sat on has ceased to exist. Recording a permanent exception in §1 for a case §1 never reached costs more than it buys, so none is proposed. **UXR-Q24.3 — three absences are genuinely unruled**, of §6's eight: raw error detail **in author mode**, where the ratified sibling *Unattributed internal failure* shows `<detail>` and cites the same FR-X-5 this surface cites to withhold it — the asymmetry is stated in §2 rather than hidden, and whether it is right is CJ's; a toast, standing status band or fifth region; and *"no reassurance clause"* as a **standing prohibition** rather than an artefact of copy already approved. Two more of the eight — no `Dismiss` / in-surface discard, and no second action — are entailed by item 2's *sole action* and are recorded as ruled. The remaining two are appearance and outside this question. **Closed by CJ on 2026-09-07, and recorded in §2 rather than here.** *(a) The close-refusal strings* — the refusal is an assembly, not a set of whole strings: fixed opener, one clause per holding guard joined with `, and`, fixed closer, with `An untitled document is open` as the ruled untitled clause, which states existence and claims no loss and so stays clear of the *dirty*/*unsaved* vocabulary J12 withholds. CJ's approved author string is unchanged and becomes the dirty-plus-run rendering. *(b) The reviewer row's missing durable clause* — a queued reviewer event is **not** a close guard, because J6 and FR-COLLAB-9 guarantee it survives this failure class and stays recoverable via `[Copy]`; only a composer draft holds the guard, which makes CJ's approved reviewer string true with no word changed. **Split out to #2437 on 2026-09-07, because they are design defects rather than prose alignment**, and CJ's closing line scoped this gate to the latter: `[Reload window]` being ungated against exactly the work Phase D's close refusal protects, on a J12 pane whose autosave is off by construction; and the fallback answering only the lower rank of §2's two-rank status region, together with dismissal memory that is per **window** while a remount rebuilds the tree without replacing the window. Both are stated at full fidelity there. **Not asked here:** the render-boundary signal, structured logging that does not consume the runtime signal, mode and guard establishment outside the failed tree, and the remount-result contract — all the **Architect's**, none inherited from TD-25 by analogy. Traces FR-X-5, D12, D15, Principles 2/3/4/7, J2, J6, J7, J12, §2's *Unattributed internal failure* (#2102), `docs/test-plan.md` §9b; `docs/design/specs/render-failure-fallback.md`, `12a`–`12f`; #2437.
- [ ] **`docs/design/`'s claim that J15’s absences are *ruled* — a mis-scoped provenance claim, not a peer-authority conflict (raised 2026-09-07 with UXR-Q24; for CJ, and materially smaller than it first looks).** **Attributed precisely, since this entry’s whole subject is a provenance claim:** the wording *"Every item below is absent by ruling, not undrawn"* is `specs/render-failure-fallback.md` §6, and `specs/state.md`’s Known-gaps line repeats it as the absences being *"ruled, not undrawn"*. Two sites, one claim. Recorded separately so it is not mistaken for a constitution-level contradiction. **It does not outrank this document on any reading:** *"absent by ruling"* is a claim about the **provenance of a decision**, not about appearance, and `docs/design/` binds appearance only — CLAUDE.md already fixes the direction, that a design-folder statement implying behaviour is *"a J# delta, not a licence to build it"*, never self-authenticating. **It is also self-inconsistent inside the folder:** the repeat sits in `state.md`’s own `## Known gaps`, the list CLAUDE.md defines as what the mocks *deliberately do not answer*, while §7 of the very spec that originates it says behaviour is *"proposed, not ratified"* — so a list of absences declared *ruled* sits under a proposal the same folder calls unratified, in the section whose stated purpose is the opposite. **And the word is unsourced but not wholly unearned:** of §6's eight bullets, **three** do trace to CJ's 2026-09-04 acceptance (agent activity, permission controls and `[Stop]` from item 1; no `Dismiss` / in-surface discard and no second action, both from item 2's *sole action*), **two** are appearance and outside the question, and **three** are genuinely unruled — the three carried in UXR-Q24.3. **So what is actually in front of CJ is those three lines**, plus, if he wants it, a bounded regeneration replacing *"absent by ruling"* with per-item provenance so the folder stops asserting a decision record it does not own. **A second regeneration is owed for a drift this delta itself created:** the 2026-09-07 `closeGuards` narrowing leaves `state.md`’s `closeGuards` row and `render-failure-fallback.md` §5a describing a guard set and a two-string refusal that §2 has superseded. Both regenerations are CJ’s to commission; this document neither makes them nor assumes them.
- [x] **Visual design layer (`docs/design/`) ratified by CJ 2026-08-04 (DH-7):** the pixel layer becomes a fifth peer constitution document, binding on appearance as this one is binding on behavior; conflicts and ambiguities between any two constitution documents escalate to CJ rather than being resolved by the reader. See the header note for the split of authority, the no-hand-edit rule, and the treatment of `specs/state.md` "Known gaps."

A dropdown was rejected because a binary switch should take one click and placing two dropdowns together would blur their distinct meanings. (CJ sign-off, 2026-07-31 — #1020; amends §4 Author mode.) **Placement superseded 2026-08-05 (UXR-Q7.2):** `Display:` moves to the status bar under §4 "Toolbar row budget." The two remain separate axes — that was this amendment's substance and it stands — but they are no longer adjacent, so the "two dropdowns together" reasoning no longer applies to their arrangement.
